# Development Log

> Suite counts in these entries are **snapshots taken when each change landed**, never current state. Several changes landed in the same shared tree within minutes, so a newer entry can honestly report a lower count than an older one; `docs/ROADMAP.md` carries the single authoritative current figure. Test counts described in an entry count *cases covered*, which is not the same as the number of pytest functions added — folding a case into an existing test still covers it.
>
> Entries cite the spec they were built from. Specs live under `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/`, which `.gitignore` excludes, so those paths resolve on the authoring machine but **not in a clone**. Each entry therefore carries its own reasoning rather than deferring to the spec, and any binding decision is recorded in the tracked planning artifacts (`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/`, notably `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md`).

## 2026-08-21 — Line-up consumes the alignment: cuts land on phrase boundaries, and every window is told which lines it covers (Phase B)

**Phase B of `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/three-step-populate-2026-08-21.md`.** The plan called line-up *"the step with the most machinery already built and the least of it wired in"*, and that was exact: `snap_cut_plan`, `vocal_gaps`, `align_lyric_blocks` and `lyric_line_tags` all existed and populate called none of them. It produced its layout blind to phrase boundaries and left the fix to a manual snap afterwards. It no longer does. **This changes what a populate produces** — windows move — so there is deliberately no byte pin on a default populate any more; the pin moved to the control arm (below).

**One snapping implementation, two doors.** `snap_cut_plan` operated on a project's *shots*, and in the chain line-up runs before any shot exists — it has windows. Rather than write a second snapper, the decision was lifted out whole:

* `timeline.snap_window_plan(windows, song, *, tolerance, minimum, maximum)` is now the entire decision — the clearance clamp, the nearest-gap rule, the left-to-right band check, the boundary-list contiguity, the four-way status. It knows nothing about `Shot`.
* `timeline.SnapWindow(id, start, duration, label, refusal)` is what it is handed. The two protections a Shot carries arrive **already worded** as `refusal`, because `window_move_refusal` is the one reader of a lock, an approval and an in-flight render and a second walk over those is the guard hole this codebase keeps finding.
* `timeline.shot_snap_windows(project, rendering=…)` is the one place a Shot becomes a `SnapWindow`, shared by `snap_cut_plan` and by the project-sourced `line-up`.
* `snap_cut_plan` is now a nine-line caller. `app.line_up_shots` is the other caller.

**Proved to agree, not asserted to.** Two tests carry it. The first runs the same song, tiling, tolerance and band through both doors and compares moves (boundary, proposed, gap), skips (boundary and which reason fired) and the resulting geometry float for float. The second substitutes `timeline._gap_snap_target` — the single editorial decision in the module, where inside a gap a cut lands — with one demanding a wider clearance, and **one** patch is observed by both doors. Two snappers would need two patches, and the second would go on answering 9.85 while the first said 9.60.

**What `ShotPlacement` now carries.** `(index, start, duration, section, vocal_seconds, voiceless)` plus:

* **`lines`** — the sheet lines sung across the window, in song order, as `timeline.LyricLineSpan(index, text, slots, start, end)`. `index` is the line's number in the Director's own sheet, so the fact points at the very line the mark was typed on.
* **`singers`** — a **property**, not a field: the ascending set of `(S1)` slots its lines name. Computed rather than stored so it cannot drift from `lines`; the wire row carries it for a reader and `alignment_from_report` recomputes it rather than reading it back, so a hand-edited body cannot claim a singer no line names.

`ShotAlignment` gained `status`, `tolerance`, `moves` and `skips`, and `moved` now means something.

**This is the seam multi-character pass 2 reads, and it chooses nothing.** No citation is picked from a slot and no window is attributed to a character — that is fill-in's, deliberately unbuilt, exactly as pass 1 left the declaration unbuilt so it could be tagged on a real song before the logic that reads it was written against a guess.

**How a line gets a time, and why it is composed rather than derived.** `_align_blocks` places each `[Tag]` block against the transcript with all of the repeat-defeating machinery that took to get right (time-dense runs, local re-scoring); `align_lyric_lines` only splits one block's own span among its own lines, with one ordered LCS whose tokens each carry the line they were typed on. Aligning lines directly against the whole transcript would need a second answer to the refrain problem, and two answers to that question is how a line gets timed to the wrong verse. Supporting changes, each a projection rather than a copy: `_sheet_blocks` is the one segmentation of the sheet (`lyric_blocks` drops its line numbers, `align_lyric_lines` needs them); `_lcs_pairs` is the one LCS (`_lcs_matches` projects the token half away); `_align_blocks` is keyed by block index because tags repeat and unanchored blocks are omitted, so the Nth answer is not the Nth block. A line no word of which was heard is **omitted**, never given the gap between its neighbours.

**Two entry points, and the confirmation split.** With a lay-out report as `plan`, line-up is a pure function of that report; its confirm moves the windows the lay-out step's own confirm created and refuses when the timeline is not that layout (`LINE_UP_WINDOWS_CHANGED`). Without a plan it lines up the timeline the project already has — report-then-confirm in `snap-cuts`' exact shape, naming every cut it would move and every one it would skip, with the locked / approved / in-flight protections honoured **in the existing wordings** because they arrive from `window_move_refusal`. **The chain asks for no second confirmation**: `confirm_replace` is consent to replace this timeline's windows, the windows being moved were created by the call it consented to, and there is no shot on the timeline for a protection to be about — `lay_out_protections` has already refused if there were. Only `start` and `duration` are ever written; the line facts are derived and are **not persisted**, so a sheet the Director retags is answered by the next line-up rather than by a stale copy in the manifest. No new persisted field, so no write path to enumerate.

**The band is the layout's own.** Line-up defaults to `POPULATE_MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS` (6 s) rather than `H3_MAX_SHOT_SECONDS`: lay-out capped its windows there on a measured render-cost decision — 5.2 s windows render in minutes where 9.5 s ones took hours — and a step whose job is to nudge a cut by less than a second must not undo it. The project-sourced door passes the wider 4–15 s band, for the reason `snap_window_plan` already argued: a stored window is not held to a rule it was never laid out under.

**Contiguity is structural and is asserted anyway.** The core carries the plan as a boundary list, assigns only interior boundaries and copies the outer two through untouched, so a gap, an overlap or accumulated rounding cannot arise arithmetically. Pinned on a 60-window, five-minute plan where per-cut drift would show: first start exactly 0, last end exactly the song length, worst seam **0.0 s** against assembly's half-frame tolerance.

**The control arm replaces the byte pin.** `POST /timeline/populate` gained `snap_tolerance` (`SnapCutsRequest`'s field, same key's meaning and bound). **0 is snapping switched off and a genuine no-op** — the core answers `"off"` before examining anything — so Phase A's two digests (`8b101523…cab0b8`, `085917f1…3c88c5`) are still pinned, now reached through `snap_tolerance=0`. They were not re-measured. What they say now is that giving line-up something to do is the *only* thing that changed about what a populate writes.

**Measured live against the Director's real project, read-only** (`data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10`; its SHA-256 is unchanged at `7f2eb2f2…5a24c4` and nothing was written to it).

* **The live 34-shot plan cannot be lined up at all, and it is not this change's doing.** Pointing the route at it returns 422: *not a contiguous tiling* — SHOT 02 ends at 16.042 s where SHOT 03 starts at 16.000 s. **16 of its 33 seams are outside assembly's half-frame tolerance**, from 27 ms to a 5.49 s overlap, because overlapping shots are now a legitimate editing gesture (the 2026-08-20 layers ruling). `snap-cuts` has always refused such a plan for the same reason, so this is a standing gap rather than a regression — but it means neither snapping door is usable on the Director's actual timeline. **Named as open below.** Two smaller consequences were fixed here: `TimelineError` escaped the new route as a 500 and is now the 422 `snap-cuts` has always given, and `SNAP_NOT_CONTIGUOUS` stopped claiming "assembly refuses the plan for the same reason", which the layers ruling made false for an overlap.
* **On the same song's real measurements, over a fresh 30-window tiling** (262 Whisper words, the 1329-character sheet, the Director's seven section boxes): 12 voiceless stretches of 0.3 s or more; **6 cuts moved**, shifts −0.185 to +0.430 s, mean 0.229 s; 23 skipped — 5 already landing clear of every phrase, 18 with no rest within 0.75 s. Contiguity after: worst seam **0.0**, first 0.0, last end 154.640. **39 lyric lines timed**; 23 of 30 windows carry at least one, 19 carry more than one, and 0 name a singer because that sheet is untagged — which is the honest `()`, not slot 1.
* **Raising the tolerance buys nothing on this track**: 0.75 s, 1.5 s and 3.0 s all move the same 6 cuts. At 3.0 s the reasons change — 11 out of tolerance, 7 refused by the band — because a longer travel would push a neighbour past the layout's 6 s ceiling. The 0.75 s default is doing as much as the ceiling allows.

**Verification.** 22 new cases (44 in `tests/test_populate_steps.py`, suite **1580 passing**), ruff and both `node --check` gates clean. A **42-mutant sweep** over `app.py` and `timeline.py` in a `git worktree`, with a sentinel in each file that had to fail: **42 killed, no survivors.** Nothing calls a model, starts ComfyUI or submits a render; `comfy.prompts` is asserted empty on every path.

**Still open, found here and not fixed.** Snapping requires a contiguous tiling, and the Director's real timelines are no longer contiguous by design. The snapper models a cut as a boundary two shots *share*; under the layers ruling the effective cut is where the later clip starts, which `assembly_plan` already computes. Teaching the snapper to read assembly's own resolved cut list would make both doors work on an overlapping plan, and it is a design decision rather than a bug fix — deliberately not smuggled into this phase.

## 2026-08-21 — Populate becomes three steps: lay it out, line it up, fill it in (Phase A — separable, named, and byte-identical)

**The Director's model, verbatim:** *"Populating the timeline is definitely a multi-step process.. Laying it out, lining it up, filling it in. Each of those 3 steps has its own sub processes for figuring out where the lyrics are, the good cut boundaries, what assets to use, camera flow through the shots, etc. so we have to ensure we are getting them right and each step helps build the next."* Ruled 2026-08-21: **three separate routes, chained for the first pass.** The plan is `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/three-step-populate-2026-08-21.md`; this is its Phase A.

**Phase A changes nothing about what a populate produces, and that is the deliverable.** `populate_timeline` was one 400-line route doing three jobs, which is why its weaknesses were impossible to attribute — a bad layout and a bad asset choice look identical from outside. It is now three module-level functions (`lay_out_shots`, `line_up_shots`, `fill_in_shots`), three routes over them, and a chain that calls the same three functions. No prompt wording moved, no model call was split, no snapping, variance, narrative role or per-shot character selection was added. Each of those is a named later phase with its own measurement.

**The byte-identity proof, and how the baseline was taken.** A realistic project — the *shape* of the Director's live one, 154.6 s with measured voice and a silent intro and outro, seven marked sections, a declared location, a promoted identity sheet, and 34 proposals that name assets both structurally and in their prose — is populated against a scripted director double, and a SHA-256 is taken over every field of every shot and section the route writes, with only the freshly-minted shot ids dropped. The digest was measured on `master` at 9c3db45 **before a line of the split was written**, by running the same fixture body against a `git stash`ed tree rather than by copying a number down: `8b101523…cab0b8` with sections marked, `085917f1…3c88c5` without. Both reproduce exactly after the split, on the response and on what a fresh `ProjectStore` reads back. A second, independent check ran the whole thing against a *copy* of `data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10` on both trees and agreed; the live manifest's own SHA-256 was taken before and after and is unchanged (`7f2eb2f2…5a24c4`).

**What each intermediate carries — the actual interface, and the thing the later phases depend on.**

* **`ShotLayout`** (step one → step two): `project` (the re-read project the write will go to, carrying the section layer this step assigned), `duration`, `required`, `proposals`, `windows`, `sections`, `sections_origin`, `message`. `proposals` is a tuple of `ShotProposal(start, duration, prompt, performance, assets)` — the model's reply frozen into data, with the `getattr` duck-typing done once where the reply is read.
* **`ShotAlignment`** (step two → step three): `layout`, `placements`, `measured`, `moved`. Each `ShotPlacement` is `(index, start, duration, section, vocal_seconds, voiceless)`.
* **Step three's output** is an ordinary `list[Shot]`.

On the wire the same three are `LayOutResponse`, `LineUpResponse` and `FillInResponse`, and each step's report is the next step's `plan`.

**Where the model call lives, and why.** In **lay-out**, and there is exactly one of it — asserted against a double that answers differently on every call, so a second ask cannot hide behind determinism. The single call answers both "how many shots and how long" and "what is in them"; splitting *that* is a later phase with measured evidence behind it (the combined ask delivered both halves on 0 of 9 rolls, 2026-08-20) and doing it here would forfeit the one safety property this phase has. Lay-out owns the call because it owns the count the model is held to and the guided retry that enforces it; fill-in receives the content half as data on `ShotLayout.proposals`, an opaque payload lay-out itself reads only the length of.

**How the chain confirms: once, at the step that would violate the protection.** Each route is report-then-confirm on its own terms, but three modal confirmations to lay out one timeline would be worse than the one button this replaces. Lay-out is the destructive step, so `confirm_replace` — populate's own field, in populate's own words — is the consent for the whole pass: line-up writes nothing at all, and fill-in writes content into windows that same consent created. The chain requires the consent up front rather than reporting first, and that is the one place it deliberately differs from the route it calls: a chained populate has never had a report step, and adding one would either spend the 300 s model call twice or hand back a plan the Director has to send again. The standalone `lay-out` route is where a layout can be read before it lands.

**Protections, asked once.** `lay_out_protections` — no song, renders in flight, locked/approved shots — is one function called by the `lay-out` route and by the chain, in the order populate has always asked them and before the model is spent. **Fill-in inherits none of them**, and that is the point: a lock and an approval refuse a *lay-out* because a lay-out replaces the windows they were placed on, and fill-in writes inside windows it never touches. That is what will later make re-running content against approved timing safe. Three things make "never touches" a guarantee rather than an intention: the write assigns five content fields and nothing else, every report row is refused unless the shot at its index still sits at the window it was laid out for, and the produced shots' windows are compared to the ones the step was handed (on the report as well as the confirm — a report describing a window the confirm would refuse is a report nobody can act on).

**The chain is a caller, not a fourth implementation.** It calls `lay_out_shots`, `line_up_shots` and `fill_in_shots` at module scope, which is exactly why they are module-level and not closures: a test monkeypatches each one and asserts the chain observed the substitution. This codebase has twice been bitten by one rule with two implementations (the reference-map numbering, the readiness/submit staleness test), and a chain with its own copy of a step would have gone on passing the byte-identity pin while the two drifted.

**Line-up is close to a pass-through, and says so rather than inventing work.** `moved` is 0, `LineUpResponse` has no `applied` flag and no `project` field, and the snapping machinery is deliberately not called: wiring phrase boundaries into the layout changes what a populate produces and this phase may not. What it does is real but small — it measures each window against the track (`shot_vocal_overlap`, which populate used to compute inline in the middle of building a `Shot`) and hands fill-in the one fact that downgrades `singing`, plus the section each window falls in. Consuming the alignment properly is Phase B, which is where the machinery that already exists gets wired in.

**No new persisted field, so there is no write-path table to enumerate.** The intermediates travel on the wire and in memory; nothing new reaches a manifest, the whole-project `PUT` gains no hole, and every old manifest loads exactly as it did. Pinned by a test asserting a chained populate's manifest holds no key outside `Shot.model_fields`/`Project.model_fields` — because the next phase's narrative role *will* add one.

**The reports, for a Director stepping through by hand.** `POST /timeline/lay-out` returns the windows, the proposals and the section layer with its origin (`director` / `structure` / `shots`); confirming it writes the section layer *only when this step laid it out* — the Director's own boxes keep their ids — and one draft shot per window carrying `start` and `duration` and nothing else. `POST /timeline/line-up` returns each window's measured voice and section. `POST /timeline/fill-in` returns exactly what would be written into each window, and its confirm lands it. Every confirm carries the report back as `plan` and is checked against its own `plan_id` and the project revision, so the layout that lands is the layout that was read and no model is asked on a confirming call — the 2026-08-21 report-then-confirm rule, applied to the step that spends the call.

**Verification.** 22 new cases in `tests/test_populate_steps.py` covering the byte-identity pin (both tiling branches), one model call per chained populate, the chain observing monkeypatched steps, each route callable alone with its report writing nothing (asserted on the project *revision*, not merely on the shot count) and its confirm persisting through a fresh `ProjectStore`, the hand-run three steps landing the same plan as the chain, line-up measuring every window and moving none, unmeasured reading as unmeasured rather than silent, the protections firing at lay-out and not at fill-in, the plan-carrying refusals, and `comfy.prompts` empty on every path. Suite 1558 passing, ruff clean, both `node --check` gates clean. A mutation sweep of 34 mutants over every new branch — run in a `git worktree`, with a sentinel that must fail and did — killed 33; the survivor is the lay-out confirm's second `lay_out_protections` call, unreachable while the revision check above it holds and kept as defence in depth, named in its own comment. One pre-existing failure is untouched and unrelated: `test_the_directors_live_project_is_unchanged_by_the_cast_fields` pins the live project at 33 shots and the Director has since added a 34th; it fails identically on `master`.

## 2026-08-21 — Who sings the song, and who sings each line: the declaration, the inline tags, the character slots, and the cast check at Populate (pass 1)

**The Director's ask, verbatim:** *"Lyrics - We should be able to select if the song is Instrumental, Female sung, Male sung, Duet, 3+, Choir. If selecting Duet or more then each line of the Lyric sheet would need to be tagged with Char1/Char2/Char3+/Both/All. This would need to be done before treatment in the Song workspace so that the LLM system can account for all that. Assets that we have in our Character section would then also need to be linked to these character references (this is something that could be flagged if the user labeled the song as a duet but they only have one character asset if the user clicks Populate Timeline). Then when the timeline gets populated and since whisper can figure out what lyrics are when - the correct character references can be used in those shots."*

**This is pass 1, and pass 2 is deliberately not built.** Populate does not choose a shot's character references from a line's singer: no citation is picked by a tag, no window is attributed to a slot, and the shots populate writes are wired exactly as they were. The Director wanted the data to exist and be checkable first, so it can be tagged on a real song before the consuming logic is written against a guess. Nothing here contacted ComfyUI, submitted a render, or called a model, and `data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10` was read for its real shape and never written.

**The storage decision, which was the design risk: the per-line dropdown edits the lyric sheet.** The Director asked for a dropdown at each line, and the obvious implementation is a parallel structure — a map from line number to singer. That map is wrong the instant a line is inserted, deleted or reordered, and wrong *silently*: every tag after the edit points at the wrong words, with no symptom, which is precisely the class of defect this repository keeps finding. So the dropdown writes into the line instead. The mark is **MiniMax H3's own speaker notation** — `(S1)`, `(S1, S2)` — reused rather than invented, because `h3_prompt._SPEAKER` already parses and validates that spelling, so the tag the Director sets is understood by the format the prompt is eventually written in. There is exactly one copy of every tag, it *is* the words it belongs to, and drift is impossible by construction rather than by discipline. The Director ruled on this mid-build: *"the hybrid inline tag sounds better, do that."*

**What the choice costs, paid honestly.** The tags live in text the Director edits directly, so they can be typed, mistyped and deleted by hand. `timeline.lyric_line_tags` therefore answers three ways, not two: `()` is *carries no mark* (every line of every existing sheet), a tuple is *tagged*, and `unreadable` is *the head of this line was meant to be a mark and could not be read* — a slot past the bound, a repeated singer, an unclosed bracket. An unreadable line is **reported and left exactly as it is**: not dropped (which leaves a line the Director believes is tagged carrying no tag), not rewritten (which edits their sheet to hide their typo). `tag_lyric_line` refuses to retag it until they fix it. The suspect pattern requires `(`, an `S` and a *digit*, so an ordinary lyric line opening `(she said)` is never dragged into the notation.

**The writer cannot write a mark its own reader would reject.** `tag_lyric_line` re-checks the reader's two conditions — slot inside the bound, no repeated singer — before it writes anything, on both sides of the wire. Without it a caller could store `(S9)`, and the Director would then be told their sheet is broken by an edit they never made by hand. Closed by a test that writes every option every dropdown can produce and reads each one back.

**A tag edit reformats nothing.** The lyric sheet is stored as supplied apart from edge whitespace — interior blank lines, indentation and `[Tag]` structure reach the payload unchanged — and a tag edit is not a licence to re-wrap it. The writer splits on a pattern that **keeps the separators** and replaces exactly one element of the resulting list, so CRLF stays CRLF, indentation survives on both sides of the mark, and every untouched line comes back byte for byte. `str.splitlines()` was unusable for this: it discards which separator each line ended with, so one tag click on a Windows-pasted sheet would have rewritten every byte of it. A test asserts line-by-line identity across an edit.

**One consequence found by looking rather than by failing.** `_lyric_tokens` reads words with `[a-z]+` off lowercased text, and `(S1)` lowercases to `(s1)`, out of which that pattern reads the bare token `"s"` — one phantom word per tagged line, inflating every block's token count and its match threshold against a transcript containing no such word. Tagging a duet would have made Analyze structure align it measurably *worse* than the same sheet untagged. The mark is stripped before the words are counted; the pattern is anchored to the head of a line, so an untagged sheet tokenises byte for byte as it always did. Pinned by aligning a tagged and an untagged sheet against one transcript and asserting the results equal.

**Which vocal types are tagged, and why the other four are not.** `unstated` has declared no cast to attribute a line to. `instrumental` has no sung line. A solo song's every line is sung by the one voice the song-level choice already names — a dropdown whose answer never varies is noise on every line of the sheet, which is the Director's own objection. A **choir** is a mass voice rather than a cast: there is no Char 1 to distinguish from a Char 2, so the per-line question has no answer to offer, and a choir song with named soloists is a duet or an ensemble *with* a choir and is declared as one. Only `duet` (S1/S2/Both) and `ensemble` (S1/S2/S3/All) name singers. `ensemble` stops at three because that is the roster the Director named; a fourth is one row in `VOCAL_TYPE_SPECS` plus a bound that widens with it, because `CHARACTER_SLOT_LIMIT` is **derived** from that table rather than typed — a hand-typed `3` would let a dropdown offer "Char 4" and then refuse the asset slotted to it.

**Instrumental is a real case.** Declaring it offers no tag, needs no slot, and says one thing once: `INSTRUMENTAL_NOTE`, restating the roadmap's stage-1 finding that instrumental songs lean on the Treatment much harder because there is no singer to associate. What it does **not** do is touch a shot. It does not sweep `singing` to `not_singing` — nothing in this codebase infers a singing state, and a declaration about the song is not a measurement of a window. The vocal-band guard is untouched and still the only thing that decides a sung window: `Song.vocal_spans` is Whisper's own voice activity, a truly instrumental track measures voiceless everywhere, and populate already downgrades every window of one through `shot_vocal_overlap`. A shot marked singing over a measured-voiceless window still gets no sings clause, whatever the label says.

**`Asset.character_slot`, and the check the Director named.** A line tagged `(S1)` resolves to whichever character asset holds slot 1 — a number on the Asset rather than an asset id in the sheet, so replacing a character is one re-slot instead of an edit to every tagged line. `0` is unslotted, which every existing asset is. The route refuses three ways, each leaving the asset untouched: a non-character asset (a slot names a singer), a slot another character already holds *by name* (one slot, one character, or a tagged line points at two references and the render picks by accident), and a number past the bound, refused by the schema. At **Populate Timeline** — the Director's own moment — `vocal_cast_problems` reports the shortfall on `PopulateTimelineResponse.cast_notices`: *"Duet declared, and 1 of the 2 character slot(s) it needs are filled — S2 unfilled."* **Flagged, never refused**: the plan still lands, because the slots are set in another tab and sending the Director away from the button they pressed would cost them the plan. Silent for every type that names no cast, which is every project that existed before this change.

**Every write path for both fields**, enumerated as those routes' docstrings require:

| Route | `Song.vocal_type` | `Asset.character_slot` | Per-line `(S1)` marks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `PUT /song/vocal-type` | **the one writer** | — | — |
| `PUT /assets/{id}/character-slot` | — | **the one writer** | — |
| `PUT /projects/{id}` (whole manifest) | re-adopts stored, **before** the song comparison | re-adopts stored per asset id, `0` for an unheld id | (writes `lyrics` as it always has) |
| `PUT /projects/{id}` with a confirmed song replacement | reset to `unstated` beside `_detach_song_recovery_slots` | — | — |
| `PUT /projects/{id}/shots` | not on the wire | not on the wire | not on the wire |
| `PUT /song/context` | not on the wire | — | **the one writer** — the tags are lyrics |
| `POST /song/context/{field}/restore` | untouched | — | swaps the whole sheet, tags included |
| `POST /songs/upload`, `POST /generate/music`, `songplanner` | fresh `Song`, defaults to `unstated` | — | as supplied |
| `DELETE /song` | Song goes | — | — |
| `POST /assets/upload`, Flux, multiview, AI Mod, `replace-citations` | — | fresh `Asset`, defaults to `0`; a promoted sheet does **not** inherit a slot | — |
| `POST /timeline/populate` | reads only | reads only | reads nothing |
| every model tool schema (`FillShotsArguments`, `AssetProposal`, `PlannedShot`, `director_result_schema`) | absent | absent | absent |

The whole-manifest `PUT` is this repository's recorded guard hole, and these are its **sixth and seventh** appearances. Both fail the same two ways the recovery slots did: a client written before the field existed omits it, so the defaulted value arrives and one ordinary save un-declares the cast or un-slots every character at once; and a client that *invents* one declares a duet nobody declared. Both directions are tested. The vocal type is adopted **ahead of** `project.song != current.song`, `_adopt_song_recovery_slots`' own reason: a body differing only there must compare equal, or an old client's ordinary save would be told it is replacing the song.

**Neither field reaches a model in pass 1, and that is a decision.** `Asset` gained the classification guard `Song` and `Shot` already had — `ASSET_DIRECTOR_VISIBLE`/`ASSET_DIRECTOR_WITHHELD`, checked at import, so the next field added to `Asset` cannot enter every Director prompt without someone deciding that it should. Everything that was in the dump yesterday is classified visible, so it changed no prompt. `vocal_type` and `character_slot` are withheld on the never-been-in grounds plus their own: nothing has yet been designed for a model to *do* with them, and shipping a bare `"vocal_type": "unstated"` key into every chat turn and every populate call would change what every existing project's model sees in exchange for a fact no instruction mentions — and would put the live project's populate out of byte-identity with the plan the Director already has. Deleting those two entries is pass 2's first line, and the test that pins them names them so the removal is a decision.

**The Director's real project is unchanged, pinned rather than asserted.** Before any code was written, a digest was taken on `master` at `0896832` over all 33 shots — each one's `song_audio_prose`, its reference-map tag lines, its reference numbering, and the citations populate's own rules (`assets_for_proposal` → `with_default_setting` → `prefer_identity_sheets`) derive for its prompt — plus a second digest over the whole Director context dump. The post-change tree reproduces both exactly (`ef4931d4…`, `98148c67…`), and the pre-change value was re-measured against a stashed tree rather than trusted. The project declares no vocal type, slots no character, and its real tagged lyric sheet reads as untagged and not as unreadable. The test skips rather than fails where that data root is absent, because a pin that fails for being run elsewhere is a pin people delete.

**Tested** (33 cases; suite 1504 → 1537). Every vocal type's roster including the four that offer none; the `(S1)` parser and writer over a fixture carrying CRLF, indentation, a blank, a `[Tag]` header, a lower-case mark, an unreadable mark and an ordinary parenthetical; the dropdown round trip (write, read back, re-select); line-by-line byte identity across a tag edit; **the drift case** driven both in Python and over HTTP — tag a sheet, then insert a block and delete a line, and every surviving tag is still on its own words; the slot route's three refusals; the populate flag firing for "duet declared, one slotted" and silent when satisfied; instrumental end to end; both new fields' generic-`PUT` guards in both directions; an old manifest carrying neither field round-tripping through a fresh `ProjectStore`; and the frontend executed under node — the per-line select is **rendered into the stub DOM, found the way the panel's own code finds it, fired, and read back**, never grepped, which is the only way to prove a solo song is offered no control at all. **44 mutants swept in a `git worktree`** holding this change alone, never the shared tree, with a sentinel mutation that must fail proving the mutant is what loads and every anchor's match count asserted before a byte was written. Six survived the first pass and every one was **uncovered rather than tolerated**: a prop and a contested slot smuggled in by a hand-edited manifest (`character_slot_assets` re-validates, and a contest resolves to the first holder in append order, never the last); an asset the generic `PUT` both introduces and slots in one body; a confirmed song replacement carrying the old track's cast across; and an unclosed `(S1` — the one unreadable shape that never matches the mark pattern at all, which the fixture had no example of. The seventh was an **equivalent** mutant rather than a gap: an early `if not spec.slots: return []` that the `missing` loop below it already subsumed, deleted rather than tested, because a branch that only restates a later one is a branch that can drift from it. All 44 are killed now, the last of them (`character_slot` inherited by a promoted identity sheet) only after its test was rewritten to go through the promotion **route** instead of a hand-built sheet — the decision is made by omission there, and a test that never reaches the route cannot see it.

**Found and not fixed.** (1) The whole-manifest `PUT` can still write `Song.lyrics`, and therefore can write a tag, because the tags are the sheet. That exposure is pre-existing and unchanged — a body that rewrites the sheet is rewriting the sheet, visibly, in the one box the Director reads — and it is not the *inference* the hard rule forbids: nothing computes a mark, and the only writer is the Director's own dropdown. Closing it means making that route re-adopt `lyrics`, which is a decision about the sheet rather than about this feature. (2) A tagged sheet's `section_lyrics` carries its `(S1)` marks into the H3 expansion specialist's input. That is H3's own notation and arguably where it should end up, but nothing yet tells the specialist what to do with it; no existing project is affected, because no existing project has a tag. Both are pass 2's to settle.

## 2026-08-21 — Four recorded interaction defects cleared, the seed randomizer made per shot, and one wrong sentence deleted rather than fixed

**All five behavioural items are interaction defects, and every one of them was driven in a real browser.** This panel has now produced six defect classes a stub DOM structurally could not see. Two new self-hosting Edge harnesses were written for them and one was extended; nothing here contacted the user's ComfyUI, submitted a render, or called a model, and `data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10` was read for its real shape and never written.

**1. The Clips tab went blank when ComfyUI was down — and it did not have to.** Every card pointed a `<video>` at ComfyUI's own `/view`, so all thirty-three 404'd whenever the Director had not started ComfyUI, which is an ordinary state and not an error. The brief asked whether `GET /api/projects/{id}/media/{path}` could serve takes instead. **It cannot**: an H3 take's `output_files` entry is a path inside ComfyUI's *output* tree (`music-video-producer/{project}/shots/…`), and that route serves `store.media_dir(project_id)` and 404s anything outside it. But a different route already can, and already does — `GET /api/projects/{id}/shots/{shot}/take` resolves the shot's own `latest_output` under `settings.comfy_root / "output"` **on disk**, streams it with Range support, and is what the Monitor plays. No ComfyUI process is involved. It takes ids and deliberately no path, so it serves exactly one take per shot: the current one. So each card now decides for itself (`clipCardFace`) — the current take comes from this application and plays with ComfyUI stopped, an earlier take goes through `/view` when ComfyUI is answering, and only an earlier take with no ComfyUI wears the honest face that names why it cannot be shown, keeping its filename and its "Open shot". The tab-level notice is drawn only when there is genuinely a card it cannot show. `clipPreviewState` answers **three** ways, not two: online, offline, and *this browser has not been told* — saying "ComfyUI is offline" on a browser holding no health answer would be inventing a fact about someone else's process. The **Re-check ComfyUI** control is drawn in both states on purpose: health is fetched at boot and nowhere else, so a control that appeared only once the browser already knew ComfyUI was down could never be pressed by a Director whose ComfyUI stopped after the page loaded. Nothing polls; the only thing that knocks is a press. *Browser evidence*: `tests/e2e_clips_and_attach.py` runs a stub ComfyUI on a port it owns and stops it mid-run, so the application really probes and really gets a connection refused. With the stub up, three cards, two from `/take` and one from `/view`, all three reaching `HAVE_METADATA`. With it stopped, the two current takes still reach `HAVE_METADATA`, the superseded one says "Earlier take — ComfyUI offline", nothing points at ComfyUI, the notice names the address that was tried, "Open shot" still lands on the producing shot, and the console carries no errors at all. With the stub restarted and the control pressed, all three come back. The old shape is visible in the harness that was meant to catch it: `tests/e2e_seed_and_asset_tabs.py` carried `/view` in its *expected console errors* list, three 404s a run, for as long as the defect existed. That fragment is gone.

**2. "Attach to selected shot" named nothing.** The Director's words: *"hard to use since cant see timeline from assets page"* — the button acted on an invisible target, and attaching to the wrong shot looked exactly like attaching to the right one. `replaceInShotsControl` had already solved the same problem by putting its count in its own label; this puts the identity in the label (`Attach to SHOT 01`) and the id, the window and the opening of the intent in a caption beneath it. It is shut with its reason on screen in the two states where it could only fail or no-op: no selection, and an asset the shot already cites — the second was a live control that wrote the same list back and toasted "attached", which is the *appears to do nothing* shape this whole thread began with. The success toast names the shot too. One line of `app.js` was needed beyond the control itself: the Assets panel repaints the inspector on arrival, because the only way to change the selection is to leave and come back, and a named target that names the wrong shot is worse than the unnamed button it replaced. *Browser evidence*: the caption's contents and geometry hit-tested at four widths across both breakpoints, the disabled states driven, and the attach read back off the manifest on the run's own data root.

**3. The right resize handle under an overlap.** A consequence of the 2026-08-20 layering ruling: the later clip is painted over the earlier one's right edge. **The fix raises the handle, never the clip.** `.shot-clip` carries no `z-index` and is not a stacking context, so `z-index: 2` on `.resize-handle` resolves against the track and lifts every handle above every body while `overflow: hidden` still clips it to its own clip. Which picture is in front is untouched. It costs 12 px at each clip's edge where a press now resizes that clip rather than moving the one on top of it — the trade every editor makes, and the neighbour keeps its own left handle and all the rest of its body. *Browser evidence*: `tests/e2e_clip_overlap_and_split.py` builds a plan carrying the live project's measured overlaps (0.002 s to 5.492 s) and asks `document.elementFromPoint` at the middle of each handle. Two handles are buried **whole** — 12 of 12 px — and both hit-test to their own handle; the layering probe inside the 5.492 s overlap still returns the later clip's body; and a real drag on the buried handle moves that clip's window on disk (10.375 s → 9.75 s) and leaves the neighbour's alone. With the `z-index` line removed, the same run reports `shot_06`'s right handle hit-testing to `shot_07`'s `.clip-prompt` — the Director's report, reproduced. Sub-pixel overlaps are measured and *classified* rather than claimed: 0.002 s is a seventh of a pixel even at maximum zoom and covers nothing, and asserting it as evidence would have been dishonest. **Found and not fixed**: in the live plan the 5.492 s overlap is between two clips whose right edges are 0.015 s apart, so their two handles sit on the same pixels and the later one wins. No z-order can separate two edges that land on the same pixel; it is recorded in the harness rather than papered over.

**4. Split declined a window under a second in silence.** `#split-shot` checked `shot.duration < 1` and returned. `splitShotPlan` now owns both the arithmetic and the sentence, and the sentence explains rather than scolds — micro-cuts are legitimate, which is why `styles.css` deliberately draws no warning on the short end. It names all four numbers: *"SHOT 10 (shot_micro) is 0.75s long. A split halves the window, so each half would be 0.375s — under the 0.5s floor a shot window can be dragged to, which is why there is no cut to make here. Drag the window out past 1s first, or add a shot beside this one."* The floor is now `MIN_WINDOW_SECONDS`, the same constant the left- and right-edge drags clamp at, rather than a fourth copy of `.5`. Splitting with nothing selected was silent too and now says so. The halving arithmetic is unchanged byte for byte — deliberately un-quantised, so the two halves still meet exactly. *Browser evidence*: the refusal read off a real toast with the manifest unchanged either side of it, an ordinary window still halving contiguously on disk, and the no-selection refusal driven through a project with no shots.

**5. The seed randomizer is per shot (the Director's ruling, overruling yesterday's).** *"Clicking randomize integer on a shot toggles it for all shots, it should be per shot."* It shipped session-wide on the reasoning that a Director sweeping ten shots asked for randomness once. The shape is now `unlockedFromMusic`'s, written in the same file on the same day for the sister reason — the state is dangerous on the *next* shot — down to the lifecycle: a `Set` of ids, session-only, never a model field, cleared through the same `documentConsentClearedOnLoad` test so a refresh cannot untick a box mid-gesture while a project *switch* does. `nextRenderSeed` stays the single branch deciding stride-or-roll; what changed is which shot's flag it is handed. The on-screen help was the one sentence that had to move with it, and says "set per shot … arms this shot and no other". *Browser evidence*: ticked on shot A, shot B's box read as off, and then a Render again **on B** landing at `3 → 104` — the server's own `RESUBMIT_SEED_STRIDE`, not a roll — with A's number unmoved. Seeds read from the server, never from the input. That assertion is the exact inversion of the one this harness carried yesterday.

**6. `batchReadinessBlock` deleted, not corrected.** *"Ok lets not leave the wrong sentence laying around."* It was exported and called from nowhere: client-side batch refusal went away when FR-4 made the batch skip a blocked shot by name and submit the rest, and the function outlived its only caller, reachable from the executed contract alone. Its `message` came from `readinessRefusal`, which words every block as "no prompt on X" — wrong since `readiness_report` gained other blocking kinds. Fixing the sentence would have left dead code behind a correct-looking message, which is worse than either problem alone. The grep was re-checked rather than trusted, and turned up a *second* test using it that the report did not mention; both are gone, along with a `readiness_refusal` import that went unused with them, and the suite count drops by one accordingly — that is the removal, not a lost guarantee. **Found and not fixed**: `readinessRefusal` and `READINESS_REFUSAL` are now reachable only from tests as well. They are left because the server still emits that sentence and the contract test pins the browser's copy to `batch.READINESS_REFUSAL` byte for byte; deleting them would drop that cross-layer pin, which is a separate call and touches `batch.py`.

**Tested** (13 cases; suite 1481 → 1493 measured in an isolated `git worktree` holding this change alone, another agent's work having been landing in the shared tree throughout). The executed contract covers every deciding function and the markup each render actually produces — `renderClipsLibrary` and `renderAssetInspector` are *run* against the stub DOM and read afterwards, never grepped. **22 mutants, swept in that same worktree** with a sentinel mutation that must fail proving the mutant is what loads: all 22 killed, including four checked in a real browser because no offline harness can see them — a stylesheet line, a repaint on panel arrival, a control drawn in one state instead of two, and a session-wide flag. The one mutant that survived the first pass (`randomizeSeedShots.clear()` removed from the project switch) was uncovered rather than tolerated, and a test was added for it.

## 2026-08-21 — The pre-flight stops calling a shot submittable that the render route will refuse: a stale reference map now blocks in `readiness_report`, from the route's own implementation

**The gap.** Since the entry below, `POST /shots/{id}/generate/h3` refuses a shot whose stored expansion carries a stale reference map — and `batch.readiness_report` said nothing about it. So the batch pre-flight told the Director "33 ready", they confirmed the GPU cost, the batch started, and *then* one shot was skipped by name in the report afterwards. Every other thing that report exists to say — an unprompted shot, a near-duplicate pair, a window outside H3's band — is surfaced **before** that confirmation, precisely so "22 armed, 3 skipped" is seen at the one moment a Director can still act on it. This was the one refusal that was not.

**It blocks; it does not warn — and the established split is what decides it.** This codebase's rule is *emptiness blocks, sameness warns*: a gate that refuses something legitimate gets worked around, so two shots that deliberately share a prompt are reported and never stopped. A stale map is on emptiness' side of that line. There is no state of the world in which conditioning a render on a map naming the wrong pictures is what the Director meant, and the submit route already agrees — it refuses. A warning would have been strictly worse than either alternative: the report would have *predicted* a refusal while `ready` went on saying the plan may be submitted, which is the one thing `ReadinessReport` must never say about a shot the route will turn away. Blocking makes the two layers give one answer, and it costs nothing: a blocked shot has not disabled the batch button since FR-4 — the server-side batch skips it by name and submits the rest — so this names the shot and skips it rather than stopping the other thirty-two.

**What a blocked shot keeps.** `ready_count` still counts it as *having a prompt*, because it has one — `api.js`'s `readinessSummary` prints that number as "33 of 33 shots have a prompt", and a stale shot is not an unwritten one. What it loses is `ready` and a line in `blocking`, so the same summary goes on to say "1 cannot be submitted". No sentence on that surface had to be rewritten to be true.

**One implementation of "is this stale", reached from both sides.** `app.py` could not hold it: `app` imports `batch`, so the pre-flight had no way to call the route's function, and a second copy in `batch` is the failure this repository has already paid for once — the reference numbering existed twice and the two disagreed the moment a video was cited. So `stale_reference_map` and the map construction it needs (`REFERENCE_MAP_PREFIX`, `REFERENCE_MAP_ROLE_TAGS`, `reference_map_sentence`, `reference_map_tag_lines`, `song_audio_prose_expansion`) moved out of `app.py` into a new leaf module, **`reference_map.py`**, which depends on `models` and `timeline` and nothing else. `app.py` re-exports them by importing them, so `app.stale_reference_map` and every other existing spelling still resolves; the code itself is unchanged byte for byte, so every prompt ever built from it is the prompt it was. A test asserts the identity — `batch.stale_reference_map is app.stale_reference_map is reference_map.stale_reference_map` — and that no other module in the package defines the name, because "two functions that agree today" is exactly what the numbering bug was.

**Both staleness shapes are the same test, because they were already one function.** The prose form carries its map verbatim at the front of its own text and is compared against the map the shot would get now; a document expansion never writes the map down, so it is compared against `Shot.h3_prompt_map`. A shot with **no** expansion is not stale and is silent — it has not been expanded, and reporting it would tell a Director to fix something that is not there. That is asserted with a stale `h3_prompt_map` still sitting on the shot, which is the state a check keyed on the record alone would have got wrong.

**One problem, one explanation.** The refusal already named the remedy in the order that matters — clear the H3 box first, because a cleared box makes the submission build the map from the shot's own citations and is correct by construction; re-expand second, with the `mark-draft` step it actually needs. A second sentence in the pre-flight would have been free to drift from it, and a Director who reads two different instructions for one problem tries both. So the cause, the consequence and the remedy are three constants in `reference_map.py`, and `app.STALE_REFERENCE_MAP_REFUSAL` and `batch.SHOT_WITH_STALE_REFERENCE_MAP` are each composed from them — only the framing differs, because only the route is the thing that did not submit. The refusal's bytes are unchanged by the split. This is `api.js`'s own `READINESS_REMEDY` rule, applied to the second refusal that needed it.

**The one place the route had to change.** `generate_h3`'s prompt gate read `blocked_ids()` unfiltered and raised `READINESS_REFUSAL` — "no prompt on SHOT 07" — for anything in it. With a second blocking kind that sentence would have been said over a shot that has a prompt, while the stale refusal it already has sits three checks below. `blocked_ids` therefore takes an optional `kind`, the gate asks for `NOTE_KIND_PROMPT`, and each rule goes on raising its own sentence. The default still answers for every kind, which is what "may this plan be submitted" means and what every client-facing reader wants.

**Frontend: the vocabulary was extended, not duplicated.** `NOTE_KIND_STALE_MAP` joins the note kinds `api.js` already mirrors, and the readiness list draws the line under its own heading ("Stale reference map") while keeping the `blocking` list-marker class — it *is* a refusal, and the marker must not say otherwise, but "Blocked" over both blocks would make an empty prompt and a stale map look like one problem with one fix when they send the Director to two different boxes. The batch confirmation's `— N will be skipped (no prompt)` was hardcoded and would have become a lie; the noun is now read off each blocking note's `kind`, names both when a batch carries both, and falls back to "blocked" for a kind this client has never heard of.

**Cost.** `readiness_report` is called on every readiness fetch and once per shot inside a batch, so this had to stay free: it adds one `models.numbered_references` walk per **expanded** shot and nothing else — no model, no file, no ComfyUI, nothing written back (AD-5). An unexpanded shot returns on the first line without walking anything, so a plan being written rather than rendered costs zero. Measured on the Director's live 33-shot project: 0.31 ms for the blocking-only report the batch asks for, 2.41 ms for the full one — and it reports **no** stale shots there, which is the correct answer for a project whose 33 prose expansions are all fresh.

**Tested** (11 cases; suite 1481 → 1503 with another agent's work landing alongside). The headline is `test_the_pre_flight_blocks_exactly_the_shots_the_submit_route_refuses`: one project, two expanded shots, one of them made stale through the route the gesture really arrives on, and set equality asserted between what the report blocks and what the route turns away — both directions, so a pre-flight that blocked *more* than the route refuses fails too. Beside it: both staleness shapes over the pure report, a shot with no expansion staying silent, the manifest byte-identical through a fresh `ProjectStore` re-read, the shared-wording clauses asserted present in both sentences, the identity of the one implementation, and the readiness list rendered by the workspace in the stub DOM with its markup read rather than grepped. Sixteen mutants, run in an isolated `git worktree` with a sentinel mutation proving the mutant loads: all sixteen killed, one after the assertion that let `BATCH_SKIP_NOUN_UNKNOWN = ""` pass was tightened.

## 2026-08-21 — A citation change re-expands itself when that is free, and is refused at the render when it is not: no stale reference map reaches ComfyUI

**The live defect.** The Director attached the character sheet to a shot whose take had come out wrong — H3 had invented a woman in a wedding dress, because nothing in the prompt referenced a character. Attaching updated `Shot.citations`, and `h3_prompt` went on holding the old reference map, which named only the bed. `reference_prompt` returns a stored expansion **alone** when there is one (`app.py`), so the map is never rebuilt at submit: a re-render would have been conditioned on a map describing a different set of pictures than the payload wired, and would have come back plausible and wrong rather than failing. Re-expanding by hand fixed it. The ask: *"make the re-expand automatic when an asset is attached."*

**The invariant, stated once: no stale reference map ever reaches ComfyUI.** Everything below is in service of that one sentence, and it is kept two ways because expansion is two things.

**The deterministic half is re-derived; the model half is refused, never silently spent.** A song-audio reference shot's expansion is `song_audio_prose` — the reference map, the intent, the measured sings clause, the section look — built with no model call and measured at 0.0 s. All 33 shots of the Director's live project are that kind. Re-deriving one on attach is free and byte-identical to what pressing Expand Prompt Again would have written; `refresh_reference_maps` reuses `apply_expansions`' own two lines rather than composing a third spelling of the prompt. A document-mode expansion is model output. Rebuilding one on attach would spend a call nobody asked for — and attaching three assets would spend three, on a local model whose director timeout is 300 s. **Nothing in this change calls the specialist.** Those shots are left stale on purpose, and `generate_h3` refuses them by name. That is this codebase's standing preference for a named refusal over a plausible guess, and it is also the only honest answer: an unrequested model call is a cost the Director did not consent to, and a silent stale render is a take they cannot trust.

**Staleness is decided from what can actually be known about each form.** A prose expansion *carries its own map*, verbatim and at the front, so it is compared against the map the shot would get now — nothing stored, nothing that a client can clobber. A document expansion never writes the map down (the specialist weaves `<Picture 2>` into prose), so `Shot.h3_prompt_map` records the map beside it at every write of `h3_prompt`, and the comparison is against that. `""` means *not recorded* and is deliberately **not** read as stale: every expansion written before this field existed is in that state, nothing knows which map it was built from, and refusing a render on a guess is worse than the render. The next expansion records one.

**The comparison is the whole map sentence, not its lines.** Detaching the *trailing* picture leaves every surviving line still true in the old text — `<Picture 1> is the bed` still reads correctly — and only the sentence as a whole differs. `reference_map_sentence` is the single construction, read by the fallback prompt, by the record and by the check, because two spellings of the same join would report every shot in the project as stale.

**The sweep is keyed on the text, not on a diff of the request, and that is load-bearing.** The shots write deliberately does not adopt its own reply, so the browser goes on holding the `h3_prompt` it sent and reasserts it on its very next gesture — a drag, a resize, anything. A pass keyed on "what changed in this request" would see no citation change there and let the stale text stand; keyed on the text, the same pass simply re-derives it. That also means a hand-edited prose whose map no longer matches is rewritten rather than preserved. Stated rather than hidden: the map is what conditions the render, `Shot.prompt` is where the Director's own words live and is never touched, and re-deriving the prose from the intent is exactly what re-expanding means.

**The path table.** Every path that can move a citation, a `reference_labels` entry, an appearance anchor or an asset name — the four things the map is built from.

| Path | Can it change the map? | Refreshed before | Refreshed after |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `PUT /api/projects/{id}` | **Yes** — one body carries every field of every Shot *and* every Asset: citations, roles, `reference_labels`, asset names, the asset list itself | no | **yes**, after every server-owned field is re-adopted; `h3_prompt_map` is now server-owned here too |
| `PUT /api/projects/{id}/shots` | **Yes — the route the live defect came in on.** Attach, detach and re-role are one browser gesture: mutate `shot.citations`, write the whole shot list | no | **yes**; `h3_prompt_map` server-owned |
| `POST /assets/{id}/replace-citations` (on confirm) | **Yes** — rewrites citations across up to 22 shots at once, and moves the `reference_labels` entry with them | no | **yes**, every rewritten shot in the same pass, no model call |
| `PUT /assets/{id}/consistency-prompt` | **Yes** — `anchored_label` composes the anchor into every tag line, so this changes what every citing shot's map says | no | **yes** |
| `POST /assistant/fill` | **Yes** — the other writer of `citations`, onto shots that may already carry an expansion | no | **yes**, after `apply_expansions`, so a shot this turn both re-cited *and* re-expanded keeps the model's answer |
| `DELETE /assets/{id}` | **No** — refused by name (`DELETE_ASSET_CITED`) while any shot cites it, so it never clears a citation. The refusal is untouched | n/a | n/a |
| `POST /timeline/populate` | Replaces the plan wholesale with newly built Shots | n/a | n/a — a new Shot carries no `h3_prompt`, so nothing can be stale |
| `POST /director/chat` | **No** — writes `start`, `duration`, `prompt`; new Shots carry no expansion | n/a | n/a |
| `POST /timeline/clean-prompts` | **No** — writes `Shot.prompt` only, and `citation_fingerprint` is compared per shot across the write and refuses on any difference | n/a | n/a |
| `POST /assets/upload`, `/generate/flux`, `/generate/multiview`, `/assets/{id}/edit` (AI-Mod), `/assets/fill` | **No** — they create Assets; none writes a shot citation. The multiview and AI-Mod children are cited only by a later `PUT` | n/a | n/a |
| `POST /assets/{id}/analyze` | **No** — writes `Asset.vision`; `timeline.asset_anchor` reads `consistency_prompt` and nothing else | n/a | n/a |
| `POST /shots/{id}/select-take` | **No** — take provenance only | n/a | n/a |
| `POST /shots/{id}/expand-prompt`, `/shots/expand-prompts`, `/director/expand` | They are the writers of `h3_prompt` | n/a | they now **record** `h3_prompt_map` beside it, off the re-read project |
| `POST /shots/{id}/generate/h3` — and `/generate/batch`, which submits through the same handler | **No** | n/a | **refuses a stale map by name**, before the payload and before ComfyUI |
| `GET /render-status` (the 2 s poll) | **No** — it owns job state and cannot write a citation | n/a | n/a |

**The refusal names the remedy that always works, first.** Clearing the H3 box is not a gap: `reference_prompt` then builds the map from the shot's own citations at submission, so a cleared box submits a map correct by construction — and it works on a locked shot, because a lock stops the automated writers and never the human in the inspector. Re-expanding is named second and named *accurately*, with the step it needs: anything reaching that refusal is at `ready`, `shot_render_provenance` counts every status past `draft` as a render, so a document-mode shot must go back to draft first. `mark-draft` is the existing way and is deliberately as cheap as arming was.

**Who is skipped, and in whose words.** A **locked** shot is the Director's own hands-off and is skipped through `expansion_write_refusal`, whose existing sentences (`EXPAND_PROMPT_LOCKED`, `EXPANSION_LOCKED_NOTICE`) are reused verbatim. A shot with a render **in flight** is `replace_asset_citations`' one genuine correctness block — the job was submitted with the prompt as it stands, and rewriting it underneath would leave that job's record describing a submission that never happened; `REPLACE_ASSET_IN_FLIGHT` is that route's own sentence and is unchanged. A **rendered or approved** shot *is* refreshed, which is `expansion_write_refusal`'s existing carve-out rather than a new decision: the prompt each take was submitted with is recorded on its job and in the take's own PNG metadata, and the Director has already ruled that a citation change on a rendered shot is fine because the take is untouched. A shot with **no** expansion is left without one; nothing manufactures a map for it.

**One new field, defaulted, classified everywhere it had to be.** `Shot.h3_prompt_map: str = ""` — plan content, so a duplicate carries it with the prompt it describes (a copy holding an expansion whose map nothing could check would be an unverifiable claim); withheld from the Director's context dump beside `h3_prompt`, on the same never-been-in grounds and the same "bookkeeping, not a plan fact" argument; and mirrored into `api.js`'s `SHOT_PLAN_CONTENT_FIELDS`, which a contract test holds identical to the model's. The three classification sets partition `Shot` exactly, and the application refuses to start with an unclassified field, so both of those were failures before they were edits.

**And it is server-owned on the two generic writes, which is the recorded hole in this repository met again.** A whole-manifest body binds a defaulted model, so a client written before the field existed simply omits it, it arrives as `""`, and one ordinary save would erase the recorded map on every shot at once — silently retiring the refusal. `_adopt_expansion_maps` runs on both `PUT`s before the refresh: a body whose `h3_prompt` equals the stored one keeps the stored record and the body's value is ignored; a body whose `h3_prompt` differs is the inspector's box being written by hand, and is stamped with the current map, which is what lets someone fix a stale document expansion themselves; a blank expansion records no map.

**Tests: fifteen, in `tests/test_api.py`, every one asserting the model call count against a recording double.** Attach refreshes with zero model calls and the result equals `song_audio_prose` byte for byte; detach does too, including the trailing detach a per-line comparison would miss and the last-citation case; `replace-citations` refreshes all three shots it rewrote in one pass, no call; **the headline** — a stale document expansion is not rewritten, costs no second call, and is refused at `generate/h3` with `comfy.prompts` empty, then goes through after one explicit expansion via `mark-draft`; clearing the box is the other way out and submits the correct map with no model at all; a locked shot is not rewritten, answers `EXPAND_PROMPT_LOCKED` verbatim, and is refused at submit; an in-flight shot is not rewritten and `replace-citations` skips it in `REPLACE_ASSET_IN_FLIGHT`'s own words; a `reference_labels` rename refreshes that shot and no other; an appearance anchor refreshes every map that names it, on a route that writes no shot; the whole-project `PUT` refreshes *and* cannot clear the recorded map from a body that omits it; the stale-client reassertion is re-derived; a shot with no expansion is never given one and still submits on the fallback; an expansion written before the field existed is not called stale and renders unchanged; `Shot.prompt` is asserted intact per shot across all four write paths in one sweep; and the recorded map is pinned equal to the sentence the fallback builds. `comfy.prompts` is asserted empty on every path that must not submit.

**Twenty-six mutations, twenty-six killed — but six of them only after six more tests, and every one of those six was a real hole.** The first sweep killed 20. The survivors: the staleness guard dropped from the refresh sweep (which regenerates a *hand-edited* prose on every unrelated save — the guard is not an optimisation); `use_song_audio` and the resolved mode dropped from the rebuild condition (each cements the prose form onto a shot the expansion route would have sent to the specialist — turning the song off, or moving the mode to `first_last`, are both ordinary inspector edits); the generic-write guard recording no map for hand-written text (which would leave a Director who had already corrected a stale prompt told to correct it again); `apply_expansions` not recording the map (the **sibling gap** — the single-shot route was tested and the whole-plan sweep that shares its committer was not, so a plan expanded the way plans are actually expanded would have carried no map at all and the refusal would simply never have fired); and the assistant fill's refresh, which nothing drove because every other test of that route uses shots that have never been expanded. Six tests written, all six then killed.

**The sibling gap is also closed structurally, not one instance at a time.** A contract test parses `app.py` with `ast`, attributes every `h3_prompt` / `h3_prompt_map` assignment to its **innermost** enclosing function — necessary, since every route is nested inside `create_app` and a scan crediting the outer function would pass on any file that recorded a map anywhere — and asserts that a function writing an expansion also records the map. A third writer added later fails there rather than shipping undetectable. It asserts it found the writers it reasons about, so it cannot silently assert nothing. A second test drives **both** whole-shot write routes from one list, because `PUT /projects/{id}` and `PUT /shots` have been the same hole at different times. What is *not* structural is the route table above: there is no honest way to infer "this handler can move a citation" from source — the five that can do it do it through five different mechanisms — so that table was verified by hand, route by route.

**Two process notes, both learned the hard way this session.** The first sweep ran in the shared working tree, so another agent's concurrent full-suite runs each landed inside one mutation window and saw a single failing test that rotated between runs and passed in isolation — reported, correctly, as a flake. It was not one: `ps` showed the harness live. **A mutation sweep belongs in an isolated copy**, and the re-sweep used one. That copy then needs `PYTHONPATH` pointed at its own `src`, or the venv's editable install serves the live module and every mutation reports as a survivor — guarded here by asserting `music_video_producer.__file__` resolves inside the copy *and* by a **sentinel** mutation that deliberately breaks `reference_map_sentence` and must fail before any verdict is believed. It failed in 10 s. Restores by `read_bytes`/`write_bytes`, anchors converted to each file's own line ending with the match count asserted before every write, SHA-256 verified after every restore, `__pycache__` purged, every run time-boxed. The live tree was verified afterwards by the *shape* of its diff — `git diff -U0` on `app.py` contains exactly one deletion in the whole file, the line deliberately replaced — because a harness that snapshotted a mutant as its baseline would pass its own hash check and still be wrong.

**Nothing was rendered, no model was called, the GPU was not touched, and `data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10` was read and never written.** Suite: **1481 passing**, run three times consecutively with nothing else touching the tree and identical each time (1446 baseline; this change contributes 24 test functions, 23 in `tests/test_api.py` and one in `tests/test_assistant.py`) in a tree shared with another agent's frontend work. Ruff clean, `node --check` clean on both modules.

**Found and not fixed.** Three things stale a stored expansion that this change deliberately does not cover, because none of them is the reference map. Editing `Shot.prompt` — by hand, by `clean-prompts`, or by the chat Director — changes the intent the prose is built from; editing a section's prompt changes the "Section look" tail; and both already have the explicit Expand Prompt Again affordance the Director uses. Extending the refresh to those would make an intent keystroke rewrite the machine prompt under the Director's hands, which is a larger decision than this one. Separately: readiness is untouched. A stale map is refused at the submit route rather than reported in `readiness_report`, because `window_warnings` is documented as window notes only and `blocking` would need `READINESS_REFUSAL` — which says "no prompt on {shots}" and is shared with two other routes — reworded to carry a second kind. The consequence is honest and small: the browser's batch pre-flight still offers a stale shot, and `generate_batch` then skips it by name with this route's own sentence, which is that route's documented per-shot skip-and-report behaviour.

## 2026-08-21 — A take is a take whoever is touching it: every write of a shot's `start` now goes through one anchoring door, and the neighbour's own lock decides

**The Director's ruling, verbatim.** *"Well we dont want to slide the take next to the one we are adjusting either, rather move its windows edge while both clips stay in place, same for double click, those gestures should only slide the window bounds but leave the clip position intact."*

**This supersedes a decision recorded earlier the same day**, in the entry below on the music lock. That entry found the two uncompensated paths and left them, reasoning that "the neighbour is not the shot under the hand" and that a double-click closing a 0.002 s gap "must not silently re-time a take". The Director has overruled both. The principle is about the take, not about which clip the pointer is on — and the second reasoning was inverted: moving a window 0.015 s earlier and leaving `trim_nudge` alone *is* the re-timing. The earlier gap-fill decision is superseded, not contradicted quietly.

**The rule has one home, and that is the whole point of the change.** A take's anchor is `start − lead − nudge`, the song second its first frame plays at, so any write to `start` that does not compensate slides that take off the music. `api.anchoredNudge(shot, {from, to, nudge, unlocked})` is the entire rule, executed under node, with three answers: a take **and** locked, the nudge moves by exactly the seconds the window moved; **no take**, nothing to anchor; **unlocked**, the Director is repositioning deliberately and the take travels. `app.moveWindowStart` is the one function in the panel that assigns a shot's `start`, and it is the only thing that has to remember to ask. This was not fixed as two patched call sites — patching sites is precisely how it came to be applied at two gestures out of four.

**The site table**, every place a shot's `start` is written in the frontend:

| Site | Moves a `start`? | Compensated before | Compensated after |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `bindClip` move-drag (`app.js`) | the dragged shot's | yes, lock-aware | yes, through the door |
| `bindClip` left-edge drag | the dragged shot's | yes, but from `latest_output` — the lock was ignored | yes, and the lock now selects the floor too |
| `bindClip` right-edge drag | **no** — `duration` only | n/a | n/a; `duration` is not in the anchor |
| `applyPlayheadSnap` → this shot's window | on a left-edge snap | yes | yes, through the door |
| `applyPlayheadSnap` → **the shared neighbour's window** | on a right-edge snap | **no** — the reported case | **yes**, by the neighbour's own lock |
| `applyPlayheadSnap` restore before planning | restores `start`+`duration`+`nudge` as one pair | n/a (a restore) | n/a — now `restoreWindow`, which must never compensate |
| `runGapFill`, **leftward** | this shot's own | **no** — the reported case | **yes** |
| `runGapFill`, rightward | **no** — `duration` only | n/a | n/a |
| `updateShotFromInspector` (the Start box) | the selected shot's | no | yes — the same edit written as a number |
| `#add-shot`, `#duplicate-shot`, `#split-shot` | a **new** shot's | n/a | n/a — `newShotFromPlan` carries no `latest_output`, so there is no take to anchor; the split's original keeps its `start` |
| `stepHistory` (undo/redo) | restores a whole snapshotted list | no | **no, deliberately** |
| section-pill drag | a *section's* | n/a | n/a — sections have no takes |

A contract test now scans `app.js` for every `.start =` and fails unless it is a section's, or inside `moveWindowStart`, or inside `restoreWindow`. A new gesture cannot quietly become the fifth half-applied site.

**Whose lock governs a neighbour: the neighbour's own.** When a gesture on A writes B's `start`, it is B's take that must stay on the music, so it is B's toggle that decides — and B's toggle is the one a Director would go and untick if they wanted B's take to travel. This falls out of the design rather than being a special case: `moveWindowStart` reads `unlockedFromMusic.has(shot.id)` for whichever shot it is moving. Driven in the browser: shot_08 unticked while the pointer never leaves shot_07, and the same drag then lets shot_08's take travel while shot_07 stays locked throughout.

**Gap fill has two directions and only one of them moves a start.** The brief anticipated that filling rightward moves the *next* shot's start; the code does not do that. `gapFillPlan` never touches the neighbour in either direction — rightward grows this shot's `duration` and leaves its `start` exactly where it was; leftward runs this shot's own `start` back to meet the clip behind it. So the leftward fill compensates and the rightward one has nothing to compensate. Both are executed offline and driven in the browser.

**Undo and redo must not compensate, and that is asserted twice.** A snapshot already holds the `start` and the `trim_nudge` that belonged together when it was taken; running a restore through the rule would move every anchored take by the amount the gesture had just been rolled back by. `stepHistory` adopts the server's reply wholesale and contains no call to the door — pinned by a source test — and the browser undoes and redoes a snapped neighbour and compares **start, nudge and anchor together**, because a double-application that moved `start` and `trim_nudge` by the same amount would leave the anchor right and the plan wrong.

**Nothing clamps, refuses or snaps back on take-coverage grounds.** The amber reports; it never constrains. The one clamp that remains is the left edge's floor at the take's own first frame, which is a real bound and pre-existing — and it now goes with the lock, because a floor holding a cut off a frame the take is no longer anchored to bounds nothing.

**Exact seconds, never the 1/24 s grid**, everywhere, and the executed test carries the numbers a browser measured the defect in: a 1.608 s move of a window starting at 32.517 s written as a 1.625 s nudge, leaving the take 17 ms off the music with every offline assertion green. The prohibition on `grid(original.nudge` is kept.

**Tests.** Four in `tests/test_frontend_contract.py` — the rule executed over its three answers plus the off-grid case, a hundred-pointermove drag and the not-a-number guard; the structural scan that every start write goes through the one door; the neighbour's-lock test, which also executes `gapFillPlan` in both directions to show which one moves a start; and the undo test. Seven new subsections in `tests/e2e_timeline_edit.py` (§13a–g), every assertion `start − lead − nudge` read off the **stored manifest**: the snapped neighbour's anchor holds to the microsecond while its window moves 0.7045 s; undo and redo restore the pair exactly; the neighbour's own lock, unticked, lets its take travel; the leftward gap fill closes the Director's own 0.015 s residue with the anchor unmoved and the rightward one writes no nudge at all; the typed Start box holds the anchor across a 2.25 s move; the left edge holds it in both directions and its floor goes with the lock; and a **six-step** move drag, because every other drag in that file is one `move_by_offset` and therefore exactly one `pointermove`.
