**Eleven mutations, eleven killed — but two of them only after the tests were made stronger, and both were browser-only.** Compensating regardless of lock or take; writing the delta instead of adding it; re-gridding the compensation; the lock of the clip under the hand instead of the moving shot's; the snap writing the neighbour's start raw; the gap fill writing its own start raw; the left edge deciding from `latest_output`; the inspector writing raw; undo compensating on top of its snapshot; the drag compounding once per pointermove; and the restore dropping the nudge. **The compounding mutation survived section 12 and 13a–f entirely**, because a Selenium `move_by_offset` is a single `pointermove` and the bug is invisible at N=1 — §13g exists for it. **The left-edge mutation survived its first browser run too**: after the refactor that branch only selects the *floor*, and 13f dragged rightward where no floor binds; it now drags leftward into the floor in both lock states. Restores by `read_bytes`/`write_bytes` with anchors converted to each file's own line ending, the match count asserted before every write, SHA-256 verified after every restore, and — after the coordination note about a concurrent writer — the snapshot re-taken from the live files immediately before each mutation rather than from a run-start baseline.

**Nothing was rendered, no model was called, the GPU was not touched, and `data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10` was read and never written.** Suite: **1472 passing** in a shared tree carrying two other agents' work of the same evening; this change contributes three net test functions to the 1446 baseline (four added, one replaced). Ruff clean, `node --check` clean on both modules, and the full browser harness green end to end — all thirteen sections, no severe console entries, zero manifest read races.

## 2026-08-21 — The two-second poll was the application's most frequent thief: `/render-status` now saves by compare-and-swap, and a tick that loses is simply the next tick

**The defect, and the take it cost.** `read_render_status` did what every route does — read the manifest, mutate it, save it — except that it does so **every two seconds, per open project**, and it holds its copy across a `/queue` call and a round of `/history`. Anything saved inside that window was written from a copy that predates it, so the tick's whole-manifest write put it back. The case the Director brought: a submission now writes its `RenderJob` record, submits, and stamps the accepted `prompt_id` back (the record-first ruling below). A tick that read between those two saves reverted the stamp; the record kept `PENDING_SUBMISSION_PROMPT_ID`; the reconciler then settled it as **never submitted while the render was executing on the GPU**, and the take landed on disk attached to nothing. Nothing about that is specific to submissions — a shot edit, an approval, a section, a document, anything saved in the window was equally reversible.

**The store's existing guard does not catch this, and that was checked before anything was built.** `ProjectChangedDuringSave` fires in exactly one place: `_replace_atomically`, after a **replace backoff**, when the per-manifest write count moved while this save was waiting on a foreign handle. The ordinary read/mutate/save has no contention on the replace at all — `Path.replace` succeeds on the first attempt, the backoff never runs, the count is never consulted, and the older manifest lands **silently, with both callers told they succeeded**. Reproduced in isolation first (A reads, B reads-mutates-saves, A saves: no exception, B's change gone) and now pinned as a store test, so the scoping cannot be misremembered later.

**Compare-and-swap, chosen over re-apply and over a narrowed write.** `ProjectStore.read_for_update` returns the project *and* the write generation it was read at — the same `_MANIFEST_WRITES` counter the backoff guard already keeps, handed out rather than only consulted — and `save(project, if_generation=...)` refuses, under the manifest lock and before writing its temp file, if that count has moved. Taking the payload and the count under one acquisition is what makes the token honest; comparing them under the lock that every writer holds at the instant of its replace is what makes the check atomic with the write it guards.

*Re-apply* — re-read after reconciling and copy the decided fields across — was rejected for the field list it requires: today that is a job's `status`, `error`, `missing_ticks` and `output_files` plus everything `apply_job_history` writes onto a Shot, an Asset or the Song, and it would have to be kept in step by hand forever after, with a silent revert as the failure mode when it drifts. *Narrowing the write* to only-changed job records was rejected as a fix (a narrower whole-manifest save is still a whole-manifest save, because the manifest is one file) but adopted as prevention: the tick already writes only when `outcome.changed`, which is what makes collisions rare enough for a retry to be affordable, and that is now asserted on the file's mtime as well as on `updated_at`.

**On refusal the tick re-reads and reconciles again, rather than re-applying.** That costs one more `/queue` on collision and buys two things a field list cannot: the verdict is always derived from the manifest as it now stands, and `missing_ticks` therefore increments **exactly once per tick** — the discarded pass is thrown away whole, so a contended tick cannot settle a record early.

**What the poll owns, stated so it can be audited.** Each open job's `status`, `error`, `missing_ticks` and `output_files`; and what `batch.apply_job_history` writes onto what a finished job produced — a Shot's `status`, `latest_output` and `latest_review`, an Asset's `path`, the Song's `path`. Every one of those is *derived from ComfyUI's answer* and authored nowhere else. It owns **no** shot window, prompt, citation, approval, Director-made asset, section or document, and it may never be the reason one of those moves. After this change it cannot be: the only manifest it can write is one it read after the last writer landed.

**The bound is three attempts, and exhausting it is deliberately silent.** Three because the collision it exists for is a single foreign write inside one tick's window, which one re-read clears; the second and third cover a burst, which a batch submission genuinely produces. Larger would be worse — each attempt is another `/queue` and another round of `/history`, and a tick that cannot win three in a row is losing to a writer that is still going. Exhausting the bound drops **only the write**: the report still describes what the tick learned, and the tick two seconds later re-reads, asks ComfyUI the same question and reaches the same verdict, because this reconciliation is derived rather than authored. So the route goes on absorbing `ProjectChangedDuringSave` rather than surfacing it — a toast every time the Director types would be the worse bug — and both sources of that exception (the new check and the older backoff guard) now land in the same handler, since both mean "your copy is stale, re-read". No user-initiated write may copy any of this: there, the caller is owed the 409.

**Cost per tick: unmeasurable.** Against the Director's own manifest — 124 KB, 33 rendered takes, copied to a scratch root and never written — `read_for_update` and `save(if_generation=…)` are a single dictionary lookup more than `get` and `save`, and both measured *inside the noise* of the calls they replace (read 0.67 ms vs 0.76 ms, save 1.85 ms vs 2.54 ms over 100–200 runs). No second serialisation, no second parse, no extra file read on the uncontended path — the token is a count this process already keeps. The extra pass exists only on a genuine collision.

**Tests: eight, every one deterministic by ordering.** The handshake is a hook on the fake ComfyUI's `/queue`, which *is* the window by construction — `reconcile_render_jobs` reads the project, then awaits `/queue`, then the route saves — so no thread and no clock is involved and it cannot flake in either direction. Three in `tests/test_store.py`: the unguarded read/mutate/save reverts and raises nothing (the scoping pin above); a save naming its generation is refused once the manifest moves, with the other writer's bytes intact and no temp file left; and the rest of the truth table — an uncontended save lands, the token moves with the write, and a save to a *different* project is not a reason to refuse this one. Five in `tests/test_api.py`: the defect itself (the Director's edit survives **and** the tick's own verdict still lands); the reported case, set up on the last unknown tick before the settle, where the accepted `prompt_id` survives and the render is reported running instead of never-submitted; a genuinely orphaned record still settling on the third unknown tick and **not the second**, with a foreign write landing inside every tick; a tick that changed nothing writing no manifest at all, asserted on `st_mtime_ns` as well as `updated_at`; and the retry landing on the third attempt. The existing exhaustion test keeps its name and gains the attempt and `/queue` counts.

**Eight mutations, eight killed.** The compare-and-swap dropped from the tick (the defect, restored — three fail); no retry at all; the bound off by one; the changed-nothing guard removed; the exhausted tick raising instead of absorbing; the retry reusing its stale copy instead of re-reading; the store's refusal deleted outright; and `read_for_update` handing back a token that is not what it read at. The bound mutation initially **survived**, because both bound tests computed their expectations from the constant they were checking and moved with it — the retry test now asserts the number as a literal, which is the only way that branch is pinned. Anchors converted to each file's own line ending (`app.py` CRLF, `store.py` LF) with the match count asserted before every write, restores verified by SHA-256, `__pycache__` purged, every run time-boxed.

**Found and not fixed.** `GET /jobs/{job_id}`, the manual per-job refresh, is the same read/mutate/save shape and can revert the same way — left alone deliberately: it is user-initiated, it is not a loop, and a silent retry is the wrong answer for a click, which is owed its 409. And a mid-submission settle sets `shot.status = "error"` while `accept_submission` reopens only the *job*, so a shot can read `error` for the rest of a render that is genuinely running (it self-heals when the take lands). That is existing, deliberate behaviour the brief scoped out; it is worth a Director's opinion.

**Nothing was rendered, no model was called, the GPU was not touched, and `data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10` was read and never written.** Suite: **1454 passing** in a shared tree — that figure carries another agent's frontend work of the same evening; this change contributes eight test functions to the 1446 baseline measured before it — ruff clean, `node --check` clean on both modules, and no frontend file was touched.

## 2026-08-21 — The drag and the take: a move now slides the window over a take that stays on the music, with a lock beside the nudge and a second amber when the window leaves what its take covers

> **Partly superseded the same day.** Two decisions in this entry — that a snapped neighbour's uncompensated `start` was "a different gesture with a different rule (the neighbour is not the shot under the hand)", and that the gap-fill double-click should leave `trim_nudge` alone — were overruled by the Director. See *"A take is a take whoever is touching it"* above. Everything else here stands.

**The Director's ask, verbatim.** *"when I said Trim nudge I was talking specifically about the section in the Shots info panel that lets us nudge the clip along the timeline. When dragging in the timeline though it would just move the window over the clip but keep the clip aligned where it belongs with the music. Perhaps a lock/unlock from timeline toggle in the shots info panel may be useful next to that nudge input so that dragging a b-roll clip would be easier (default locked)."* And, on the warning: *"if the bounds of the shots window are dragged beyond where that clip covers then the shot would turn yellow to warn that the bounds was gone past. The user could then readjust or regenerate to fit the newly wanted shot timeframe. This should help prevent lipsync clips from being dragged away from where it actually matches up with the music."*

**Both behaviours already existed and were on the wrong gestures.** The *left edge* of a rendered clip has moved `start` while `trim_nudge` compensated since 2026-08-20 — which is exactly "the window moves over the take" — while the whole-clip **move** wrote `start` alone and carried the take with it, under a comment saying `trim_nudge` was "deliberately left alone even on a rendered shot". So nothing new was invented: the move now does what the edge does, and the toggle puts the old behaviour back for one shot when the Director wants it. The **TRIM NUDGE −1f / +1f / Reset** row is untouched, which was the Director's own ruling about it.

**The right edge needs no lock behaviour, and that is a conclusion from the code rather than a guess.** A right-edge drag writes `duration` and nothing else. The song second the take's first frame plays at is `start − lead − nudge`, which does not contain `duration` — so the take cannot move under that gesture however far it is dragged. What a right-edge drag *can* do is ask for more picture than the take holds, and that is the second half of this change: it turns the clip amber, which is the right answer for it. One path deserved a second look and was checked: **snapping a right edge to the playhead moves the neighbour's `start`** through `boundaryMovePlan`, uncompensated. That is a real way to move a take that is not the dragged shot's, and it is left as it was — deliberately, and reported below rather than silently fixed, because it is a different gesture with a different rule (the neighbour is not the shot under the hand) and changing it was not this task.

**Persistence: per shot, session only, never a model field.** Held in `app.js` as a `Set` of shot ids, cleared when the project on screen changes and never on a refresh — the precedent of the two line mutes, the snap magnet and the seed randomizer. The argument is sharper than precedent, though. What is durable about a b-roll clip is *where it sits*, and where it sits is `start`/`trim_nudge`, which are persisted fields this gesture already writes. The unlock itself is a working mode — "let me drag this one freely" — and a persisted unlock would be a **trap**: it would sit on a lip-sync shot for ever and let a drag months later pull its take off the words it was rendered against, which is the exact failure the Director asked for this to prevent. Session-only fails closed on every reload. It is per shot rather than one flag for the workspace — where this differs from the seed randomizer — because unlocking is dangerous on the *next* shot, so it must not leak to it. No new field on `Shot`, and therefore nothing new for the generic full-project `PUT` to write back, which is this repository's recorded guard hole.

**A shot with no take shows no toggle at all.** The control lives inside the trim-nudge row, so it is drawn on exactly the shots the nudge is drawn on. That is the honest answer for a control that would otherwise be live and do nothing, and it is asserted in a browser: on a take-less shot neither `#trim-nudge` nor `#take-anchor` is in the document.

**The amber composes with the band rather than inventing a second mechanism.** One new note kind — `batch.NOTE_KIND_TAKE_UNCOVERED` — in the *same* `window_warnings` list the shot-length band already uses, read by the *same* `windowWarningsByShot`, drawn in the *same* `--amber` with the same two marks. Nothing client-side re-derives it: the server computes it from `over_render_frames(latest_take_duration) / H3_FPS` (the picture the render asked H3 for, from the window it asked with) against `latest_take_lead + trim_nudge` and the live `duration`, with half a frame of tolerance at each end. Both ends warn — a window dragged back before the take's first frame and one that runs off its end. A shot can now carry **two** window notes, so `windowWarningsByShot` gained an explicit precedence — coverage outranks the band, because the band is a standing property of the plan while coverage is something that just happened under a gesture the Director can undo — and the readiness list still prints both sentences in full, each under its own heading (`Past the take`).

**A take that recorded no window of its own is never warned about, and never guessed at.** `latest_take_duration == 0` means "never snapshotted": every take rendered before that field existed — including all 33 in the Director's own project, which was read and not written — and every hand-picked clip, whose bookkeeping `select_shot_clip` clears. The only window such a take has on the manifest is the *live* one, which is a fact about the plan rather than about the file; checking against it would report a shot as uncovered precisely *because* it had been edited. Silence, deliberately, on `RESTORE_AUDIO_UNDESCRIBED_TAKE`'s own reasoning.

**The warning never constrains, which was the non-negotiable.** No clamp, no refusal, no snap-back was added anywhere. The locked move-drag is explicitly *not* floored at the take's first frame (the left edge keeps its own floor, unchanged, and the nudge buttons keep theirs): a window dragged off its take is a real, representable state that turns amber and that `assembly` refuses at export with the numbers. A negative scan test holds it — no line in `api.js` or `app.js` that mentions the new kind may also mention `disabled` or a refusal, and the move branch may not contain a `clamp(`.

**A defect the browser found that no offline assertion could.** The compensation was written as `grid(original.nudge + (shot.start − original.start))`, copying the left edge. The window steps to the frame grid, so a window that did not *start* on the grid moves by an off-grid amount — and re-gridding the compensation rounds it to a different number than the window moved by. Measured in Edge: a 1.608 s move of a shot starting at 32.517 s wrote a **1.625 s** nudge and put the take **17 ms off the music**, with every source assertion green. All three sites now use `exactSeconds` — the move, the left edge (which carried the same defect) and `applyPlayheadSnap`, which already did — and a test forbids `grid(original.nudge` anywhere in the file.

**What was driven in a real browser** (`tests/e2e_timeline_edit.py`, section 12, self-hosting Edge, empty data root, no GPU and no model): the plan is given a take through `PUT /shots` exactly as the H3 route stamps one at submission, and every verdict is read back off the **stored manifest** as `start − lead − nudge`, the song second the take's first frame plays at.

* The lock is inside `#trim-nudge`, hit-tested, ticked by default, and its title says it is not the shot lock two rows above.
* **Locked move-drag**: `start` 32.517 → 34.125, `trim_nudge` 0 → 1.608, take anchor **32.267 → 32.267 (unchanged)**.
* **Unlocked move-drag**, same clip, same distance back: `start` 34.125 → 32.5, `trim_nudge` **unchanged** at 1.608, take anchor **32.267 → 30.642 (moved)**.
* Toggling the lock sent **zero** `PUT /shots`.
* Dragged 5 s further: the clip takes `take-uncovered`, its border resolves from the acid 1 px to `rgb(255, 180, 84)` at 2 px, and its title *and* accessible name carry the sentence. Split, duplicate, delete and add all stay live, and the window really did move where it was dragged.
* The readiness list reads *"Past the take - SHOT 07 (shot_07): This shot's window runs 2.191s past the end of its take. The take was rendered for 10.125s of picture…"* — the take's **recorded** window, not the live one, which is the number a client re-deriving from the plan would get wrong.
* The take with no snapshot, carrying a 9 s nudge no coverage could survive, stays plain and is named nowhere in the report.

**Tests: 16 new cases** (1430 → **1446**). Nine on the pure server verdict in `tests/test_batch.py` — including both ends asserted at their own edges rather than in the middle, the legacy take, the shot that is both long and uncovered, the `include_warnings=False` hot path, and that nothing is written back onto the Shot. Seven executed in `tests/test_frontend_contract.py` — the three states of `takeAnchorControl`, the persistence decision asserted as a *negative* against `models.py`, the drag rule read off `bindClip` (anchored inside that function, because the section pills have a `mode === "move"` branch of their own), the toggle's position and its silence, the amber's class/sentence/stylesheet, the two-note precedence in both list orders, and a route-to-browser round trip over a real `readiness_report`.

**Seventeen mutations, every one killed.** Fifteen offline: the legacy guard removed; the front edge's sign flipped; the overrun's tolerance dropped; the note computed and never reported; the take's length read off the live window instead of the snapshot; the uncovered kind falling through to the silent one; the precedence reversed; the class drawn without its sentence; the lock ignoring the unlock; the lock offered without a take; the readiness line mislabelled; the unlock surviving a project change; the toggle saving the plan; the compensation re-gridded; and the stylesheet's amber replaced. Two **browser-only**, each leaving the whole offline suite green: the toggle's two arms swapped (both spellings still present, so every string assertion passes — the browser caught it on the unlocked drag writing `trim_nudge` −0.017), and the decided window state computed but never applied to the clip's class list (`clipWindowState` still called with the right kind — the browser caught it on the band's own section timing out). Private scratch directory, anchors converted to each file's own line ending with the match count asserted before every write, restores verified by SHA-256 against pre-run digests, `__pycache__` purged, every run time-boxed.

**Gates.** 1446 passing, ruff clean, `node --check` clean on `app.js` and `api.js`. `app.py` was not edited — the coordination boundary held; `batch.py` was, minimally and additively (one kind, two sentences, one tolerance, one pure function and its five-line call site in `readiness_report`), because the Director's verdict had to come from the server rather than be re-derived in the browser. Nothing was rendered, no model was called, ComfyUI and the GPU were not touched, and `data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10` was read and never written.

**Left for the Director.**
1. **Snapping a right edge to the playhead still moves the neighbour's take.** `boundaryMovePlan` moves both shots at the cut and only the *dragged* shot's nudge is compensated, so the neighbour's window slides over its own take by the same amount. Found while answering the right-edge question and deliberately not changed: it is a different gesture, and the amber now reports the result of it. The same is true of the double-click gap fill, which moves `start` with `trim_nudge` untouched by an explicit earlier ruling.
2. **The coverage note reads the *latest* take while assembly judges the *approved* one.** Where the two differ — a shot re-rendered after approval — the amber describes the newer file and the export refusal describes the older. Both are honest about what they measured; nothing reconciles them, and nothing needs to until a Director hits it.
3. **A pre-existing flake in this harness, fixed in the script only.** Section 11's sweep button went stale between `find_element` and `click` on two of three runs — `loadProject` fires the readiness GET without awaiting it and its reply rebuilds the cuts bar. `stable_click` (copied from `e2e_section_looks.py`) closes it. No application code was involved.

## 2026-08-21 — The record goes first, then the graph: a save race can no longer answer 409 for a render that is already on the GPU

**The defect, and the night it appeared.** Every submission route in `app.py` submitted its graph to ComfyUI and *then* saved the job record. That was harmless while `ProjectStore.save` could not fail on contention. It stopped being harmless the same evening `save` gained its `ProjectChangedDuringSave` refusal (the lost-update fix): from then on a save that lost a race answered **409 for a prompt already accepted**. The GPU rendered, the take landed in ComfyUI's output tree, and nothing recorded it — no job, no `latest_output`, no `Shot.status`, no path anywhere in the manifest by which the application could ever find the file. The previous entry left it as *"the store contract's blast radius, not this finding's"*, naming `restore_song_audio`, `generate_h3` and `enhance_with_ltx25`.

**The Director's ruling: save the job record first, then submit.** A save race then refuses *before any GPU time is spent*, which is the cheap direction to fail. The stated cost was accepted with it: a record briefly exists for a graph that is not queued yet, so a crash in that window leaves an orphan — and reconciliation is supposed to heal exactly that.

**Nine call sites, not three.** They were enumerated off the live app rather than grepped, because the existing contract test already does that (`app_py_submitting_routes` reads the source of every route whose body calls `comfy.submit`). All nine had the same shape and all nine were reversed: `generate_h3`, `restore_song_audio`, `enhance_with_ltx25`, `generate_music`, `generate_songplanner`, `generate_flux`, `generate_multiview`, `edit_asset`, `fill_assets`. A new contract test holds every one of them — and any route added later — to `store.save` before `comfy.submit`, the `RenderJob` constructed on the near side, and `settle_unsubmitted_jobs`/`accept_submission` strictly on the far side.

**What `prompt_id` holds in the window: `PENDING_SUBMISSION_PROMPT_ID`, the string `"pending-submission"`.** Deliberately **not** the empty string, which this application already spends on an unrelated meaning: an empty `prompt_id` is the *local-work* marker, keyed on by `heal_orphaned_local_jobs`, by the assemble route's busy check and by `api.js`'s assembly-progress branch, and it is excluded from `reconcilable_jobs` because nothing on ComfyUI can answer for it. An `h3` or `post` record carrying an empty id would therefore be read as an ffmpeg job — reconciled by nobody, and for `kind="post"` healed to an *assembly* error at the next startup. The non-empty sentinel gets the opposite treatment with no new rule anywhere: the record is reconcilable, so the poll watches it and `shot_render_in_flight` reads the shot as busy; ComfyUI's queue never contains that string and its history answers `known=False`; `ComfyClient.cancel` is a no-op against it (an id in no bucket is deleted and no `/interrupt` follows); and it is not a UUID shape, so it can never be mistaken for one ComfyUI minted.

**Reconciliation does heal the orphan — verified, not assumed.** `batch.reconcile_render_jobs`, `queue_locations` and `heal_orphaned_local_jobs_at_startup` were read and then driven: a record carrying the sentinel is absent from `/queue`, answers `known=False` from `/history`, accumulates `missing_ticks`, and at `MISSING_TICKS_LIMIT` settles to `error` with the h3 shot released from its in-flight status — the same three-unknown-tick rule that ends a prompt killed by a cleared queue. Two tests prove it, one on the pure reconciler and one driven through `/render-status` end to end, ending in `active: false`. Startup healing is *not* the mechanism here and must not become one: it deliberately never touches a job carrying a `prompt_id`, because ComfyUI outlives this process.

**One thing the ruling did not name, and it had to be fixed for the claim to hold.** The wording. `JOB_LOST_WITH_QUEUE` says *"ComfyUI no longer knows this prompt — its queue was cleared, restarted, or crashed"*, which was never true of a graph that was never sent. The reconciler now settles a sentinel-carrying record with `JOB_NEVER_SUBMITTED` instead — same terminal state, same last two sentences (*"Nothing was produced. Render again re-opens the shot."*), an explanation that is true. The submission routes settle their own failed record with the identical constant, so one state has one sentence.

**And the window is wider than the settle, which the reconciler cannot know.** The pre-submission VRAM eject is allowed twenty seconds and the `/prompt` call thirty; three ticks of the browser's two-second poll is six. A slow-but-successful submission can therefore find its own record already settled as never submitted — a verdict that was right on the evidence and is wrong the moment ComfyUI answers. So `batch.accept_submission` writes the prompt id *and* reopens the record (`status` back to `queued`, `error` cleared, `missing_ticks` zeroed). Without that, a record left `error` beside a live prompt id is never reconciled again — precisely the orphaned take this whole ordering exists to prevent.

**Only the job record goes in the pre-submission save, and that is what keeps the failure path honest.** Everything else each route writes is what the *acceptance* means, and none of it may be written for a submission that never happened: the Shot's `status`/`prompt_id`/take snapshot, an Asset appended to the library, and — the one that would have been a real regression — the Song. `generate_music` and `generate_songplanner` replace `project.song` wholesale, which is the destructive act `_require_song_replacement_confirmation` exists to guard; moving it ahead of the submission would trade a lost job record for a lost song on every ComfyUI outage, the expensive direction and the opposite of the ruling. So the record goes first and the replacement still waits for the accept. `supersede_target_jobs` stays after the accept too, for its own docstring's reason. The consequence is that a failed submission leaves **no phantom in-flight shot**: there is no state to restore, because none was written.

**A failed submission settles its record; a save race while settling it is swallowed.** `settle_unsubmitted_jobs` marks the record `error` in `JOB_NEVER_SUBMITTED`'s words and writes the manifest. If *that* save loses a race, the refusal is logged and dropped rather than raised: the caller is on its way to a 502 naming why the submission failed, which is the fact the Director needs, and a 409 about the manifest would report the wrong failure. Nothing is lost — the record is left carrying the sentinel, which is exactly what the reconciler settles, with the same sentence. `fill_assets` uses the same function for its whole un-sent tail, which is also what writes the accepted half of a partial batch to disk (it replaces the old `if submitted: store.save(project)`).

**Tests: fifteen cases, thirteen of them new functions.** The headline is the ruling's own claim — a save race before the submit answers 409 and **`comfy.prompts` stays empty**, on all three routes the defect was reported against. Ordering is asserted as ordering, not inferred from the end state: a recording transport reads the manifest at the instant the graph is handed to it and then fails the submission, and what it saw is `[{"kind": "h3", "status": "queued", "prompt_id": "pending-submission"}]`. Then: a failed submission settles its record and leaves the Shot at `ready` with an empty `prompt_id` and a zeroed take snapshot, and the next attempt succeeds rather than being refused by the wreckage; a settling save that races is swallowed and the 502 survives; an orphaned record reconciles through `/render-status` to `error`, `JOB_NEVER_SUBMITTED` and `active: false`; the sentinel is asserted *not* to be the local-work marker; `accept_submission` reopens a record the reconciler settled mid-submission; a partly-submitted asset fill leaves one live record and two settled ones with one asset; a music outage does not destroy the song it was going to replace; and the two eject cases — the eject still fires before a submission the record was saved for, and it does not fire, nor even probe the host, when the save refused first. Every existing refusal is unchanged and still passing in its existing words: the readiness gate, in-flight, locked, approved, the three take refusals, and the whole-manifest write guards.

**Twenty-two mutations, every one killed.** In `batch.py`: the sentinel replaced by the empty string; the reconciler forced to each of its two sentences; `accept_submission` in turn not writing the prompt id, not reopening the status, not clearing the error, and not zeroing the counter. In `app.py`: the settled record left `queued`; the settling save removed; the settling save re-raising its race over the 502; the failed record left un-settled; the accepted id never adopted; the Song replaced before the accept; `fill_assets` leaving its un-sent records open; and the shipped defect itself — the save put back after the submit — re-applied to **all seven** single-submission routes and to the fill loop, each killed separately. The `A9` mutation (the Song replaced early) survived the first pass and is why `test_a_music_outage_does_not_destroy_the_song_it_was_going_to_replace` exists. Run in a private scratch directory, anchors converted to each file's own line ending (both are CRLF in this checkout) with the match count asserted before every write, restores verified by SHA-256 against pre-run digests, `__pycache__` purged between runs, every run time-boxed.

**Nothing was rendered, no model was called, ComfyUI and the GPU were not touched**, and `data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10` was never written to. Suite: **1430 passing** in a shared tree that also carries another agent's frontend work of the same evening (its own baseline that hour was 1416); ruff clean, `node --check` clean on both frontend modules, neither of which was edited.

**Left for the Director.**
1. **The poll can still revert the post-submission stamp**, and this change neither introduced nor fixed it. `/render-status` reads the project, reconciles, and saves — an ordinary read/mutate/save, so a tick that read the manifest just before the stamp lands will write its own copy over it a moment later, and "last writer wins" is the standing rule for that everywhere in the application. What is new is only that the pending record can *arm* a tick on a project that would otherwise have been idle. If it happens the record keeps the sentinel and the reconciler settles it as never submitted while the render is in fact running — better than the take being lost entirely, which is what happened before tonight, but still wrong. The fix is a narrower one than this ruling: have the poll's save re-read and re-apply rather than write its whole copy.
2. **A hand cancellation inside the pending window is undone by the acceptance.** `cancel_job` on a record still carrying the sentinel settles it (`comfy.cancel` against the sentinel is a safe no-op), and `accept_submission` then reopens it when ComfyUI answers, because it cannot tell that verdict from the reconciler's. The window is one HTTP call wide and the ComfyUI prompt really is running at that point, so reopening is arguably the honest answer — but it is a decision about what a cancel means, not an arithmetic one, and it was not made here.

## 2026-08-21 — Two Director requests in one panel file: the seed gets a randomize toggle that names when it re-rolls, and the Assets clips library stops eating the sorted sections

**Request 1, verbatim.** *"in the Shots info panel i can see the 'Seed' section, we should shorten that box a bit and add a randomize toggle (1-99999) which would RNG a number and hold it unless regenerate gets hit later with randomize still checked."*

**Decided: "regenerate" is the per-shot Render again, and only when it actually queues.** The panel has three candidates and they are not equally plausible. Render again is the gesture that spends GPU time and whose whole point is a different take; it is also the only client-side place the seed already moves, so the toggle lands where a second source of movement would otherwise collide. Mark ready queues nothing — it flips a status — so a seed that moved there would break a fixed-seed comparison for no render at all. Generate All strides on the *server*, inside `generate_batch`, and never sees this client's session state. Cancelling Render again's dialog returns before the seed line, so the old contract is untouched: re-opened, seed untouched, nothing queued. **The label says all of this on the screen rather than in a hover** — the control reads `Randomize on Render again`, because a toggle whose re-roll moment has to be guessed is worse than a button, and its title names the two gestures that are *not* it.

**The two sources of seed movement are chosen between in one function, so they cannot both fire.** `nextRenderSeed(shot, randomize)` returns `seed + RESUBMIT_SEED_STRIDE` when the toggle is off — byte-for-byte what the click did before — and a fresh 1–99999 roll *instead of* the stride when it is on. Never both: a random number with a stride added to it is an invisible drift on the one value the Director has just asked to own, and rolling on top of the stride would make the stride's own guarantee unreachable. A roll that lands on the number already stored is nudged one step on, for the reason the stride exists: one take in 99999 that reproduced the previous one exactly would be reported as a bug, not as luck. `0` is deliberately outside the bounds even though the field accepts it — it is what populate and the Flux form leave behind for "nobody chose", and a randomizer that could return it would make "random" and "unset" indistinguishable on the one field where they must be told apart.

**A hand-typed seed clears the toggle**, in the same gesture that types it. Typing a specific number is a statement that you want *that* number, and leaving the box armed would throw it away on the next Render again without saying so. This is why `#shot-seed` is bound apart from the inspector's shared change list: nothing else in the panel has a side effect on another control, and folding it in would mean deciding which element fired from inside a handler that is deliberately field-agnostic.

**Session-wide, session-only, and no new model field.** The flag is a module-level `let` beside the two line mutes and the snap magnet — never persisted, never sent, never in the manifest. A per-shot persisted flag is a new model field, and this codebase's repeat offender is the generic full-project PUT writing every defaulted field back, so a field earns its keep or it is not added. This one would not: what is durable about randomizing is the **number**, and the number is already a persisted per-shot field that this toggle writes. The flag is a working mode — *I want different takes right now* — which is a fact about the session in front of the screen. Session-wide rather than per-shot for the same reason: a Director sweeping ten shots asked for randomness once, not ten times. The visible consequence, stated because it is visible: ticking rolls a seed for the *selected* shot only; another shot keeps its stored seed until its own Render again queues, which is exactly what the label says. A reload leaves the box off, and browser QA asserts that rather than working around it.

**The box is shortened to the number it holds**, 116 px against a 280 px inspector, and the room it gives up carries the toggle on the same line.

**Request 2, verbatim.** *"in Assets the generated clips are eating up all the room and hiding the sorted asset sections and should be their own subtab along with CHaracters/Settings/Props/Style."*

**The panel's filter strip became its tab strip, and the diagnosis was a grid row.** `.library-panel` is `grid-template-rows: auto 1fr`, and the clips library was a *third* child — an implicit third row sized to its content — so thirty-three takes pushed the asset grid up until the sorted sections were off the bottom. Both panes now take the same row and exactly one is ever a grid item: `renderAssets` sets `hidden` on the other. The `[hidden]` rules in the stylesheet are load-bearing, not belt-and-braces — `display: grid`/`block` beats the user agent's `[hidden] { display: none }`, so without them the attribute would be set and both panes painted, which is the report unfixed. No `grid-row` is pinned: a `display: none` element is not a grid item, so the showing pane auto-places on its own, and a pinned row would look like the guarantee while being inert.

**Where the three unnamed asset kinds went: a Media tab.** `models.AssetKind` has **seven** members and the Director named four. `image`, `audio` and `video` are the three that were not named, and they are the default landing kind for every upload — an asset under no tab is one a Director can neither cite, replace nor delete from this screen. They share one tab, `Media`, whose title spells out what is in it. `All` is kept because it is the strip's existing behaviour and the only view that sorts by nothing. A contract test asserts the union of the tabs' `kinds` is exactly `models.AssetKind`, so a kind added later cannot go invisible by omission, and browser QA asserts the same thing from the other end: every asset the All tab draws is drawn by one of the kind tabs too.

**Every affordance still works and the clips did not change, only move.** Upload, Flux generation, Asset Fill, multiview promote, AI Mod, the consistency-prompt field, Attach to selected shot, "Replace in N shot(s) with…" and delete all live in the generator column and the inspector, neither of which the tabs touch. The clips keep hover-to-play and "Open shot". Empty tabs say what is missing in their own words — `No props yet`, `No clips yet` — rather than rendering an empty box that reads as a panel that failed to load.

**On the Director's earlier note that "Attach to selected shot" is hard to use because the timeline is not visible from Assets: it is not made worse, and that is asserted.** Switching tabs changes only `state.assetTab`. The selected shot and the selected asset both survive it, so the attach button stays live on every tab including Clips — driven in the browser, with the button read back as enabled while the Clips tab is showing. The underlying difficulty is untouched and still open.

**The tab strip is built once and updated in place.** A press calls `renderAssets`, which redraws the strip — and a redraw that rewrote `innerHTML` would destroy the button under the pointer, taking its keyboard focus with it. That is the shape of the defect found in this tree on 2026-08-21, where a `dblclick` never fired because `pointerdown` re-rendered every clip. The click handler is delegated off the strip for the same reason.

**Driven in a real browser: `tests/e2e_seed_and_asset_tabs.py`**, a new self-hosting Edge harness on the `e2e_take_swap` pattern, at **four window widths** — 1600, 1100, 950 and 820 CSS px, crossing both of `styles.css`'s breakpoints. Every seed assertion reads the seed back **from the server**, never from the input on screen. Measured there: the box is 116 px against a 280 px inspector and still 116 px against a 681 px one, so it is sized to the number rather than to its container; ticking moved the stored seed 12 → 54243 and holding it across a full inspector teardown (another shot selected, this one re-selected, each dragging its readiness reply) left it at 54243; typing by hand stored 4242 and cleared the toggle; Render again **unticked** stored exactly 4242 → 4343, the server's own +101; Render again **ticked** stored 29785 → 35910, in bounds and not the stride; Mark ready with the toggle still ticked left 35910 alone; a reload left the toggle off. On the Assets side, at every one of the four widths the clips pane paints **0 px** on an asset tab while the grid reaches the bottom of the library panel, and the reverse on the Clips tab; the seven tabs are hit-tested and unclipped at each; pressing a tab with the keyboard leaves that same button focused and still in the document; the clips card's `mouseenter` reaches `play()` exactly once and `mouseleave` `pause()` once; "Open shot" lands on the producing shot with the timeline panel active. **No GPU was spent and the user's ComfyUI was never contacted** — `MVP_COMFY_URL` was pointed at a dead port this run picked, and the one submission the script drives was refused 422 by this application before anything left the machine; the seed lands on the manifest *before* that call, which is the ordering under test. `data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10` was read and never written.

**Tests: six new contract cases plus the browser harness.** The re-roll rule at both ends and past them, including `Math.random() === 1`, which the specification forbids and no engine promises never to hand back; roll-on-tick, hold across two redraws with zero writes, and clear-on-hand-typing, all executed against the workspace rather than grepped; the help text naming Render again, Mark ready and Generate All; the tab table pinned to `models.AssetKind` with no kind claimed twice; per-tab filtering, case-insensitive search inside a tab, and a distinct empty message for every tab; and `renderAssets` executed for one-pane-at-a-time on five tabs. Suite **1416 passing** with this work alone against the `c4eaa5c` baseline of 1410, and **1430 passing** on the final gate run — the tree is shared, and another agent's rewrite of `app.py`'s submitting routes and `batch.py` landed alongside it. While that work was mid-save its own source-enumerating contract tests flapped (`fill_assets`, the eject ordering); they settled green and none of them touches anything changed here. Ruff clean, `node --check` clean on both frontend modules.

**Thirteen mutations, twelve killed.** Offline, all eight killed: the randomize branch inverted; the collision nudge removed; `randomSeed` off by one so 0 becomes reachable; a hand-typed seed no longer clearing the toggle; unticking rolling a seed too; `assetsForTab` ignoring the tab's kinds; the Media tab dropping `audio` and `video`; and both library panes shown at once — the Director's report, restored. In the browser, four of five: the `[hidden]` rules dropped so a hidden pane is painted anyway; the seed box left stretching to its container; the clips pane never painted; and the tab strip rebuilt with `innerHTML` on every render, which the keyboard-focus assertion catches. Restores were byte-for-byte with SHA-256 verified against pre-run digests, anchors converted to each file's own CRLF and the match count asserted before every write, `__pycache__` purged, every run time-boxed. Two mutants survived a first pass and both were real gaps rather than equivalence — the seed-width thresholds were loose enough to pass a two-way split of the same row, and a pinned `grid-row` turned out to be inert — so the assertion was rewritten to measure the box at two panel widths and the inert declaration was deleted.

**The one surviving mutant, and why it is left.** Removing `flex-wrap: wrap` from the tab strip changes nothing at 1600, 1100, 950 or 820 px: seven tabs and a 180 px search box still share a line with no label cut off at all four. It is a defensive declaration against a window narrower than any width this stylesheet describes, and the CSS comment now says exactly that rather than the claim it carried first, which measurement disproved.

**Left for the Director.**
1. **Generate All still does not read the randomize box.** Its stride is server-side, in `generate_batch`, and a session flag in one browser is not something a route should infer. If randomizing a whole batch is wanted, it wants a field on the batch request rather than this toggle growing a second meaning — the tooltip says so on the control today.
2. **The Clips tab's card is still pointed straight at ComfyUI's `/view`**, so every card is blank whenever ComfyUI is down. That predates this change and is recorded here because the tab now makes it more visible, not less.

## 2026-08-21 — Three routes claimed things they could not prove: the restore stage now records the window its take was rendered for, the 0 s exemption states the reason that actually holds, and the all-written report stops telling you to send it back

**Finding 2 — `restore_song_audio`'s numbers stopped describing the take the moment the window was edited, while the docstring and `AudioRestoreResponse.requested_picture_seconds` called them "the same count the submission sent".** The morning's fix had made the restore window the *take's* rather than the exposed slice's, and then read that window off the shot's live `start`/`duration`. A take is fixed at submission; a window is not. Drag a rendered clip's left edge and `start` moves by `delta` while `trim_nudge` compensates — the take goes on beginning where it always did — so `over_render_window` was handed `start + delta` and the master was laid `delta` seconds off the lip-sync it was performed against. `duration` moves with it, so `over_render_frames` could report a count the render never asked for.

**Decided: record the missing number, then stop claiming where it is absent.** The brief offered three answers — refuse a moved window, record what is missing, or just correct the prose — and the second is the only one that makes the claim true rather than retracting it. It is also the codebase's own idiom: AD-13 already snapshots `approved_start`/`approved_duration` at approval so assembly can decide staleness, and `Shot.latest_take_start`/`latest_take_duration` are the take-side twin, written by `generate_h3` in the same statement as `latest_take_lead` and read by nothing else. `latest_take_duration == 0` means "never snapshotted", exactly as `approved_duration == 0` does and for the same reason: `duration` is `gt=0`, so zero is unrepresentable as a real window. Old manifests load unchanged and read as undescribed.

**A moved window is reported, not refused, and that is the deliberate half.** With the snapshot the take's placement is *known*, so refusing would refuse work the route can now do correctly: the take is a fixed file, its recorded window places it exactly, and the restored sibling is the same file this stage would have written the moment the render landed. What has changed is the plan around it, and a window edited away from its take is `assembly.ASSEMBLY_STALE_REFUSAL`'s question at export, not this stage's. So the response says it — `RESTORE_AUDIO_WINDOW_MOVED` names both windows in numbers — and lays the master over the take regardless.

**For a take with no snapshot the route uses the live window and says so.** `describes_take` is a new field on the response and `RESTORE_AUDIO_UNDESCRIBED_TAKE` opens the note: *these numbers are read from the shot's window as it reads now, and they describe the take only if it has not been edited since, which the manifest cannot say*. Refusing was considered and rejected on evidence: nothing distinguishes an unmoved legacy window from a moved one, so a refusal would disable this stage for **every take that exists today** — including the 33-shot batch that was on the GPU while this was written — over a staleness there is no evidence of. And the harm it would avert is a *reported* length, not a silent desync: the offset an undescribed take is placed at is exactly what `RESTORE_AUDIO_NO_LEAD_REFUSAL` already refuses to guess. A loud refusal is right for a fault you can name; this one cannot be named, only suspected.

**Finding 4 — the no-lead refusal's 0 s exemption was defended by an argument that proves nothing.** *"A post-margin submission records a zero lead only at 0 s"* is true and irrelevant: a take at 0 s is exactly as likely to be a legacy one as a take at 12 s, and `min(ideal, extra, start)` could not have recorded anything but zero there, so the value distinguishes nothing. **The exemption survives on a different reason, now written down.** What the refusal protects is the *offset*, and at a window start of 0 s `over_render_window` answers `trim_start = 0` for lead 0 — which is also where a pre-margin take at 0 s begins. Pre-margin or post-margin, that take's first frame is the song's first sample; there is no desync to guess wrong about. What can still differ is the take's **length**, and a length is reported rather than assumed, which is Finding 2's machinery doing the work. The refusal is also now asked of `take_start` rather than `shot.start`, so a rendered shot dragged to 0 s is still refused for the take it actually holds, and the sentence names the second the picture is really at.

**The mismatch sentence was rescoped rather than deleted.** *"They differ because the song ends before the picture does"* is true of the numbers the route computes — `over_render_window`'s only clamp is the song's own end — but "the picture" read as the file, and for an undescribed take the requested count is the plan's rather than the render's. It now says *"the master runs out before the requested picture does"*: a statement about the two numbers being reported, which is all this route ever knew. The final sentence — the frames the file holds are an ffprobe reading, never a number this application claims — is unchanged and is what the whole note leans on.

**Finding 5 — the all-written report is a plan that writes nothing while telling you to use it.** `fill_section_looks` short-circuits ahead of the model call when every section already carries a look, and said *"send overwrite=true to replace what is there"*. Followed literally — `{"confirm_apply": true, "overwrite": true, "plan": <that report>}` — the confirm passed every check, `continue`d on every row because the short-circuit never asked the model and so put no prompt on any row, and answered **200 with `applied: false` and nothing written**: the route answering its own documented sequence with silence. **Both halves are fixed, and the browser flow is untouched.** The message now names the step that works — report again *with* the consent, read what it proposes, confirm that — which is precisely what `app.js` has always done, so no frontend file was changed. And the report is refused as a plan if it comes back anyway: its rows carry `SECTION_LOOK_SKIP_ALL_WRITTEN` instead of the ordinary written-skip reason, which is a real distinction rather than a marker (that row genuinely has no proposal to consent to), and `plan_fingerprint` covers `sections`, so the marker cannot be stripped without the digest failing first. The plan-carrying guarantee is intact: no model is called on a confirm, and applied text is still provably the reported text.

**Tests: nine cases, five of them new functions.** A window edited at both edges after the render restores the take's own seconds — the same `mvp:song` window the undragged shot sent, 107 frames rather than the 141 the new 5.25 s window would ask for — and says the window moved, naming both; an unmoved window is byte-identical to the pre-snapshot manifest in payload and in every reported number, differing only in `describes_take` and the sentence that explains it; a legacy take at 0 s is placed at the song's first sample, reports `describes_take: false`, and — the assertion the old wording failed — names no cause for a mismatch, because there is none; the submission records the window and leaves it alone when the shot is edited afterwards; a hand-picked clip clears the snapshot with the lead; the no-lead refusal asks the take's window, not the shot's; the all-written report is refused as a plan under both `overwrite` values while the documented re-report sequence writes end to end. `comfy.prompts` is empty on every refusal path asserted, including the new one. Both edges of the drag are exercised deliberately: `over_render_frames` answers 107 for every window under four seconds, so a drag that moved only the offset would have left the length claim untested — a mutation proved exactly that and the case was widened until it died.

**Eighteen mutations, every one killed.** `describes_take` forced true and forced false; `take_start` and `take_duration` each collapsed back to the live window; `window_moved` disabled; the no-lead refusal asking `shot.start` again; the payload built from the live start; the reported lengths built from the live window; `describes_take` asserted true on the wire; the undescribed caveat and the moved-window clause each dropped from the note; the mismatch sentence restored to the wording that blamed the file; the submission recording no window; a hand-picked clip keeping the old snapshot; the snapshot removed from `SHOT_TAKE_PROVENANCE_FIELDS` so it would ride into a duplicated shot; the all-written report accepted as a plan again; its rows given the ordinary written-skip reason; and its message put back to "send overwrite=true". Run in a private scratch directory with restores verified by SHA-256 against pre-run digests, anchors converted to CRLF and the match count asserted before every write, `__pycache__` purged, every run time-boxed. A first pass reported four false survivors purely from `-k` selectors that matched no test — "restore" does not match `test_restoring_…` — which is the same failure mode as a mismatched anchor and is why the selectors are now substrings the collector was checked against.

**Nothing was rendered, no model was called, ComfyUI was not touched.** A 33-shot H3 batch was on the GPU throughout and `data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10` was never written to. `restore_song_audio` submits a graph and was never called against a live ComfyUI; the claim here is the submitted graph and the arithmetic, not a take. Suite: **1410 passing** in a shared tree that also carries another agent's store work of the same evening; ruff clean, `node --check` clean on both frontend modules, neither of which was edited.

**Left for the Director.**
1. **`trim_nudge` is still not in the audio offset**, and this change did not decide it — the prior entry left that question open and it stays open. What has changed is that the question is now *narrower*: the two readings of the field disagreed only for a take whose window was edited after it was rendered, and such a take now restores from its recorded window either way. If the nudge belongs in the take's origin, the branch is one line in `restore_song_audio`.
2. **Selecting an *earlier take* of a shot (`select-take` with `output`) does not move the snapshot or the lead**, so both go on describing the newest take while `latest_output` points at an older one. That is pre-existing — the same hole `latest_take_lead` already had — and it is a take-management decision (which take's bookkeeping is *the* bookkeeping) rather than an arithmetic one, so it was not decided here.
3. **`restore_song_audio` submits to ComfyUI before it saves the job record.** With the store's new `ProjectChangedDuringSave` contract, a save that loses a race now answers 409 for a graph that is already on the GPU, and the job record is lost. `generate_h3` and `enhance_with_ltx25` have the same shape. Not touched — it is the store contract's blast radius, not this finding's.

## 2026-08-21 — The replace backoff could revert a save that had already answered 200: the retry now refuses instead, and `save` says so by name

**A regression introduced by the same night's fix.** `21df7f3` gave `store._replace_atomically` a `threading.Condition` backoff that **releases `_MANIFEST_LOCK` across the wait**, because a `time.sleep` under the lock stalled every async request including the `/render-status` poll the lock exists to protect. Both halves of that were right and both survive here. What the release admitted is a lost update: save A serialises its payload, takes the lock, fails `Path.replace` on a foreign handle, and waits with the lock released; save B — a `PUT /shots` from the browser — acquires the lock, writes its complete manifest and **returns 200 with a new `updated_at`**; A re-acquires and replaces the target with the temp file it built *before B ran*, reverting B and restoring the older `updated_at`. B's client then holds a revision the server no longer has, so its next save is refused with `PROJECT_CHANGED_REFUSAL` **while its edits are already gone** — the exact shape of the 2026-08-19 defect where one background save reverted 32 prompts.

**The "last writer wins" analogy the old code defended itself with does not hold.** `_replace_atomically`'s docstring argued the window admits nothing worse than the read/mutate/save that every route performs as three separate acquisitions. The difference is who was told what: in read/mutate/save the loser never received a success reply, so the client knows to re-read. Here the loser was answered 200. A caller told 200 must not have its write silently undone, and `tests/test_store.py` was **pinning that behaviour deliberately** — the test is rewritten, not deleted, and now guards the opposite guarantee under a name that says so.

**This is not fixable inside `save`, and the honest answer is a contract change.** `save` is handed a `Project` a caller already mutated. If another writer landed in between, the *in-memory object* A holds is itself stale, so re-serialising it on each attempt — the obvious fix — overwrites B with exactly the same fields, only now carrying a **newer** `updated_at`: it converts a detectable revert into an undetectable one. Nothing reachable from inside `save` can reconcile A's object with B's manifest. So the retry detects the conflict and **refuses**: `ProjectStore.save` now raises `ProjectChangedDuringSave`, a named, recoverable failure that says what to do — re-read, re-apply, save again. Note the contract had already allowed a raise (an `OSError` when the destination stays locked for the whole bounded retry, pinned by an existing test); what is new is a **specific exception in a case that previously succeeded**, and it succeeds only by reverting somebody.

**Detection is a per-manifest write count, not a re-read.** `_MANIFEST_WRITES` maps `normcase(abspath(target))` to how many times this process has replaced that manifest; it is incremented on every successful replace and read only under `_MANIFEST_LOCK`, which every write in this application holds at the instant of its replace. `_replace_atomically` captures the count before its first attempt and re-checks it **after the wait**: unchanged means nobody wrote and the loop proceeds byte for byte as it did before this guard existed, so the ordinary foreign-handle retry costs nothing and is not refused. Moved means a complete manifest landed and this save's bytes are stale. A stat-based check was rejected — `st_mtime_ns` granularity can miss two writes in a tick, and stat'ing a destination that is contended is the one call likely to fail. The counter's scope is one process, which is exactly the lock's own stated scope; a second process on the same data root is not serialised by either, as the module already documents. The key is normalised rather than the `Path` itself because the module lock is on the module precisely so two `ProjectStore` objects can address one data root, and two spellings of one manifest would be two counters and two writers that cannot see each other.

**The caller audit, since the contract moved.** `batch.reconcile_render_jobs` never saves — it mutates and the caller writes, so nothing there changed. `heal_orphaned_local_jobs_at_startup` already guards its save per project with a broad `except` and its whole pass with another, on the standing rule that boot cannot fail; the refusal falls inside that and the project is simply not counted as healed. Roughly seventy `store.save` calls remain across `app.py` routes, and the refusal means the same thing at every one of them, so it is answered once by an app-wide handler: **409** with `SAVE_RACE_REFUSAL`, a separate sentence from `PROJECT_CHANGED_REFUSAL` (which is pinned byte for byte to a copy in `api.js` by a contract test) because this refusal is about something the client could not have pre-flighted and therefore states "nothing was saved" explicitly. Same status, same remedy, so `api.js` needs no new branch. The store's other failure — a manifest locked for the whole retry — is deliberately **not** handled there: it is not a concurrency problem, a retry will not fix it, and it stays a 500 with its traceback. One route is exempt: `read_render_status`, the two-second poll, absorbs the refusal and logs it, because refusing that tick's save is *correct* (it read the project before the Director's edit landed) and the next tick re-derives the identical answer from ComfyUI two seconds later — a toast every time the Director types would be the worse bug. Noted and left alone: the batch-submit route's per-shot loop catches `HTTPException` only, so a refused seed-bump aborts the whole submission with the 409 rather than skipping one shot; that is the honest answer to a data race, but it is a behaviour worth a Director's opinion.

**Tests: four store cases — one of them the rewritten pin — and four caller cases, all deterministic by ordering.** The lost update itself, following the existing stall test's shape — the interleaving thread starts only once the first `replace` has failed and it ends the backoff by notifying the condition rather than by letting a clock run out, so the five-second backoff is a bound on how long a broken version takes to fail, never a measurement. Cases: B's manifest survives and A raises; the revision B was handed is still the one on disk, asserted as the optimistic-concurrency check sees it; a save to a *different* project during the backoff is not a reason to refuse (the busiest ordinary case — a batch writing job records while the Director edits elsewhere); two `ProjectStore` objects spelling one data root differently still see each other's writes, reached through a `..` segment; and the four caller cases — the poll answers 200 and leaves the manifest alone, `PUT /shots` answers 409 in `SAVE_RACE_REFUSAL`'s words, `create` (which calls `save` under the same lock) answers 409 rather than a traceback, and startup healing survives and counts nothing. Every pre-existing guarantee's test is unchanged and still passing: a reader never makes a writer fail, a reader never sees a half-written manifest, nested `create` → `save` does not deadlock, the backoff does not hold the lock, the nested backoff releases the `RLock` to its full depth, and a permanently blocked save raises and leaves no temp file.

**Thirteen mutations, twelve killed and one proved equivalent.** In `store.py`: the guard removed entirely (tonight's shipped behaviour — three tests fail), the comparison inverted, successful replaces stopping counting, the key taken as the raw path spelling, the refusal leaving its temp file behind, the backoff put back under the lock as a `time.sleep` (kills the stall *and* the nested-create tests), and — the check that the guard was not hollowed — `get` no longer taking the lock, which fails the reader-never-breaks-writer reproduction. Re-reading the count baseline at the top of each attempt instead of once **survived and is an equivalent mutant**: the lock is held from loop top through the attempt and re-acquired after the wait, so the value cannot move in between. In `app.py`: the handler removed, the handler answering 500, the handler answering the other refusal's sentence, the poll no longer absorbing, and the startup healer counting a project it could not write — all killed. The `app.py` mutations were run in a **throwaway `git worktree`** with my own edits re-applied from their anchors, because another agent was editing that file in the shared tree and restoring bytes over a concurrent edit is the very defect this entry is about; the worktree needed `PYTHONPATH` pointed at its own `src`, since the venv's editable `.pth` otherwise imports the live checkout and every mutation silently does nothing (the first run reported five false survivors that way). Restores verified by SHA-256, anchors matched against each file's own detected line ending — `store.py` is LF where `app.py` is CRLF — with the match count asserted before every write, `__pycache__` purged between runs, and every run time-boxed.

**Nothing was rendered, no model was called and the GPU was not touched**; a 33-shot H3 batch was running throughout and `data/projects/project_59f14d19ff10` was never written to. Suite: **1406 passing** at the moment this landed — a shared tree, so that figure also carries another agent's work of the same evening; this change contributes seven test functions to a 1398 baseline — ruff clean, `node --check` clean on both modules — no frontend file was touched.

## 2026-08-21 — The render-again browser assertion had been failing at baseline

`tests/e2e_shot_controls.py:364` asserted the render-again button's tooltip contains
"no GPU time is spent". That was true while render-again only *re-opened* a shot; since
2026-08-18 it queues a take in one gesture (`713d09b`) and `RENDER_AGAIN_HELP` correctly
says so instead. The behaviour changed and its guard did not follow, so the script has
failed at baseline ever since — three separate agents reported it as "not mine" while
working around it.

Now asserts "queue one new take", the current wording's own cost statement, with a comment
recording why the old sentence died so the next reader does not restore it.

**Verified:** the phrase is present in the shipped `RENDER_AGAIN_HELP` and the old one is
absent, checked against `api.js` directly; `ruff` clean. **Not verified:** the script was
not run — it needs a real browser and an isolated data root, and a 33-shot render batch is
using the GPU. This corrects an assertion, and does not claim the surrounding script passes.

## 2026-08-21 — Restore-song-audio laid the exposed slice over the whole take: the audio now covers the take at the take's own offset, through the render's own arithmetic

**Two defects, one root cause, both recorded as follow-ups on 2026-08-19 and both made larger by the micro-cut floor.** Since the over-render margin a take is not its window — it is `over_render_frames(duration)` frames of picture beginning `latest_take_lead` seconds *before* the window, and below ~3.271 s it is centred on it. `POST .../shots/{id}/restore-song-audio` still windowed the master by the bare `shot.start` / `shot.duration`, so it laid the **exposed slice's** seconds over the **whole take**: the sound ran `lead` ahead of the mouth and stopped a margin early. On the Director's shortest live window — 2.083 s at 12 s — it sent 2.083 s starting at 12.000 for a picture that is 4.4583 s starting at 10.7917: wrong length and wrong offset. Beside it, `workflows.audio_replace_lengths` still computed `align_h3_frames(max(5, round(duration · 24)))`, the pre-margin formula, and reported that 2.083 s shot's take as **50 frames** when the render had asked H3 for **107**. Nothing rendered from that number; it is the sentence the Director reads, and it disagreed with what the take is.

**The arithmetic was extracted, not re-derived — that was the whole design constraint.** The invariant is the one `over_render_lead` already states: **take second `t` is song second `start − lead + t`**. It now lives in exactly one function, `timeline.over_render_window(start, lead, picture_seconds, song_duration)`, which answers `(start − lead, min(start − lead + picture_seconds, song))` and nothing else. `generate_h3`'s reference branch was rewritten to call it — the three lines it had inline — and the restore path calls the same function with `over_render_frames(duration) / 24` for the length and the **recorded** `Shot.latest_take_lead` for the offset. The submitted graphs are therefore one computation rather than two that agree, which is the property the stage was built on and had quietly lost. `align_h3_frames` is no longer imported by `workflows.py` at all: both numbers come from `over_render_frames`.

**A shot at 12 s with a 0.25 s lead is now restored from 11.75 s for 4.4583 s, and that preserves the measured precedent rather than moving it.** The +0.9945-at-lag-0.000 s correlation of 2026-08-18 was measured against a take whose frame 0 *was* song second `start`; a post-margin take's frame 0 is song second `start − lead`, and frame `round(lead · 24)` = 6 is song second 12.000 exactly either way. `test_the_restored_window_is_the_one_the_render_conditioned_with` pins it the strongest way available offline: it submits a real `generate_h3` for a window, reads the master's `trim` off the graph ComfyUI was handed, then restores a take carrying the lead that submission recorded and asserts `seek_seconds` and `duration` equal that trim — two routes, two payload builders, one window, compared as submitted graphs rather than as two calls to the same helper. Normal windows are unchanged where it matters: the shot's own past-the-end refusal is still `song_audio_window`'s, in the same words, and the two H3 payload digests did not move (no digest literal was re-pinned in this change).

**The legacy take is refused by name, and that is the one decision here rather than an arithmetic.** `latest_take_lead` defaults to 0 and *"every take rendered before the margin existed correctly reads 0"*. But `over_render_lead` answers `min(ideal, extra, start)` with `ideal ≥ 0.25` and `extra > 0` always, so **a post-margin song-audio submission records a zero lead exactly when the shot starts at 0 s**. A zero lead any later is therefore unambiguously *not* a post-margin take: it is a take rendered before 2026-08-19, or one whose bookkeeping was cleared when an external clip was selected (`select_shot_clip` zeroes the pair deliberately). Neither can be placed against the song from the manifest, so the route refuses with `RESTORE_AUDIO_NO_LEAD_REFUSAL` — the same reasoning as the `use_song_audio: false` row, where guessing a window is called worse than leaving the generated audio in place. A shot at 0.0 s with a zero lead is **not** the legacy case and is not refused. Note what this costs: the old bare-window behaviour was *correct* for a genuine pre-margin take, so this trades a working path for a legacy state against a guess about provenance the manifest cannot support. Re-rendering records the lead.

**`audio_seconds` moved too, and deliberately.** It was the shot's bare `duration` — the same defect on the other side of the report, and after the window fix it would have described something the graph does not send. `audio_replace_lengths` now takes the same four numbers the builder takes and reports the window `over_render_window` actually produces. The pair therefore agrees by construction, and the one way left for it to differ is honest: a take whose window was moved after it was rendered can reach past the end of the song, where the tail clamps and the audio comes up short. The note's mismatch clause was reworded from "the 17k+5 frame grid rounds up" to "the song ends before the picture does", because that is now the only cause. For a shot rendered where it sits the clamp provably cannot fire — a shot ending inside the song always leaves `overflow` of buffer for `over_render_lead` to spend.

**Tests: seven cases, and the mismatch case exists because a mutant survived without it.** The restored window is the take's own seconds for a normal window, a centred micro-cut, a shot at 0.0 s (lead clamped to 0, whole buffer tail) and a shot at the song's end (whole buffer ahead of the cut) — each asserted against `over_render_window` *and* against the invariant `seek_seconds + lead == start`; the cross-path pin above; the legacy refusal plus the 0 s shot that is not it; a lead that cannot describe its shot (negative, longer than `start`, non-finite) refused in the builder; the reported frame count equal to `over_render_frames` across the micro-cut band and driven as a mutation; and the song's-end clamp reporting `lengths_match: false` in the route. `comfy.prompts` is empty on both refusal paths. **Sixteen mutations, every one killed** — the lead's sign, the clamp, the take length collapsed back to the bare window, the recorded lead ignored in the builder, in the reported lengths and in `generate_h3` itself, both new validation branches, the pre-margin frame formula restored, `audio_seconds` back to `duration`, the no-lead refusal removed and over-broadened, and the note naming the window instead of the take. Restores verified by SHA-256 on a CRLF tree with the anchor match count asserted before every write; one anchor caught matching twice and was disambiguated rather than applied. Suite: **1398 passing**, ruff clean, `node --check` clean on both modules.

**Nothing was rendered to verify any of this**, and a 33-shot H3 batch was on the GPU throughout — `restore_song_audio` submits a graph and was never called against a live ComfyUI. The claim is the submitted graph and the arithmetic, not a take: no correlation was measured, and the +0.9945 figure quoted above is the 2026-08-18 measurement of the *old* path against a pre-margin take, cited as the precedent this change had to keep true rather than as evidence for the change.

**Left for the Director, both deliberately undecided.** First, **`trim_nudge` is not in the audio offset.** The window sent is `[start − lead, …)` per the stated invariant, and the nudge only selects which slice is *exposed*. But `app.js` also writes the nudge as drag compensation when a rendered shot's window is moved (`nudge += start − originalStart`), and under that reading the take's origin is `start − lead − nudge` instead. The two readings of the field disagree only for a take whose window was edited after it was rendered; that shot's restored audio would be out by the nudge. Second, **an old take rendered before the margin is refused rather than windowed by the pre-margin rule**, as above — the alternative is a one-line branch if the Director would rather have the legacy path back. Third, `over_render_frames(shot.duration)` describes the take a submission *now* would produce, so a window resized after rendering also changes the reported picture length; the recorded lead is used, but there is no recorded *length* to pair with it.

## 2026-08-21 — Four frontend findings, every one an interaction defect: one Undo ate two gestures, a refused edit still wrote, a drag left a double-click behind, and the overwrite consent could not be reached

**Four findings from the same review over `d6d2e3a..21df7f3`, all in `app.js`, and none of them visible to a test that reads source.** This project has a recorded incident where substring assertions over `app.js` let three UI guarantees invert with a green suite, and the same week a `dblclick` listener was found never to fire at all because `pointerdown` re-renders every clip. So each fix below is proved by driving Microsoft Edge against a server this run started, on an isolated port and an isolated data root, and reading the **stored manifest** or the **text actually painted** rather than the fact that a handler was bound.

**Finding 4 — one Undo stepped back two gestures.** `restores`, the undo snapshot, was cloned from `shotsBaseline` when a save was *queued*; `shotsBaseline` only advances when a save *lands*. Two gestures made inside one round trip — split then drag, two rapid clicks on `#split-shot` — therefore both captured the same baseline `B0` and both pushed `{kind, B0}`. One Undo rolled back **both** while the button said "Undo resizing a shot", a second Undo replayed `B0` and visibly did nothing, and the plan between the two gestures was unreachable.
