**The extraction landed in `models.py` rather than `timeline.py`, and it needed one new import edge.** `app.py` imports `timeline`, which imports `models`, so the shared rule had to sit at or below `models` for `song_audio_tag` — which lives there and is the master song's number — to be derived from it rather than pinned against it. `models` now imports `REFERENCE_TAG_NAMES` from `h3_prompt`, which is a leaf module with no intra-package imports at all: the checker's three tag names and the writer's three tag names are one mapping, and the direction is the only one that cannot become a cycle. **Nothing may ever import `models` from `h3_prompt`**, and the import carries that sentence.

**Then the skip this earned was removed, and only that one.** `app.reference_slot_counts` returned `None` — check nothing — for a shot citing any video or audio, precisely *because* of the divergence: bounding a shot whose specialist had been handed the wrong tag would have refused it for writing the tag it was given. That reason is gone, so the skip is gone, and `<Video 2>` on a one-video shot is now a 422 before the GPU pass in the same words pictures have always had. **The other skip stays**: a dangling citation still returns `None`, because the route's own `Unknown reference asset` refusal must not be replaced by a sentence about the prompt, which sends the Director to fix the wrong thing.

**No pinned digest was re-pinned. Nothing moved.** Both `shot_expansion_input` digests (`EXPANSION_INPUT_REFERENCE_ONLY_DIGEST`, `EXPANSION_INPUT_FIRST_LAST_DIGEST`) and every H3 payload digest pass untouched, on the original literals. That is the whole safety argument for doing this in `timeline.py`: a picture-only shot's expansion input is byte-identical because the shared rule assigns the same series to it, position for position — including a citation whose Asset is missing, which is still numbered rather than dropped, or its neighbours would shift. The pinned expansion project holds *no* assets, so every one of its citations is that case. Submitted payloads did not change at all; this story touches what the specialist is *told*, never what is wired.

**Eight cases added.** A picture-only shot's expansion input asserted against the old single-series rule expressed independently; a video and an audio cited together numbering into their own series with the interleaved picture staying `<Picture 2>`; the master song landing after two cited audios as `<Audio 3>` in both places; keyframe roles taking `<Picture 1>`/`<Picture 2>` beside a cited video; the expansion input's tags asserted equal to the tags read back out of the prompt ComfyUI was actually sent, rather than transcribed, so the two cannot drift behind a stale expectation; `<Video 2>` on a one-video shot refused and `<Video 1>` submitted; and `KEYFRAME_TAG_ROLES` pinned equal to `REFERENCE_MAP_ROLE_TAGS`' keys. The one that is the actual deliverable is `test_every_reference_numbering_surface_reads_the_one_shared_walk`: the bug was a duplicated rule, so the guard is against duplication rather than against this instance of it — the shared walk is displaced under `monkeypatch` (one extra picture slot appended to whatever it returns, in all three modules that bind the name) and the map, the bounds count, the expansion input and the submitted payload must all move with it. A surface that grew its own counter again would keep the old numbers and fail there, whatever those numbers happened to be.

**Mutation pass: 15 defects, 15 killed** — but only after the first pass left one alive, which is the finding worth recording. Making keyframe roles number by the asset's own kind instead of by the role **survived** every test, because a keyframe citing an image is a picture either way and a keyframe citing a video or an audio is refused by the route before it renders. The branch was real but unobservable through any route, so a case was added at the model level: an audio cited as a first frame takes `<Picture 1>` and leaves the master song at `<Audio 1>`, which is the consequence that escapes the numbering. It then died. The other fourteen: kinds collapsed back to pictures (3 fail), one counter shared across the kinds (3), a dangling citation dropped from the walk (3), the song's `+1` made `+2` (3), the song's slot dropped from the totals (2), the totals counting citations instead of reading the numbering (3), the bounds count withheld for every shot (3), the dangling skip removed too (1), the stored map forced to pictures (1), the submitted map forced to pictures (3), the payload wiring everything into picture slots (3), the submitted song tag hardcoded (2), the expansion input forced to pictures (3), the expansion input's song tag hardcoded (2). All three source files restored and SHA-256 compared; **one restore was wrong and was caught by that compare** — a targeted reverse-replacement whose anchor was a substring of a more deeply indented line four lines above it, which silently rewrote the keyframe branch's `"kind": "picture"` to `"kind": numbered.kind`. Behaviourally identical, and still wrong; it was restored by hand and the diff re-read. The lesson beside the other agent's `write_text` line-ending note: a mutation anchor must be unique *as a substring*, not merely as a stripped line.

**What was verified: offline only.** 1073 passing at the time of writing in a shared tree carrying two other agents' concurrent work (a full run mid-story reported two failures — a `test_batch` supersede case and a frontend-contract case — both reproduced on a tree with this story's changes stashed out and both gone by the final run, so neither belonged to this change). `ruff check .` clean, `node --check` clean on `app.js` and `api.js`; no frontend file was touched. **No render was submitted, no ComfyUI call was made and no live model has seen the corrected tags** — every assertion here is about what the builders emit, not about what a specialist writes when handed `<Video 1>` or what H3 does with the result. The claim is that the prompt and the payload now name the same slots, which is a bytes-level fact; whether a video-citing shot renders *well* is exactly as undemonstrated as it was this morning, because no such shot has ever been rendered.

**Found and not fixed.** `models.reference_slot_totals` and `app.reference_slot_counts` differ only in the dangling guard, and the second exists to keep the render's own refusal from being pre-empted — worth collapsing if the route ever validates citations before it counts them, but not while the two answers mean different things. And `mode_specification_problems` refuses a `middle`-role citation on a `references` shot, so the `middle` role never reaches this numbering; it is classified by the asset's kind like any non-keyframe citation, which will want a decision when the first/middle/last adapter exists.

## 2026-08-20 — The master preset stops re-mastering the song

**The Director's ruling, made today and implemented as given:** drop the loudness pass from the `master` export preset. The entry below ("Draft and master are the same cut, built twice") shipped `loudnorm=I=-16:TP=-1.5:LRA=11` on delivery and flagged the target as "a starting point, not a ruling". The ruling is that there should be no target at all. Our export's audio track **is** the Director's master song — supplied already mastered, or produced by MiniMax Music 3 — and re-normalizing it re-masters someone's finished work. -16 LUFS is the broadcast/podcast convention; a modern music master sits well above it, so the pass was a haircut on every delivery.

**Measured on this machine, not argued.** A synthetic 220 Hz tone at the level `tone_wav_bytes` produces measures **-37.95 LUFS integrated**. Through the old master preset the same tone came out of the export at **-15.96 LUFS** — a **+22.0 LU** lift, the pass dragging the song to its target. Through the new master preset the export measures **-37.95 LUFS**: **0.00 LU of movement**, with the AAC re-encode both presets have always performed accounted for. The draft of the same plan measures -37.95 LUFS too. On a source at modern-master level (pink noise limited to -8.50 LUFS) the old preset delivered -16.20 LUFS — **7.7 LU pulled down**, which is the audible half of the ruling. `test_the_master_preset_exports_a_verified_file_and_preserves_the_songs_loudness` asserts the export within **0.5 LU** of the song file that went in, on both presets, and carries the numbers into its failure message.

**The old master-preset tests asserted the lift; they now assert its absence. That is a spec change, not a broken contract** — the assertion is inverted deliberately and both tests say so in their docstrings. The route test's cruder cross-check (`volumedetect` mean, immune to `loudnorm`'s own analysis being the thing measuring it) says the master is not louder than the draft.

**The 48 kHz conform stays, on new reasoning, and the reasoning was measured before it was written** (`assembly.py:372`). Its original justification is genuinely gone: `loudnorm` ran internally at 192 kHz and AAC caps at 96 kHz, so a 44.1 kHz song used to deliver at 96 kHz — reproduced on this machine, and with the pass removed a 44.1 kHz song now delivers at 44.1 kHz with no filter at all. What is *not* gone is the source this application actually takes. A Director-supplied master can be any rate, and a 192 kHz one still lands at 96 kHz through AAC's ceiling — also reproduced. The conform therefore earns its place as a delivery decision rather than as a leftover: it makes the rate deterministic at the one rate video delivery expects, and it is **level-transparent**, which is the property the ruling cares about — a clean tone measured -27.15 LUFS before the resample and -27.15 LUFS after. Sample-rate conversion is not mastering. The test measures it *as a rate*: the master export is 48000 Hz, and the filterless draft carries the song's own 44100 Hz through, which is what says the conform is chosen rather than inherited.

**`draft` is byte-identical, and the plumbing is now pinned for any filterless preset.** `TODAYS_TRIM_ARGV` and `TODAYS_CONCAT_ARGV` pass untouched, three ways as before. A new test builds a preset with no audio filters at all and asserts both audio paths stay well-formed: no `-af` flag with an empty argument (ffmpeg reads an empty filter description and refuses), and no dangling `,` before `[mix]` on the mix graph. Those are the two shapes a naive "always append the chain" would build, and neither was reachable through a test until a filterless preset was asserted against both branches.

**The existing verification is unchanged and still passes for `master`**: the export ffprobes at 8.000000 s against an 8.000 s song, within one frame, with exactly one video and one audio stream.

**What was verified: offline, plus real local ffmpeg.** 1070 passing at the time of writing in a shared tree carrying several agents' concurrent work; the two failures present (`test_api.py`'s reference-slot-count and expansion-tag cases) and the single `ruff` F841 both sit in another agent's in-flight mutation pass on `app.py` (`if False: return None` at `app.py:605`) and are untouched by anything here. Both `node --check` gates clean — no frontend file was touched. Live ffmpeg ran on synthetic `testsrc`/`sine`/`anoisesrc` sources and on the suite's synthetic clips; no GPU work, no ComfyUI, `comfy.prompts` asserted empty on every path. **Not verified: no real song has been through the new master preset, and nothing was judged by ear** — the claim made here is that the export's integrated loudness equals the song's, which is a measurement, not a judgement. That is the whole point: there is now nothing to judge, because nothing is being decided about level.

**Mutation pass: 4 defects, 4 killed**, `assembly.py` and both test files restored byte-identically (SHA-256 compared, `d94d6a6…` for `assembly.py` before and after). Loudness re-added to `master` (4 tests fail); the mix graph's separator made unconditional, leaving `,[mix]` for a filterless preset (4 fail); `-af` emitted unconditionally, giving `-af ""` on the draft (12 fail); and the conform applied to `draft` too, which the longhand argv pins catch (6 fail). One process note worth recording: the first restore used `Path.write_text`, which rewrote a LF file as CRLF and changed its hash without changing a character — mutation restores in this tree must go through `read_bytes`/`write_bytes`.

**Not fixed, deliberately.** `AssemblyRequest`'s docstring in `app.py` said `master` "is the only thing that normalizes loudness"; that sentence is now false, so it was corrected in place (`app.py:2401`) — the only `app.py` edit here, and no behaviour moved with it. Nothing else about presets changed: the encoder settings, `+faststart`, the trim rule, the cumulative grid, overlap-as-layers and the refusal reports are all untouched. And no preset offers a loudness target as an option — a Director who wants one has an ffmpeg command and a finished file, and putting the choice on the wire would re-open a question that has been answered.

## 2026-08-20 — Four surfaces, one state: the re-render says so, and a copy claims no take

The two defects the supersession entry below reported and deliberately left unbuilt, now built — plus a third found while enumerating fields for the second. **`latest_output` and `approved_output` semantics are unchanged: nothing here blanks, clears or conditionally hides a pointer.** The fix throughout is to state the state, never to change it.

**The inspector was honest and three surfaces were not, and two of them made affirmative wrong claims.** `shotRenderInFlight` (`api.js`) is now the one reading of "a render is in flight" — the same predicate `approvalControl` decides `APPROVE_IN_FLIGHT` from, so a surface cannot be honest while another is not — and `shotRenderState` turns it into the word and the sentence each surface draws. The **Monitor**: `monitorState` returns a `previous-take` kind carrying the sentence, and the take plays exactly as a settled one does — same file, same slice, same mix — because it is the only evidence the Director has. `monitorShowsTake` keeps both kinds on screen, so nothing had to learn that a displaced take is still a take. The take *has* to keep playing: judging whether a re-render was worth it means watching the thing it is replacing. The **takes strip** is now decided by `takesStripRows` rather than by ternaries inside the template literal, `shotPromptCell`'s rule — the displaced row reads `Previous` instead of `Current`, and the take that is coming gets a row of its own (`Take 3 · not landed yet`, `Rendering`) instead of being left to inference. The **timeline clip** carries the word `RENDERING` in a `.clip-state` span and the whole sentence in `title` *and* `aria-label`, which is the only one of a clip's signals a screen reader announces; the left-border hue stays as the second, redundant signal, as `.no-prompt` already does.

**The Monitor needed a text layer, because it structurally had none.** `.showing-take` `display: none`s `.monitor-overlay`, so while a picture was up the Monitor could not say anything at all. `#monitor-note` is a band across the top of the frame, written from `monitorState`'s label and hidden by `:empty` when there is nothing to say — so "nothing to say" is the absence of a band rather than an empty one.

**One sentence, four surfaces.** `TAKE_DISPLACED_BY_RENDER` is `app.py`'s `APPROVE_IN_FLIGHT_REFUSAL` opening sentence, and `APPROVE_IN_FLIGHT` is now built from it, byte-identical to what it was. Four wordings for one state is how a Director ends up believing there are four states.

**Duplicate gave the copy the original's take, and so did Split.** Both `structuredClone`d the Shot and reset `status` alone, so a Shot that had rendered nothing played the original's take in the Monitor, offered it in the takes strip, and read as approved. It also satisfied `shot_render_provenance`, so the automated writers refused to touch it, citing a render nobody ran. `newShotFromPlan` **builds from the plan** instead of subtracting from a clone, which changes the failure direction: a field nobody classified is absent from the copy rather than inherited by it.

**The classification lives in one place and is partitioned against the model itself** (`models.py`): `SHOT_PLAN_CONTENT_FIELDS` (inherited) — `start`, `duration`, `prompt`, `h3_prompt`, `mode`, `asset_ids`, `citations`, `reference_labels`, `singing`, `use_song_audio`, `seed`; `SHOT_TAKE_PROVENANCE_FIELDS` (never) — `status`, `prompt_id`, `latest_output`, `latest_review`, `approved_output`, `approved_start`, `approved_duration`, `latest_take_lead`, `trim_nudge`, `mix_take_audio`, `flagged`; and `SHOT_UNINHERITED_DECISION_FIELDS` — `locked`. The three plus `id` must equal `Shot.model_fields` exactly, asserted by a contract test, so **a field added to `Shot` and classified by nobody fails the suite** instead of riding into every copy from then on. `api.js` mirrors the plan list and the same test holds the two identical.

**The two judgement calls, stated.** `trim_nudge` and `latest_take_lead` select a slice of one specific file; on a Shot with no file the pair would cut a future take at an offset nobody chose. `mix_take_audio` accepts *this take's* audio into the mix. **`flagged` does not copy**: it is AD-5's mark on "a shot whose take fell short", and inheriting it enlarges the flagged batch's scope — GPU minutes on a shot nobody flagged. **`locked` does not copy, and is classified as neither plan nor provenance** because `shot_write_refusal` already keeps those apart ("a lock is a decision the Director made and provenance is a fact about media"). A copy exists to be worked on; one that arrived locked would be refused by the sweeps, fills and re-renders reached for next, giving a reason the Director never set on it. Unlocked is the cheap, visible state — an inherited lock is invisible protection nobody asked for.

**The server does not share the defect.** There is no duplicate or split route: `app.py`'s only `Shot.model_copy` calls build transient payloads (`h3_prompt` stripped for one prompt build, a windowed duration for one picture pass) and persist nothing, and `timeline.py` creates no Shot from another. So the client/server agreement pinned is the classification itself rather than a second implementation of the copy.

**What was verified: offline only. No browser was driven.** Five new tests in `tests/test_frontend_contract.py`, and they *execute* rather than grep — this repo's own recorded lesson is that substring assertions over `app.js` let three UI guarantees invert with a green suite. `monitorState`'s settled view is pinned as an exact JSON string, key for key, so "say more about the in-flight case" cannot become "say something different about every case"; `syncMonitor` is then driven through the real `#master-audio` `timeupdate` binding and the note, the `showing-take` class and the overlay are read off the stub DOM. The takes strip is read as the markup `renderShotInspector` writes (`>Current<` absent, `>Previous<` and `>Rendering<` present). The clip is read out of `#shots-track` after firing `#zoom-in`, which is the shortest path to a real `renderTimeline` from outside it. Duplicate and Split are fired as clicks and the copies compared **field by field** against `Shot.model_fields`' own defaults, with a fixture guard asserting the source differs from every default so "the copy holds the default" cannot pass on a copy that inherited everything. `uv run ruff check .` clean; both `node --check` gates clean.

**Suite: 1063 passing, 1 failing — and the failure is not from this work.** `tests/test_api.py::test_a_shot_citing_a_video_skips_the_bounds_check_rather_than_guessing` exercises `app.py`'s new `reference_slot_counts`, a concurrent in-flight change by another agent in the same tree; `tests/test_frontend_contract.py` is green on its own, before and after every mutation below. Baseline before this work was 1059; the five added make 1064 collected.

**Mutation pass: 11 defects reintroduced one at a time, 11 killed**, every file restored from its original bytes and verified by SHA-256, `__pycache__` purged before every run. `shotRenderInFlight` inverted; `shotRenderState` claiming a displacement on a first render; `monitorState`'s in-flight branch dropped; `shotPromptCell` dropping the note from the accessible name; the takes strip calling a displaced take `Current` again; the pending row dropped; `newShotFromPlan` spreading the clone back in; `syncMonitor` narrowed to `kind === "take"` (which blacks out the displaced take); the Monitor's note never written; the clip's `RENDERING` word dropped; and one `Shot` field left out of the classification. No survivors.

**Not verified.** Nothing was driven in a browser: the note band's placement over the picture, the `:empty` hide, the clip's `.clip-state` line inside an 82 px clip at small zoom, and the amber on the strip rows are all read off `styles.css` rather than seen. **No render was submitted, nothing was queued, and ComfyUI was not touched.** Whether `Previous` reads better than some other word to a Director mid-batch is a judgement, not a measurement.

**Not fixed, deliberately.** The generic `PUT` routes still write `Shot.status` with no in-flight check — the sibling-write-path hole recorded four times now, and still a separate ruling. `Add shot` builds its literal by hand rather than through the new classification; it makes a Shot from nothing rather than from another Shot, so it has no provenance to inherit, and rewriting it would be a change with no defect behind it. And the Monitor's `no-take` label still says only "This shot has no rendered take yet" for a shot whose *first* render is in flight — the clip and the strip both say `RENDERING` for that shot, and the Monitor has no take to caption.

## 2026-08-20 — Boot is when the crash happened, so boot is when it is admitted

From the Calliope teardown (`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/calliope-teardown-and-improvement-plan-2026-08-20.md`, phase 1.3): two pieces of job hygiene that project ships and we partly lacked — stale `running` jobs reset at startup, and a leftover job explicitly superseded when a new render is queued for the same target. Both landed narrower than the source, because our reconciliation is subtler than theirs and the difference is exactly where the value is.

**Startup healing is the clear win, and it is the *same rule*, not a second opinion.** `heal_orphaned_local_jobs` (`app.py:2323`) is the predicate the assemble route has always applied, lifted out of that route's body unchanged and given a `live_job_ids` parameter: the assemble route passes `app.state.live_assemblies`, and boot passes `()`. The empty tuple is the argument rather than a shortcut — a process that has just started is running no assemblies, so the registry *is* empty, and passing it makes the two moments one question. `test_startup_healing_and_the_assemble_paths_healing_agree_on_the_same_input` puts one crash manifest through both paths in two data roots and compares the resulting records field for field, so a divergence at either end is a test failure rather than a slow drift.

**A local job cannot survive a restart; a ComfyUI job very well may, and this is the whole boundary.** `prompt_id` empty is the local-work marker `reconcilable_jobs` and the frontend poll already key on: nothing on ComfyUI knows about such a job, so the in-process registry was the only thing that could ever settle it. A job carrying a prompt id may be executing on the Director's GPU *right now* — ComfyUI is user-managed and outlives this process — and healing one to `error` on the strength of our own restart would throw away a render being paid for in GPU minutes. Those stay the reconciler's, which asks (`/queue`, then `/history`, then the three-tick settle) rather than assuming. The `kind == "post"` clause came across verbatim too, and its survival is now pinned by a test with the reason written down: the only sentence this rule knows how to write is *the assembly's*, and stamping "This assembly was interrupted" onto a shot render would be a false record. Widening it is a decision with its own wording, not a side effect of moving the rule.

**Boot cannot be allowed to fail, and that is tested rather than asserted.** `ProjectStore.list` already skips a manifest it cannot read or parse, so a corrupt project is invisible to the pass; on top of that the listing and each save are guarded separately, so a data root that cannot be listed and a single project that cannot be written both degrade to a logged warning. Those two guards are unreachable through a real `ProjectStore`, so they are driven by store subclasses that raise — which is the shape a permission error actually has. A project with nothing to heal is **not written at all**: every save is a whole-manifest write with an fsync behind it and it bumps `updated_at`, which `PUT /projects/{id}` compares for optimistic concurrency, so a boot that touched every project would invalidate every client's snapshot for nothing.

**Supersede on resubmit turned out to be reachable on exactly one route, and the investigation is most of what shipped.** `generate_h3` is the only submission route whose per-target guard is a *Shot status* (`shot.status != "ready"`) rather than a read of the job records. `PUT /api/projects/{id}` and `PUT /api/projects/{id}/shots` both replace `Shot.status` wholesale with no in-flight check, so a client holding a snapshot taken before the submission re-arms the shot under a live job and the route accepts — the same generic-PUT hole recorded three times before, and one this suite already walks through (`rearm_shot` in `tests/test_api.py` exists so the profile tests can submit one shot repeatedly). Every other route's guard reads the job records themselves, and every non-terminal status is an in-flight status, so `shot_render_in_flight` and its two siblings cannot be walked past; a test drives all six refusals on a shot with a live render and asserts none of them spent a submission or settled the job.

**The harm is specific, and it is the pointer.** `apply_job_history` adopts an h3 completion by `target_id`, so a late answer from the stale record moves `latest_output` back onto the older take and drops the newer take's `latest_review` with it — silently. `batch.supersede_target_jobs` (`batch.py:584`) settles the leftover as `cancelled` with a reason and a new defaulted `RenderJob.superseded_by` naming its successor. `cancelled` rather than a status of its own, deliberately: it is already terminal on both sides of the transport, so the poll releases and the queue panel renders it with **no client change at all**, and `superseded_by` is what keeps it distinguishable from a hand cancellation. Nothing here touches ComfyUI — no cancel, no interrupt — so an older prompt already executing goes on executing and its file lands beside the new one under the same prefix.

**The music routes are deliberately left alone, and that reversal is the sharpest thing found.** They are the *easiest* two-live-records state to reach — every music job carries the constant `target_id="song"` and neither route has a per-target in-flight refusal — but the older record cannot do the harm supersession exists to prevent, because `apply_job_history` gates song adoption on `Song.prompt_id`, which the new submission has just replaced. What it *can* still do is record where its audio landed, and `test_a_completing_music_job_matches_the_song_by_prompt_id_not_by_source` has pinned that since the day it was written: the orphaned take is recoverable from the old job's `output_files`. Superseding it stops reconciliation and leaves that list empty forever — a real loss traded for cleanup the three-tick settle already performs. The first cut of this change did supersede music jobs; that test caught it, and the route now carries a comment saying why it does not.

**The same cost is accepted on the h3 path, and is asserted rather than glossed.** A superseded record is never reconciled again, so if its prompt was still executing its `output_files` stays empty: the file is on disk under the shot's prefix and the record still names the prompt that wrote it, but the takes strip — which reads `output_files` — will not list it. `test_supersession_stops_a_late_answer_overwriting_the_newer_take` asserts both halves: the newer take keeps the pointer when the older prompt answers last, *and* the older record's `output_files` is empty. A wrong pointer is silent; a missing strip row is visible, and the file is not lost.

**Take-pointer semantics were investigated and deliberately not changed.** Calliope also nulls the stale output pointer so the UI reads "generating"; for us that means `latest_output`, which drives the Monitor, the takes strip and finishing, and which has a recorded provenance incident behind it. **The finding is that the UI is not uniformly honest here, and that the fix belongs in the frontend rather than in the pointer.** The inspector *is* honest — a `shot-status` chip reading `queued`/`running`, and the disabled Approve button's `APPROVE_IN_FLIGHT` sentence ("the take on screen is about to be displaced") rendered as visible text. Two surfaces are not. The **Monitor** decides entirely from `latest_output` and never reads `shot.status` (`api.js` `monitorState`), and `.showing-take` then `display: none`s the only text layer it has — so a Director scrubbing the timeline sees the previous take play, in sync, framed identically to a settled one. The **takes strip** labels the displaced take `Current` while a newer render is in flight and shows no pending row at all, which is an affirmative wrong claim rather than a mere absence. The timeline clip carries render state as a border hue only, with no word and nothing in its `aria-label`, against that stylesheet's own stated "colour is never the only signal" rule. **Recommendation: add an in-flight indication to the Monitor and a pending row to the takes strip — leave `latest_output` exactly as it is.** Blanking the pointer would make every one of those surfaces lose the take entirely, including the one a Director watches to decide whether the re-render is even needed, and it would put an editorial decision inside a cleanup. Nothing in the frontend clears `latest_output` today (the only assignment is guarded against empty), and nothing here changed that.

**What was verified: offline only.** 1059 passing (`uv run pytest`), `uv run ruff check .` clean, both `node --check` gates clean. Twelve new tests across `tests/test_batch.py`, `tests/test_api.py` and `tests/test_assembly_route.py`, covering: the pure supersede rule and its four bystander axes; the chain when a third render arrives; persistence read back through a **fresh `ProjectStore`** on every route claim; startup with no projects, with a corrupt manifest beside a healthy crash, with a jobless project, with a store that raises on `list` and one that raises on `save`; and `comfy.prompts` asserted empty on every new path, with `comfy.cancelled` empty too — nothing here submits or cancels anything.

**Not verified: no crash was reproduced.** The orphaned state is constructed by writing the manifest a crash would have left and then building the app, which is what boot actually reads, but the application was never killed mid-export and restarted. Nothing was driven in a browser: the `latest_output` finding above is read off `app.js`/`api.js`/`styles.css` source, not observed on screen. **No render was submitted and no GPU time was spent.** The reachable-only-through-a-stale-client framing for supersession is an argument from the two PUT routes' code, not an observed incident — no Director has been recorded hitting it.

**Mutation pass: 23 defects reintroduced one at a time, 23 killed**, every file restored from its original bytes (never by inverse replacement) and verified by SHA-256, with `__pycache__` purged before every run. Among them: the heal ignoring the local-work marker, the live registry, the terminal-status check and the `post` kind; the heal writing no reason and settling as `cancelled`; the startup pass not running, saving every project it read, treating every job as live, and each of its two boot guards removed; the assemble path's own heal removed; the `generate_h3` call site removed and pointed at the wrong kind; each of `supersede_target_jobs`' four clauses; the record left non-terminal, unreasoned, unnamed, and with its strike counter standing; and the model field defaulting to something. One survivor was fixed rather than reported — the `kind == "post"` narrowing had no test that could tell it from a widened rule, and now does.

**Not fixed, deliberately.** The two dishonest surfaces above are reported, not built — they are frontend work with their own wording decisions. `superseded_by` is provenance and nothing reads it for a decision; there is no UI for it, so a superseded job appears in the queue panel as an ordinary `cancelled` row. And the generic `PUT` routes still write `Shot.status` from a body with no in-flight check, which is the hole that makes supersession reachable in the first place — closing it is the fourth appearance of the sibling-write-path pattern and a separate ruling, recorded here rather than taken.

## 2026-08-20 — Populate counts its own shots, and asks twice at most

From the Calliope teardown (`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/calliope-teardown-and-improvement-plan-2026-08-20.md`, phase 1.2) against the roadmap's own run-2 measurement: this machine's local model under-delivers on list length and drops fields when the ask is large — three rolls of a 32-shot layout returned zero `sections` each, and `performance` landed on one roll of three. The four-part pattern ported from that project is: compute the count in code, state it as a hard constraint, verify the reply, then one guided retry with the failure named.

**The count is arithmetic, so the server does it.** `populate_required_shots` (`app.py:2004`) is `round(song / POPULATE_TARGET_WINDOW_SECONDS)` — the 5.2 s target that already existed as the render-speed decision, now divided into a number instead of paraphrased as "about N". The same number is stated three times in the instruction the model actually receives: the opening `Return EXACTLY {count} shots`, a numbered `HARD CONSTRAINTS` block, and a `FINAL CHECK` line appended **after** the section map so it is genuinely the last thing read (`app.py:2094`). The pattern's canonical third site is the system prompt; `SYSTEM_PROMPT` is shared with the chat route, so populate's own opening sentence took that slot rather than teaching every chat turn about shot counts.

**The reply is counted, not believed** (`app.py:6410`). A short one buys exactly one retry, at 0.2 instead of `PLAN_TEMPERATURE`'s 0.7 — the fault has been named in numbers, so what is wanted is obedience, not another creative roll. `DirectorClient.plan` grew a `temperature` parameter for it; every other caller takes the default and sends byte-identically what it always sent. **One retry, not expansion's four**, and the ceiling differs for a stated reason: `attempt_expansion` retries one *shot* against a format checker inside a sweep that runs unattended, while populate is one click with the Director sitting in front of it and a recorded 300 s timeout per call. Four attempts is twenty minutes of a button that looks stuck.

**A second short reply fails loudly and writes nothing.** `POPULATE_SHORT_PLAN_REFUSAL` names both counts, says plainly that the timeline was not replaced, and gives two things to do (mark sections so each is a smaller ask; point `MVP_LLM_MODEL` at a larger model). It is raised before the destructive replace, so the Director's plan is untouched on disk — asserted by re-reading through a fresh `ProjectStore`.

**Dropped fields are named the way a short count is, but do not buy their own retry.** When a retry is being spent anyway and the model was asked for `sections` and returned none, the corrective feedback says so. It does not *trigger* a retry: this model family drops `sections` on most rolls, and a check that fires on most rolls is a check that doubles every populate's cost — the exact failure mode the "guard against a retry that always fires" test exists for. `performance` is deliberately not checked at all: an all-false answer is a legitimate reading of an instrumental and cannot be distinguished from a dropout, which is precisely why every other mechanical field on this path is set server-side.

**Two-stage populate shipped behind `two_stage`, defaulting off** (`app.py:2158`). Stage one asks for structure and nothing else, from the lyric sheet already in the project context (not pasted a second time — "JSON in context begets JSON" has bitten this codebase before). Its repaired boxes are then *handed* to the shots call, which drops `POPULATE_SECTIONS_ASK` and its hard constraint entirely, so the second ask actually gets smaller rather than being sent twice. Stage one gets one call and no retry — re-asking a small question with a second 300 s call argues with the premise that small asks work — and an empty structure pass simply falls through to the single-call wording. The default is `false` because **the split answers a measurement with an argument**, and turning it on would spend a second local-model call on every Director's populate on the strength of that argument. `false` is byte-for-byte the old behaviour, so this is reversible in either direction by one word.

**What did not move, on purpose.** `populate_windows` is untouched and now pinned value-for-value in `tests/test_timeline.py` — the temptation that comes with count enforcement is to let the enforced count leak down into the geometry, and the pin exists to make that a failing test rather than a silent drift. The 4–15 s band, the 6 s enforced ceiling, `confirm_replace`, the protected-shot refusal by name, the in-flight-render 409 and the post-await re-read all behave exactly as before. Shots still land as drafts; `comfy.prompts` is asserted empty on every new path, and nothing here arms, queues or approves anything.

**Two existing test fixtures had to grow, and that is worth stating plainly.** `test_populate_timeline_lays_out_the_whole_song_from_the_models_shape` proposed 3 shots for a 60 s song and `test_populate_adopts_the_models_sections_when_none_are_marked` proposed 2 — both are *precisely the under-delivery this change refuses*, so both now propose the enforced twelve. Every assertion in them survived unchanged (contiguity, the band, the draft status, the proportional prompt mapping, the section-boundary tiling, the empty `comfy.prompts`); only the double's proposal list and the `proposed` count moved. One prompt change also rides along: a project whose sections the Director already marked no longer gets the "first divide the song into sections" sentence, because it is now dropped whenever the structure is already known.

**What was verified: offline only.** Suite green on the six new cases and every existing populate case; `ruff check .` clean; both `node --check` gates clean (no frontend file was touched — `two_stage` is a defaulted request field, so `api.js` sends what it always sent). **No live model has run against any of this.** The new prompt wording — the three count placements, the `FINAL CHECK` line, the retry's "PREVIOUS ATTEMPT FAILED" framing, the sections-first instruction — has been verified only as *text handed to a recording double*. Whether a real local model obeys a stated count better than it obeyed "about N", whether the lower retry temperature helps, and whether the two-stage split actually recovers `sections` are all unmeasured. The counts and refusals are code and are tested; the persuasion is not.

**Mutation pass: 11 defects, 11 killed**, `app.py` and `timeline.py` restored byte-identically (SHA-256 compared per mutation). The count math flattened to 1; the verification made unconditional so no retry ever fires; the retry's temperature reverted to the first attempt's; the attempt ceiling raised from two to three; the fail-loudly branch made to accept a second short plan; the dropped-`sections` sentence removed from the corrective feedback; the stage-one gate widened to run unconditionally; the sections ask kept in the shots call so the second ask never shrinks; stage one's boxes dropped at the post-await re-read; the `FINAL CHECK` moved ahead of the section map; and the tiling rounded to centiseconds against the new pin.

**Not fixed, deliberately.** `two_stage` has no UI — it is a request field with no control in `app.js`, and wiring a toggle for a path that has never met a live model would be advertising an unmeasured choice. The retry does not replay the failed answer as an assistant turn the way `expand_shot` does; `plan` is single-turn and the shortfall is a number rather than a text to correct, so the fault is named in the request instead. And the count is enforced as `>=`, not `==`: over-delivery is harmless because `populate_windows` clamps it to the feasible band, and spending 300 s to punish it would buy nothing.

## 2026-08-20 — Draft and master are the same cut, built twice

From the Calliope teardown (`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/calliope-teardown-and-improvement-plan-2026-08-20.md`, phase 4.2) against the open roadmap line "Draft/master export presets". Three details were worth taking from that project's ffmpeg chain: a `loudnorm` pass on delivery, `libx264` + CRF + `-movflags +faststart`, and progress read from `-progress pipe:1` with a **concurrent stderr drain**. Two of the three were already here — `trim_args` has encoded libx264 at CRF 18 since FR-22 and `concat_args` has always written `+faststart` — so what actually shipped is the loudness pass, the progress channel, and a name for the settings that already existed.

**`draft` is not a new configuration. It is the export this application has always produced**, and it is the default for exactly that reason (`assembly.py:370`). The alternative — making `draft` genuinely cheaper than today and defaulting to it — would have changed what an existing Assemble click writes, without anyone asking. `TODAYS_TRIM_ARGV` and `TODAYS_CONCAT_ARGV` in `tests/test_assembly.py` are the argv written out longhand rather than derived from the preset object, and the default's command is asserted against them three ways: called with no preset, called with the default *name*, and called with the preset object. veryfast is already the second-fastest x264 preset, so "draft is at least as fast as today's export" holds by identity rather than by promise.

**`master` moves two things and nothing else.** The picture, at the trim stage — x264 `slow` at CRF 16 — because the join is `-c:v copy` and a CRF there would encode nothing (`assembly.py:388`). And the audio, at the join: `loudnorm=I=-16:TP=-1.5:LRA=11`, then `aresample=48000`. The resample is not decoration: `loudnorm` runs internally at 192 kHz and hands that on, AAC caps at 96 kHz, and without the conform a delivery file ships at 96 kHz. With accepted take audio there is no `-af` to hang the pass on, so it rides the tail of the mix graph *after* `amix=…:normalize=0` — the finished programme, not one contributor to it, and the song still is not ducked.

**The loudness target is a starting point and is flagged as one in the source** (`assembly.py:378`). `-16 LUFS / -1.5 dBTP / LRA 11` is the streaming-delivery convention Calliope ships and is deliberately conservative; **this is a music video**, and music masters commonly sit considerably louder. It is one constant, read by nothing else, and no verification depends on its value.

**`loudnorm` does not move the duration, and the verification was not touched to make it fit.** Measured, not assumed: on synthetic clips over an 8.000 s song the master export ffprobes at 8.000000 s, one video stream and one audio stream — the same assertions FR-22 has always made, re-run by hand on the master's own file in `test_the_master_preset_exports_a_verified_file_and_lifts_its_loudness`. The single-pass form's lookahead flushes at EOF, and `-shortest` was already bounding the container to the verified video length.

**Progress exists because nothing else could report it.** The route is synchronous by design and the AD-1 poll deliberately ignores a `post` job with an empty `prompt_id` (there is nothing on ComfyUI to reconcile), so a multi-minute export had no observable state at all. Both stages now run under `-progress pipe:1`, and `ExportProgress` folds two clocks into one number: the trims cover the timeline once and the join covers it again, so a raw reading walks backwards several times per export. It is monotonic by construction, weighted 90/10 (the trims re-encode every frame; the join stream-copies), and bounded by capping the *stage fraction* rather than by a second clamp at the write — the two weights sum to one, so a fraction that cannot exceed 1 is a percent that cannot exceed 100, which `RenderJob.progress`'s `le=100` would otherwise refuse. `parse_progress_us` ignores every line it cannot use — other keys, the `N/A` that key carries before the first frame, a fragment handed over mid-word — because a progress reader that can kill an export is worse than an export with no progress reader. Writes are throttled to whole-percent movement: every save is a whole-manifest write with an fsync behind it, and that caps the entire bar at 101 of them however long the export runs.

**The concurrent stderr drain is the guard the whole progress branch needed.** Reading stdout to exhaustion before touching stderr is the classic subprocess deadlock, and ffmpeg writes diagnostics throughout — a 4 s trim at debug verbosity produces ~60 KB, the order of a pipe buffer. The no-progress branch keeps `communicate()`, which does this drain itself and is therefore byte-for-byte the function it was. **The deadlock itself was not reproduced**; it is asserted structurally (the drain task must be created before the stdout loop) and, from the other side, by a forced trim failure proving ffmpeg's own sentence still reaches the 502 and the job's `error` — a branch that collected nothing would look healthy until something failed.

**What was verified: offline, plus real local ffmpeg.** 1014 passing (`uv run pytest --ignore=tests/test_director.py`), `ruff` clean, both `node --check` gates clean. Live ffmpeg ran on synthetic `color`/`sine` sources, never on the Director's media, and no GPU work of any kind: a master export measured for duration, stream shape, sample rate and loudness against a draft of the same plan; the progress values read back off the manifest as the export ran (`[0, 44, 89, 99, 100]` for a two-shot 8 s plan). `comfy.prompts` is asserted empty on every new path. **Not verified: nothing was driven in a browser.** The preset select, the running percentage and the two-second progress tick have offline coverage only — the select's options are held against the route's `Literal` by a contract test, and the rest is asserted on `app.js` source. No real song, no real take and no export longer than eight seconds has been through the master preset, so the loudness target has not been judged by ear on actual music, which is the one thing that decides whether -16 LUFS is right for this application.

**Mutation pass: 34 defects, 34 killed**, files restored byte-identically (SHA-256 compared per mutation). Both presets' encoder values and the default's identity; the loudness filter dropped, drifted, applied to the draft too, and dropped from the mix-graph tail; the sample-rate conform dropped; the preset withheld from the trims and from the join separately (a route honouring it only at the join would normalize a file every frame of which was encoded at draft settings); the progress flags moved behind the inputs and `-nostats` dropped; the parser accepting any key, raising on an unparseable value, and passing a negative clock; monotonicity, the fraction cap and the zero-length guard; the stage weighting; the model field's default; both stages' reporting; the settlement's 100; the drain task moved after the loop and its await turned into a second sequential read; and four on the client — the default, the terminal-status filter, the clamp, and the request body. Two survivors were fixed rather than reported: a `min(100, …)` at the write that no test could distinguish from the fraction cap above it (removed, one cap now, killable), and a lost-stderr mutation that nothing checked (the forced-failure test above).

**Not fixed, deliberately.** The preset is not remembered anywhere — not on the project, not in the job's `inputs` — so "which preset made this export" survives only in the response and in this log; the job records the file, not the recipe. And the two-second progress tick is a second polling timer beside AD-1's, running only while the request is open; folding it into the one poll would mean teaching `hasActiveRenderJobs` about jobs that have nothing to reconcile, which is the distinction that keeps local work out of the ComfyUI path.

## 2026-08-20 — A name is not a description: the appearance anchor

From the Calliope teardown (`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/calliope-teardown-and-improvement-plan-2026-08-20.md`, phases 2.1 and 2.2). Epic 3's goal — "a character recognizably the same person across the Shots of a finished video" — has had its mechanism since 2026-08-18 and has never been demonstrated; `docs/ROADMAP.md` says so, and the one live artifact had a reference sheet's own layout bleed into a shot's composition. Calliope attacks the same problem one layer lower and cheaper, at the *text*, and the two stack.

The gap was small and exact. Our prompts already say "hold identity, wardrobe, palette and lens fixed" in three places. What was missing was **anything for those instructions to point at**: `reference_map_tag_lines` emitted `<Picture 1> is Lucy`, where `Lucy` is the asset's *name*, and a name carries no appearance information at all. The model writing the prompt was told to hold a look fixed and never told what the look was, so it described whoever it imagined and fought the reference picture.

- **`Asset.consistency_prompt`** (`models.py:120`) is one user-editable phrase per asset. It **wins** over `Asset.prompt` and over the vision summary everywhere a description is consumed; `timeline._asset_description` is the single place that ordering is written down, and it is where the assistant's library reads it. Bounded at 400 characters after trimming — an anchor, not a second style bible; it is appended to every tag line of every prompt citing the asset, where a paragraph would crowd out the shot's own direction.
- **Nothing infers one.** No route derives it from a generation prompt, `analyze_asset` writes `vision` and only `vision`, and neither model-facing schema carries the property — a test asserts its absence from `assistant_tools()` and `AssetProposal`. That is the recorded rule for a mechanical field after the boolean-dropout incident (2026-08-19): a model may suggest, an explicit `PUT` writes.
- **`timeline.anchored_label` is the one composition**, shared by `reference_map_tag_lines`, the submit route's own tag walk and `shot_expansion_input`. The three had to agree or a prompt would promise a description the render never sends. A per-shot `reference_labels` rename composes as apposition — `the woman upstage, a woman in a red leather jacket` — because the rename says who this picture is *in this shot* and the anchor says what she looks like in every shot; a rename meant to replace the look is expressed by clearing the anchor.
- **The write path, and its siblings, enumerated.** `PUT /assets/{id}/consistency-prompt` is the only writer. `upload_asset`, `generate_flux` and `fill_assets` create assets that cannot carry one. `generate_multiview` **inherits** the source's anchor and `edit_asset` deliberately **does not** — a turnaround sheet promises the same subject unchanged and is the asset shots actually cite, while an AI Mod is the act of changing what the subject looks like, so inheriting would carry a description the edit was run to invalidate. `analyze_asset` leaves it alone. `delete_asset` takes it with the asset. And **the generic full-project `PUT` re-adopts the stored value per id**, writing `""` for an id the stored project does not hold: that route has been the hole in a guard three separate times, because it binds a whole `Project` whose every field is defaulted, so a body that merely omits a key is indistinguishable from one that cleared it. The same server-owned treatment the recovery slots, the locks and the thread already get.
- **Byte-identity for the empty case, everywhere, and that is what makes it shippable.** `anchored_label` returns the bare label unchanged when no anchor is stored, so an anchor-free project's reference map, expansion input, specialist system prompt and submitted payload are character-for-character what they were. The two pinned H3 payload digests and the two pinned `shot_expansion_input` digests pass untouched — and because the expansion digests are computed over a project holding *no* assets, a second test re-digests the same two shots with their cited assets present and anchor-free, which is the shape a builder emitting `"anchor": ""` unconditionally would have slipped past.
- **The persona rule lives in exactly one file** (`h3_expansion_prompt.APPEARANCE_ANCHOR_RULES`) and rides only where the data does, gated on the payload actually carrying an `anchor` key — `KEYFRAME_REFERENCE_RULES`' pattern, for its reason. It is not repeated in `assistant_prompt.py` or `director.EXPANSION_SYSTEM_PROMPT`: those write plain-prose *intent*, not the rendered prompt, and they receive the anchor as an ordinary asset description. Three copies of one sentence is three places to iterate it and two places to forget. Written as a prescription with an example rather than a prohibition, this machine's measured pattern three times over.
- **Mutation-checked: 22 defects reintroduced one at a time, 22 killed, every file restored byte-identical (SHA-256 compared).** Among them: the anchor appended even when empty; the anchor read raw instead of whitespace-collapsed; the anchor no longer outranking vision and prompt; the expansion input emitting the key unconditionally and ignoring the shot's rename; the reference map and each of the submit route's *two* tag templates dropping it; the whole-project PUT adopting only over a blank and keeping an invented anchor on an unknown id; the multiview sheet not inheriting and the AI Mod child inheriting; the route's bound, its trim and its 404 each removed; the persona rule forced on and forced off; and four frontend rules — the audio exclusion, both halves of `savable`, and the box drawn below the generation prompt instead of above it.

**What was verified: offline only.** 24 cases across 14 new tests (`tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_timeline.py`, `tests/test_frontend_contract.py`), including a fresh `ProjectStore` round-trip of the stored anchor and of its clearing, a hand-written manifest with the key genuinely absent, and the frontend's decision rule executed under node for every `AssetKind`. 1041 passing; `uv run ruff check .` clean; both `node --check` gates clean.

**Not verified: the UI was not driven in a real browser**, and no live model has ever seen `APPEARANCE_ANCHOR_RULES` — its wording is a first draft against no output at all, unlike the keyframe and sings-to-camera clauses beside it, which were measured before they shipped. **No render was submitted and no GPU time was spent**, so whether an anchor actually improves character consistency in a finished take is exactly as undemonstrated as it was this morning; this change gives the Director the lever, not the evidence. The `<Picture N>` numbering disagreement between `shot_expansion_input` and the per-kind route walk is untouched and still open — it is a separate story with its own byte-identity surface.

## 2026-08-20 — A reply is not the same thing as the JSON in it

From the Calliope teardown (`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/calliope-teardown-and-improvement-plan-2026-08-20.md`, phase 1.1). Every structured Director call did `json.loads(self._content(response))` on the whole reply, which is correct exactly while the provider behaves. Two recorded failures say it does not always.

- **The one that already happened.** `enable_thinking: false` stopped taking effect on 2026-08-19 (logged that day) and the loaded model began reasoning **inside `message.content`** before answering. The payload is then `<paragraphs of reasoning> {"real":"json"}`, and `json.loads` refuses the whole string over chatter sitting beside a perfectly good object. Every one of those turns reached the Director as a 502 about an "invalid response" that was, in substance, valid.
- **The one that has not happened here.** Some OpenAI-compatible servers reject `response_format` outright with a 400.

**`extract_json` is a four-rung ladder** (`director.py:531`), pure and I/O-free. Rung 1 is `json.loads` on the stripped text and is byte-for-byte what this module did before — a reply that is exactly one JSON object never reaches rung 2, which is the point: the happy path may not pay for the recovery path. Rung 2 is the contents of a markdown fence. Rung 3 points `json.JSONDecoder().raw_decode` at each `{` and `[` in turn and takes the first offset that decodes. Nothing decodes and the **first** failure is re-raised unchanged, so every caller's `except (..., json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError)` still translates it into the same `DirectorError` with a comparable message. All four `_content` call sites route through it — `plan`, `stage_manager`, `expand`, `inspect_image` — asserted per call, because when the host starts reasoning in `content` it does so on all four and a ladder on one would leave the other three as 502s.

- **The fence is tried before the scan, and that ordering is a decision, not a formality.** A reasoning model that quotes a fragment of the schema while it thinks (`{"message": "<one sentence>", "shots": []}`) and then answers inside a fence has emitted *two* valid JSON objects; the fence is the only thing that says which is the answer. Scan-first returns the scratch note as the plan. `test_a_fence_beats_a_json_fragment_quoted_in_the_reasoning_in_front_of_it` is the only test that kills a dropped fence rung — the plain fenced-reply case survives it, because the scan finds fenced JSON perfectly well.
- **Rung 3 is a parser, not a brace counter.** `raw_decode` consumes exactly one value, so a `{` inside a string — and an H3 prompt is full of punctuation — cannot end the object early, and trailing chatter is simply not part of what it consumed. A hand-rolled depth counter would truncate such a prompt *silently*, into a shorter object that still validates.
- **`response_format` is not dropped by default and this is the fallback only.** An *unexplained* 400 against a body that carried `response_format` is retried once with the key removed (`director.py:699`); the ladder is then what makes the unconstrained reply usable. The strict `json_schema` reaches LM Studio's constrained decoder, which is stronger than anything parsing can recover, so giving it up by choice on the setup this project runs on would be a downgrade. "Unexplained" is load-bearing: a 400 that said "Model is unloaded" has a known cause that the older retry has just handled, and re-sending it schema-free would be a third request failing for the second one's reason while burying the provider's own refusal. A body that never carried the key is never retried at all.

**Mutation-checked, five defects, all killed, restores verified by SHA-256** (an inverse string replacement is not a restore — recorded 2026-08-18): dropping the fence rung, dropping the `raw_decode` scan, firing the retry regardless of `response_format`, firing it on an unloaded-model 400 too, and never firing it. The fourth is killed by a *pre-existing* test (`test_a_models_listing_of_an_unexpected_shape_does_not_escape_as_an_attribute_error`), which is the honest report: that guard exists because the older contract already forbade the third request.

**What was verified: offline only.** 11 cases across 10 new tests in `tests/test_director.py`, driven entirely by `httpx.MockTransport` — clean JSON, fenced JSON with and without a language tag and with prose in front, a truncated fence, reasoning chatter before the object, chatter after it, braces inside a string value, a decoy object before a fenced answer, four unparseable shapes still raising `json.JSONDecodeError`, a non-string still raising `TypeError`, all four call sites parsing a chatter-wrapped answer, all four still raising the same `DirectorError` on prose with no object in it, and the schema-free retry firing exactly once with everything but the one key unchanged. 1006 passing; `ruff` clean; both JS gates run and clean (no JS was touched).

**Not verified: any of it against a live LM Studio.** No model was loaded, no request left the process, and the reasoning-chatter fixture is a *reconstruction* of the 2026-08-19 shape written from the log entry, not a captured transcript — so it proves the ladder handles that shape, not that it is the shape the host currently emits. The `response_format` 400 has never been observed on this project's provider at all; it is ported from Calliope's report. Both need one live Director turn to move from "handled" to "seen handled".

**Not fixed, deliberately.** `parse_assistant_reply` still calls bare `json.loads` on a tool call's `arguments` string (`director.py:364`); that is a different channel with a different failure mode (the recorded one there is *boolean dropout*, not chatter), and routing it through the ladder would be an unmeasured change to the one path that already has a documented workaround. `document_rejection` also keeps its bare `json.loads` on purpose — it asks "is this whole string JSON masquerading as prose", and a ladder would make it reject any treatment that happens to quote an object.

## 2026-08-20 — A prompt can no longer cite a picture that is not attached

From the Calliope teardown (`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/calliope-teardown-and-improvement-plan-2026-08-20.md`, item 2.3): that project caps its prompt's subject roster at the workflow's actual wired reference-slot count. `docs/ROADMAP.md` has claimed since 2026-08-18 that "undefined references" are checkable before a render. **They were checkable and were not checked** — `h3_prompt.py` had `check_shots`, `check_orphan_cuts`, `check_dialogue`, `check_sound_fields` and `check_retention`, and nothing anywhere compared a `<Picture N>` against what the shot actually cites. The roadmap sentence was a plan read as a status.

- **`h3_prompt.check_reference_bounds(prompt, *, slots)`** is the rule, pure and both ways. Over-citation is **fatal**: H3's media slots are anonymous, so `<Picture 3>` on a two-picture shot is not an error the sampler can report — it renders, plausibly, conditioned on a slot nothing fills. Under-citation **warns and never blocks**, matching `batch.readiness_report`'s split between emptiness (blocks) and sameness (warns): a style or lighting reference may honestly have nothing to say for itself in prose, and a gate that refuses that gets worked around along with the true refusals.
- **The three kinds are counted separately**, because the conditioner wires pictures, videos and audios into three per-kind slot lists — `<Video 1>` and `<Picture 1>` are different slots, and one shared counter would have produced false refusals on every mixed shot. `<Subject N>` is deliberately *not* bounded: a subject is defined by the prompt itself and wired into no slot at all.
- **A kind the caller omits is skipped in both directions, and that clause is the design.** `app.reference_slot_counts` returns `None` — check nothing — for a shot citing any video or audio, and for one citing an Asset the project does not hold. The first is honest about a real disagreement found while building this: `timeline.shot_expansion_input` numbers *every* citation into the `<Picture N>` series regardless of kind, while the route and `reference_map_tag_lines` number per kind. On a picture-only shot the two agree exactly, which is every shot the application produces today; on a video-citing shot they do not, and bounding it would refuse the specialist for writing the tag it was handed. The second keeps the route's own "Unknown reference asset" refusal from being replaced by a sentence about the prompt. **The upstream numbering disagreement is reported, not fixed** — it is a `timeline.py` change with its own byte-identity surface.
- **Wired in two places, deliberately.** The blocking half runs in `generate_h3` before any payload is built, over the text the submission actually sends. The whole rule also rides `h3_prompt.check`'s new optional `reference_slots` argument, so `attempt_expansion`'s retry loop feeds an invented slot back to the model as a corrective turn — fixed for free, in its next answer, before a GPU pass — and the advisory list on `/expand` carries the unmentioned-picture warnings.
- **No payload moved.** `reference_slots` defaults to `None`, so every existing checker call is byte-for-byte the call it was; the route guard only refuses or falls through. The pinned digest tests (`test_both_existing_shot_shapes_still_build_the_byte_identical_payload` and its citation-written twin) pass untouched — and the reference digest shot cites a video, so it takes the documented skip.
- **Verified offline only.** 979 passing (`uv run pytest --ignore=tests/test_director.py`; that one file was mid-edit in a shared tree by concurrent work and fails to import a `director.py` symbol that does not exist yet — unrelated to this change, and the whole suite including it was green when this work started). `uv run ruff check .` clean, both `node --check` gates clean. Mutation pass on every branch introduced: **15/15 killed**, files restored byte-identical (SHA-256 compared). One kill is worth naming — removing the fatal-only filter at the route broke `test_h3_submission_serializes_multiple_references_and_master_audio`, an existing test whose shot attaches media its prompt never names, which is direct evidence that blocking on under-citation would have refused work the application already produces. **Not verified: no render was submitted, no ComfyUI call was made, and no live model wrote a prompt against this check.** Whether the retry loop actually recovers an over-cited slot in one turn is unmeasured.

## 2026-08-18 - The Director lifts a ceiling: keyframes ride references mode, with the song

I reported that keyframe shots cannot lip-sync, and the Director pushed back: "the input node I see in my version... allows multiple types of input including audio/video. I think you need to take another serious look at that because it is huge for the MiniMax workflows." They were right, and the correction is the more instructive for the schema reading having been *accurate*: `MiniMaxH3ImageToVideo` genuinely has no audio input - but that is the simple path, not the ceiling. MiniMax's guide 2.2.2 uses a reference picture **as** a shot's first frame, keyframe or last frame, declared in the structured prompt with `fully_preserved` retention - on `MiniMaxH3ReferenceToVideo`, the node that takes the windowed master song. **Keyframes and lip-sync combine in references mode.** H3's media slots are anonymous; the prompt is where a slot becomes "the first frame". A correct fact, framed as a boundary it is not, misleads exactly as well as a wrong one.

- **The blocking line was one line**: `SHOT_MODE_SPECS["references"]` accepted only the `reference` role. Now `first` (0-1) and `last` (0-1) join it, counting against the node's own 9-picture ceiling (9+1 -> 422, tested).
- **One numbering, structurally.** `citations_in_prompt_order` is the single `(role, order)` walk both prompt channels and the payload's media order read, so `<Picture 2>` in the prompt is picture 2 on the wire. The first mutation pass exposed two survivors here - adversarial fixtures where `(order, role)` coincided with `(role, order)` - fixed by making the fixtures actually adversarial. A tag pointing at the wrong slot renders plausibly and wrongly, the worst failure class this project knows.
- **The specialist's keyframe rule was measured, and the winner is a prescription, not a prohibition.** Baseline: **0/16** answers anchored the keyframe declaration in the guide's form. A candidate that *instructed abstractly*: 0/6. A candidate that *showed the shape as a positive template*: **12/12**, with the placeholder-copy risk counted and absent, and the pre-existing malformedness not worsened. After two prohibitions that made output worse earlier today, the pattern is now three-for-three: this local model follows examples and copies prohibitions.
- **The thinking flag drifted again mid-measurement** - `enable_thinking: false` stopped working after another model reload; a 5-way probe found `reasoning_effort: "none"` is the control that holds. Both are now sent. A sweep suddenly full of retries is telling you about the host.
- Byte-identity held everywhere it was promised: reference-only shots, both dedicated keyframe modes, and every other shot's specialist prompt (the keyframe rules append only when a references shot actually cites a keyframe role).
- 888 passing; pre-flight OK 248 nodes across 14 variants including the new mixed-role variant. The live proof - a pinned opening frame *and* a window-driven performance in one references render - follows immediately.

## 2026-08-18 — The Director's own flow, rendered: first frame in, motion out

The keyframe adapter's live check ran the flow the Director described when this direction was set: *"have an image generated so I could try to go for something specific or get a preview or first frame."* A Flux frame (a wolf at a birch treeline, sodium dusk), cited in the `first` role on an `image_to_video` Shot, marked ready, rendered — 344 s at 20 steps.

Every prediction held, and each one traces to a specific piece of earlier work:
- **1056×608** — the measured 0.6 MP selection the resolution story established.
- **124 frames for a 5 s shot, 5.167 s** — 17×7+5, the grid the schema's own tooltip names.
- **`fl2va` in the submitted graph, `ref2va` nowhere** — the orphan analysis at import time is what made loading the wrong 20-GB checkpoint a caught mutation instead of a live surprise.
- **`last_frame` genuinely absent from the wire** — the schema-optionality reading, honoured rather than papered over with a null.
- **Frame 0 of the take is visibly the cited image**, and frame 100 shows the prompt's motion (the wolf stepping from the treeline toward camera). That is the adapter's whole promise, seen rather than asserted.
- Generated aac audio present — and no song audio, because `MiniMaxH3ImageToVideo` has **no audio input of any kind**: the fact was learned from the schema before design, so `use_song_audio` refuses in words instead of silently dropping, and the mode labels say "no song lip-sync" before the click. A singing shot belongs in `references` mode.

## 2026-08-18 — Completion reaches the screen, and a sweep stops needing luck

Two streams landed together, both from the Director's live session.

**The polling gap.** The Director generated a character; ComfyUI rendered it and the asset card said "Rendering" forever, because **no automatic polling existed anywhere in the frontend** — the only timer in `app.js` dismissed toasts, and `refreshJobs` was bound solely to the manual queue-panel button. The silence invited a second click, and the form's fixed seed made ComfyUI render the identical image twice. The architecture spine had ruled on the transport (AD-1: 2 s polling, one `/queue` fetch per tick) on 2026-08-16, and it was never implemented.

- Built as ruled: `GET /api/projects/{id}/render-status` reconciles once per tick; an idle project makes zero requests; the browser timer exists **iff** the project has open jobs. The completion mutation moved out of `read_job` into `apply_job_history`, so Shot.status has exactly one completion writer.
- The frontend patch is **monotone** — settled jobs never regress, assets never un-land — because the whole-list shots PUT re-asserts every local field, and a stale patch would be written back to the server by the next drag. The sibling-write-path hazard, again, in a new costume.
- The report shape carries **no inspector-editable field**, so a poll can structurally never overwrite typing; and a tick is skipped while `shotWriteInFlight`, so it can never interleave with a sweep's read-to-save window.
- Verified in a real browser against a scripted ComfyUI double: idle = zero polling requests over three intervals; completion reached toast, queue row and painted asset card with no click; polling stood down after the last job settled; `/prompt` hits asserted = 0.
- A vanished prompt (ComfyUI restarted mid-render) keeps its status rather than being invented an error — parity with manual refresh, pinned by a test.

**Expansion retries, per the Director's ruling.** Up to four calls per shot through one shared loop under all three doors. A checker-rejected answer retries as a corrective follow-up turn carrying the checker's own sentences; budget exhaustion got its own error type and retries clean; unavailability never retries. Attempt counts are reported everywhere because a shot that took three tries is diagnostic signal.

**The `not_singing` dialogue rule shipped with its honesty intact.** Baseline reproduced the live defect exactly once in 12 well-formed answers — a verbatim lyric inside `<d>[English]…</d>` with "she does not sing it" written around it. The shipped rule quotes no example lyric (the copy-the-prohibition trap that sank an earlier rule), and the stated claim is only that it does not backfire; 0/8 against 1/12 proves nothing about efficacy and says so.

**Two process findings worth keeping:**
- **An inverse string replacement is not a byte restore.** Two `app.js` mutation restores re-anchored at the wrong occurrence of an identical string; the SHA-256 gate is what caught it. The hash check is the restore verification — the replacement is just an attempt.
- **`enable_thinking: false` did not survive a model reload.** Mid-measurement, LM Studio resumed spending entire budgets on reasoning (7 of 10 calls) despite the flag — probe-confirmed host drift. A sweep suddenly full of "took N tries" after a model reload is telling you about the host, and it is exactly the failure class the retries absorb.

## 2026-08-18 — A shot can say what it is

Closed under `spec-shot-mode-foundation.md` — the keystone the whole shot-mode direction rests on. `generate_h3` chose between the text-only and reference paths by asking whether `asset_ids` happened to be empty, so a shot could not declare its kind or be wrong about it before a render.

- **The taxonomy is a table, not branches.** `SHOT_MODE_SPECS` carries label, per-role minimum and maximum, song-audio capability and adapter name, so adding a mode is a row. `ShotMode`, `AssetRole`, `SingingState` and `AssetCitation{asset_id, role, order}` are the vocabulary.
- **`Shot.mode` already existed, which the spec did not know.** It was a dropdown position nobody read, holding legacy strings. Reading them as declarations would have changed what existing shots render — a direct violation of the story's own "Never" clause — so they resolve to **undeclared**, and the new vocabulary deliberately shares no spelling with them (`"reference"` → `"references"`) so the two can never be silently conflated.
- **Byte-identity was verified against a baseline taken before any edit**, extracted from `git show HEAD:` into a scratch tree: `e523c516…` for the reference shape, `3745c411…` for text-only, both reproduced exactly and frozen as constants in a permanent test. Worth recording the harness bug found on the way: the first normalisation missed a **doubly-escaped temp path inside `media_state`** — a JSON string nested inside JSON — which produced a digest that changed every run and looked exactly like a regression. It was not one, and a less careful reading would have "fixed" a payload that was already correct.
- **Shots were never classified for the Director's context guard; only songs were.** So every field ever added to `Shot` has entered the model's prompt the moment it was declared, with nobody deciding. `SHOT_DIRECTOR_VISIBLE`/`SHOT_DIRECTOR_WITHHELD` now exist with **withheld deliberately empty** — removing a field from the dump is Ask First — so today's dump is asserted byte-identical to yesterday's. What it buys is that the *next* field fails at import instead of leaking.
- **`singing` is `unknown` by default and nothing infers it.** A `Literal` rather than `bool | None`, so `if not shot.singing` cannot quietly mean "not singing", with a test grepping all of `src/` for any assignment that would guess. The enhancer moves lip position, so a wrong default in either direction is worse than an honest absence.
- **One mutation was initially missed, and the miss was the interesting part.** Reverting the adapter gate to the old `shot.asset_ids or shot.use_song_audio` passed every test, because the only shape where the two disagree is a shot that *declares* `references` while citing nothing — which cannot exist in any saved manifest. It is real, though: it now routes to the reference graph and is refused there in the adapter's own words, where the old condition would have quietly rendered it text-only. A new test kills it.
- **Three planning-table rows were deliberately not made modes**, and saying so matters more than the code: image editing is a `length: 5` reference render and therefore a *parameter* of `references`; enhancement is an operation on a take with its own route; slicing and audio replacement are file utilities. Each is one row if that judgement is wrong.
- **Two shared test facilities were repaired**, both latent holes rather than new breakage. The stub DOM answered every *scoped* `querySelectorAll` with `[]`, so anything bound via `$$(sel, scope)` was bound to nothing and unreachable by any test — an entire class of frontend guarantee was untestable. And `run_module` decoded node's stdout with the Windows code page, mangling every typographic character the workspace draws.
- **16 mutations, 16 caught**, all files restored under SHA-256. 637 passing, ruff clean, `node --check` clean, and `tests/e2e_shot_controls.py` green in a real browser with nothing submitted.

## 2026-08-18 — The real song goes back over the picture, verified by correlation

The audio-restore stage ran live on a take conditioned at 12–15.75 s. **Correlation +0.9945 at exactly lag 0.000 s** against the master's own 12–15.75 s, extracted independently of the application — against the ≈ 0.01-at-every-lag the take itself measures. The restored file is the master's seconds, not a regeneration.

Three departures from the export were each validated by the same run:

- **90 frames preserved.** The export's loader carries `format: "LTXV"`, which declares `frames: [8, 1]`. An H3 take sits on the **17k+5** grid — 90 frames for 3.75 s — and `8n+1` does not contain 90, so the picture would have been silently cut to 89 under audio that was not. Every restoration would have been a frame out of sync, in the same direction, forever. Uses `"None"`, and the pre-flight asserts **both** halves — that `"None"` conforms nothing *and* that `"LTXV"` still conforms something — so the check cannot pass vacuously.
