- **24 fps preserved.** The export's saver hardcodes `frame_rate: 25` against an H3 take's 24, a 4% speed-up over an unmodified soundtrack. Read from `VHS_VideoInfo` instead.
- **The window landed at zero lag**, which is the shared `song_audio_window` doing its job. The builder takes no window parameter at all — only `start`, `duration` and `song_duration`, the three numbers `generate_h3` already passes — so there is no seam for a second computation to drift through.

The take was untouched: `latest_output` still points at the H3 render, and the restored file is a sibling. Both the generated audio and the restored audio now exist side by side, which is the point — hearing "voices but no phonetics" is what found a real conditioning bug, and a pipeline that discards H3's own output discards its best diagnostic.

One honest narrowing carried from the implementation: "the picture is copied through" is a **re-encode**, not a stream copy. ComfyUI has no stream-copy mux, so the take is decoded to tensors and re-encoded at the crf it was written with. No generative model, sampler, VAE or model file appears anywhere in the payload — the graph names **zero** model files — so the frozen "never re-encoded through a generative model" holds exactly, but bit-for-bit frames do not. A truly bit-identical video stream needs `ffmpeg -c:v copy`, which is not a thing a ComfyUI graph can express.

## 2026-08-18 — The first render with all three inputs correct

Three variables were wrong at once, and a three-way comparison separates them. All three frames are the same shot, same seed, same turbo bundle, frame 45.

| Render | Frame | Window | Face |
|---|---|---|---|
| Original | 640×384 (0.25 MP) | none — conditioned on all 154 s | Blurred and malformed; eyes indistinct |
| Control | 640×384 (0.25 MP) | `trim 12.0–15.75` | **Coherent** — properly formed mouth and eyes, still soft |
| Corrected | **1056×608** (0.64 MP) | `trim 12.0–15.75` | **Resolved** — teeth, eyeliner, blood-tear makeup, earrings, separated hair strands |

The control run existed only to isolate the two fixes, and it earned its GPU minutes: **the two contributions are different in kind.**

- **The audio window bought coherence.** Conditioned on the track's 12–15.75 s — where the Director actually sings — H3 produces a properly formed face and mouth shapes that correspond to phonemes. The original was conditioned on 154 seconds of song dominated by a wordless intro, and produced the malformed mouth the Director described as "phantoms trying to speak from a tomb".
- **Resolution bought detail.** At the same window, going from 0.25 MP to the Director's own 0.64 MP is what makes eyeliner, teeth and makeup exist at all. It does not fix a malformed face — the control proves that was the window's doing.

Had only the resolution been changed, the result would have been a sharper malformed face, and the conclusion would probably have been that H3's identity fidelity is poor. Had only the window been fixed, the face would have been correct and still too soft to judge, and the conclusion would probably have been that the resolution was fine. **Either fix alone would have supported a confident wrong verdict**, which is the argument for the control run rather than a two-variable comparison.

Verified in the submitted graph, read back from `/history`: `width 1056`, `height 608`, `length 90`, master song `"trim": {"start": 12.0, "end": 15.75}`, turbo LoRA at 0.7 with `beta`/`euler`/4 steps. Measured output: 1056×608, 90 frames, 3.750 s video against 3.744 s audio, sync delta −0.006 s. Elapsed 136.8 s.

Unchanged and still true: the output audio does **not** resemble the master track, because H3 regenerates it from the conditioning window. That is by design and is not a defect.

## 2026-08-18 — Three cheap test values, mistaken for three properties of the system

The Director looked at a rendered clip and said the face was badly degraded and the audio had "voices but not speaking words — phantoms trying to speak from a tomb, haunting but no phonetics". Chasing that produced four corrections, three of which are mine, and one genuine defect.

- **We were rendering at 0.25 MP where the Director's pipeline uses 0.6 MP.** `tests/smoke_h3_reference_app.py` pinned 640×384 to make the run cheap. The Director's `ResolutionSelector` picks 0.6 MP / 16:9 / multiple 32, measured on this machine as **1056×608**. On a full-body framing the face occupies the same fraction of frame either way, so ours landed on tens of pixels of height. **Every quality verdict this project has recorded — the 4-step versus 20-step comparison, the turbo assessment, the "coherent cinematic frame" line — was made at that size and is void.**
- **A correction to that correction, found by the implementation.** The shipped *API* default was **1344×768 (1.03 MP)**, not 640×384; the browser posts `{}` and got that. 640×384 came only from the smoke's constants. So this change *lowers* the API default to 0.64 MP while *raising* what the smoke renders from 0.25 MP. Both move toward the Director's own selection, and neither had been chosen by anyone. The comparison baseline is the 640×384 smoke frame; **no 1344×768 frame appears to exist.**
- **The reference node never windowed the audio at all, and my spec said the opposite.** `docs/WORKFLOW-MAP.md` recorded "the audio reference is trimmed to the render window by the node", inferred from a 154 s master producing 3.744 s of output. Reading `comfy_extras/nodes_minimax_h3.py` settles it: standalone `ref_audios` go straight to `_encode_ref_audio`, which VAE-encodes the **entire waveform** — reference *videos* are cut to `frame_count`, their audio is not, and neither is standalone audio. The 3.744 s was the *generated* latent's own length from `temporal_shape(length)`. Two coincidental numbers read as cause and effect. **So a 3.75 s shot was conditioned on 154 seconds of song riding through every sampling step**, which is worse than the bug as originally written.
- **The window lives one node upstream**, in `MiniMaxH3MediaLoader`'s `media_state` as `{"trim": {"start", "end"}}` — not on `MiniMaxH3ReferenceToVideo`, which declares no window input of any kind. Worth checking rather than assuming, since the Director path's `start_second`/`end_second` made the assumption inviting.
- **The `start = 0` byte-identity row was renegotiated once its premise fell.** The frozen matrix required a 0 s shot to send no window, justified by the node-trims premise above. With that gone, byte-identity at 0 s preserves *the defect* — and preserves it for the shot most likely to exist in a fresh project. The window is now always sent. The cost is stated rather than hidden: **no reference payload is byte-identical to a pre-fix one any more**, which is correct, because every one of them was conditioned on the whole song.
- **What the Director heard was H3 working correctly on the wrong input.** Lyrics on that track start at 8–10 s; the shot was 0–3.75 s. H3 was conditioned on wordless intro atmospherics and faithfully regenerated them. Separately established the same day: H3 **generates** its output audio by design — `VHS_VideoCombine.audio` comes from a `VAEDecodeAudio` on the sampler's latent in our adapter and in both canonical exports alike — so the output correlating with the master at ≈ 0.01 is expected, not a fault. Muxing the real track back over a finished cut is the Director's separate `AudioReplacer` graph and is a **missing pipeline stage**.
- **The pattern is now three for three, and deserves naming.** 4-step sampling, `start = 0.0`, and 640×384 were each chosen to make a test cheap, and each became the value every conclusion was drawn from. Nothing in a passing test announces that its sample is unrepresentative. The smoke's docstring now carries that warning where the next person will read it, and the run aborts before spending anything if its start ever returns to 0.

## 2026-08-18 — Enhance a take instead of regenerating it, and a prose file that cannot be trusted

Two adapters worked in parallel. One closed a long-open gap; the other overturned the premise it was written on, which is the more useful of the two outcomes.

### The LTX 2.5 enhancer

Closed under `spec-ltx25-enhancer-adapter.md`. LTX was reachable only by regenerating H3 inside the reference chain, so improving a take cost a full H3 pass and produced a different picture.

- **Four things the export contradicted, each found by reading it rather than the description.** The orphan is the *audio* `VAELoaderKJ` **node**, not the class — the reachable video VAE is the same class, so a per-class dependency rule would have dropped a model the graph needs. `VHS_LoadVideo` cannot be reproduced at all: its `video` combo enumerates ComfyUI's **input** directory while a take lives under **output**, so `VHS_LoadVideoPath` carries it. The detailer LoRA is invisible at the top level of `Power Lora Loader (rgthree)`, the same finding the turbo profile hit from the other direction. And the frame-count grid has an **earlier cause than the sampler**: the VHS loader's `format: "LTXV"` declares `frames: [8, 1]`, so the clip is conformed to 8k+1 *at load* — 185 is 8 × 23 + 1. That is a second, upstream cause rather than a replacement for the tiling explanation, and nothing claims which dominates.
- **The pre-flight caught its own blind spot before any GPU time**, reporting three model dependencies instead of four because the rgthree nesting hid the filename. That is the audit doing exactly the job it exists for, offline rather than as an opaque 502.
- **The route writes nothing to the Shot.** Mutations that let the enhancement share the render's filename prefix, write status or `prompt_id` at submission, or move `latest_output` on completion are each caught. Enhancement is deliberately not approval.
- **8 mutations, 8 killed**, run against an isolated copy so the concurrent agent's tree was never touched. Including dependencies taken from the 20-node list instead of the 18 reachable, and a test that asserts output frames equal input frames — which fails a guard that greps the adapter, route, pre-flight and tests for exactly that claim.
- Deliberately not built: no UI, since take comparison is what would make a second output meaningful on screen and remains unbuilt; and no locked/approved refusals, since neither is in the matrix and neither is endangered by a route that writes nothing.

### Live verification, 2026-08-18 — three runs, and one result that changes how the enhancer may be used

- **Multiview, character and object, both good.** A Flux character and a Flux cargo ship promoted through the shipped path. The character sheet holds identity, wardrobe and hair across a face detail plus front, profile and back; the ship sheet holds hull, panel lines, turret and boarding ladder across four angles. The child inherits its source's kind, so a promoted ship stays a `prop`.
- **The enhancer works and the frame-grid prediction was right.** 640×384 / 90 frames → **1920×1152 / 89 frames**, 3.750 s → 3.708 s, audio surviving, 549 KB → 2.98 MB, aspect preserved exactly. **89 = 8 × 11 + 1** — the 8k+1 grid the VHS loader's `format: "LTXV"` imposes at load — against an input of 90, which is H3's 17k+5 grid. The take was untouched: `latest_output` still pointed at the H3 render afterwards.
- **But the enhancer is not an upscale, and this is the finding worth the GPU time.** The output is markedly sharper — corset lacing, arm-guard studs, hair strands, roof trusses, light fixtures — and it is also a **different performance**. Sampled at frames 20, 44 and 70, the source is mid-vowel with teeth visible while the enhanced clip has the mouth **closed**, consistently rather than as a one-off. The cause is in the graph rather than inferred: `ManualSigmas` starts at **0.909375**, a high starting noise level, so it re-generates rather than refines. For a project whose premise is H3's audio-driven lip-sync, that disqualifies it on any singing shot. Usable on B-roll, objects and scenery; unproven-to-harmful on vocals. **Nothing about this was predictable from the graph alone**, which is exactly why the spec required measuring rather than asserting.
- **A process failure of mine, recorded because it nearly produced a false result.** The first object promotion returned the *old* 422 refusal. The code was correct; an app instance from an earlier probe had never exited, so the new server could not bind to the port and the health check passed against the stale process. I was one step from reporting "object promotion is broken" on the strength of a server running hour-old code. **A health check that only proves *something* is listening proves nothing about *what*.**
- **The enhancer's 409 in-flight guard fired for real** on first attempt: the target shot carried two `queued` job records that had completed in ComfyUI but were never reconciled. That is precisely the case the guard was built for — reading job records rather than status alone, because a stale status is what hides a live render — and its message named the fix.

### `Music-Video.md` is not a reliable description of `Krea2_CharacterSheet-20260814.json`

This entry exists because **I asserted the opposite, prominently and wrongly**, and the correction is worth more than the story that produced it.

I read the creator's prose — "three KSamplers: 10-step euler for the initial sheet, 8-step euler at CFG 0.3 for the refine pass, 8-step res_multistep for the final output" — and concluded that our multiview adapter ran one third of the documented pipeline, calling it the single largest lever on reference fidelity in the project. Reading the graph settles it the other way, on five counts:

- The file has **45** nodes, not 44.
- It has three `KSampler`s but **only one is in the sheet path**. Node `53` is `mode=0`; nodes `146` and `172` are both **`mode=4`, bypassed on load**, and sit in a "2nd Sampling" group at x ≈ −1129 while every `CharSheet-*` group is at positive x. They generate a character *portrait* that may optionally become the sheet's input image.
- "8-step euler at **CFG 0.3**" is a **widget-order misread**. The order is `[seed, control, steps, cfg, sampler, scheduler, denoise]`, so node `172` is cfg **1.0**, denoise **0.3**.
- `res_multistep` is the **first** of that bypassed pair, not the final pass; the euler pass runs after it.
- The "refine prompt toggle to regenerate weak panels" drives an LLM prompt-**enhancement** subgraph that expands the character description. It has nothing to do with panels.

**Our adapter already matched the creator's active sheet path value for value.** The two extra passes were therefore reverted rather than shipped: they are bypassed in the source, are not part of the sheet path, and the final pass's denoise had to be *invented* because no source specifies it. Shipping them would have changed every reference sheet in the project on the strength of a prose error — the same "third combination nobody has rendered, presented as proven" hazard that made the H3 sampling profiles reproduce whole bundles instead of blending them. The single pass is now pinned against the export, with node `53`'s `mode=0` and `146`/`172`'s `mode=4` asserted, so the next person who reads the prose and "fixes" the missing passes fails a test that explains why.

Also resolved from the same file: the prose's "1 MP sweet spot" is a note the creator did not follow — the graph emits 1536×1024, 1.57 MP, exactly what the adapter emits. Nothing changed, and it is now pinned so it is not re-litigated from the note.

## 2026-08-18 — A third sampling profile, and a node class that cannot be copied

The Director's per-mode API exports of 2026-08-17 revealed that the shipped `turbo` profile — derived from the "04 - H3 Music Video - LTX 2.5 READY" pipeline — is **not** the bundle the canonical `MiniMaxH3Turbo References2V` mode uses. Both are real. The Director chose to keep the shipped one and add the canonical alongside it, each reproducing its own source whole rather than being blended into a third combination nobody has rendered.

- **`turbo-references2v`**: `minimax_h3_turbo_v4_step600_ema_pruned_comfyui.safetensors` at strength 1.0, `simple`/8/`euler`, every value read out of the hash-pinned export rather than typed into a test — the correction that the shipped `turbo` profile's own test needed, applied from the start this time. Named after the source graph, not after a speed claim: `turbo-canonical` was rejected because "canonical" describes the *export* and would read as a verdict on the shipped `turbo`, which is equally evidenced and is the only one with a rendered frame behind it.
- **The rgthree node class cannot be faithfully reproduced, and live schema is what settled it** — not taste. `Power Lora Loader (rgthree)` publishes **no `lora_*` inputs at all** on `/object_info`: its `required` is empty and its LoRA rows are client-side widgets. Copying it would move the filename and strength beyond the schema's reach, so `check_model_files` could no longer confirm the file is installed, the strength check would govern an unused node, and `preflight.validate` would reject `lora_1` as a non-existent input. **The faithful copy would fail its own audit**, and relaxing the validator to admit it would delete the guarantee the audit exists for. Both turbo profiles therefore carry their LoRA through `LoraLoaderModelOnly`, evidenced in their values and not in their wiring — stated in the builder's docstring, in the profile comment, and asserted in a test rather than left for a reader to discover.
- **The audit-coverage tests were generalised rather than extended.** One of them hardcoded `len(with_lora) == 1`, which a second LoRA-carrying profile would have broken; it is now parametrised over every profile, with a second test asserting the other direction — that the audit samples no bundle a profile does not declare.
- **The orphan hazard the manifest warns about is present in this very export.** It carries an `fl2va` `UNETLoader` unreachable from its output, alongside resolution plumbing and a frame-count expression. The tests derive only from the chain reachable from the `ref2va` loader, so the orphan cannot contaminate them. A profile is a sampling bundle, not a graph, and nothing claims otherwise.
- **10 mutations, 10 caught**, files restored byte-identical. Including each of the five values drifting from the export, the variant vanishing from the audit, the audit overriding the profile's step count, and the LoRA node silently becoming the rgthree class. The tenth confirms the `7e25ad0` digest guarantee is still live: mutating the default profile's `shift_audio` from 3 to 4 still fails.
- Pre-flight now reports **`OK 176 nodes across 10 variants (19 classes)`** against live ComfyUI, up from `OK 157 / 9 / 19`. Only `/object_info` is fetched; nothing submitted. `uv run pytest` **545 passing**, ruff clean.
- Scope, stated plainly: **nothing has been rendered on this profile.** And its speed should not be predicted from its step count — the `turbo` comparison measured 23% off the wall clock for a 5× cut in steps.

## 2026-08-18 — A shot can be marked ready, and the test that would have caught it

Closed under `spec-mark-a-shot-ready.md`, the same day the render-again story exposed the gap. No shot could reach its **first** render through the application: `Shot.status` defaults to `draft`, the frontend only ever *read* `status === "ready"` to filter what the queue button may submit, and nothing in the interface ever wrote it. Every live render in this project's history was driven by a script or an API client, including the smoke scripts.

- **The end-to-end test is the story, not the routes.** Written first and confirmed failing for the right reason, it drives create → add shot → prompt → mark ready → submit using **only** routes the shipped frontend calls, behind a client wrapper that **fails on any request body carrying a shot status other than `"draft"`**, recursing into whole-`Project` bodies. That constraint is mechanical rather than promised: one stray `"status": "ready"` in a shots save would make every assertion pass while proving nothing. Two refusals in the middle are load-bearing — the placeholder shot is refused for its *prompt*, and the prompted shot is still refused for its *status*.
- **Why the gap survived a suite that covered everything.** Every guard around rendering had been tested in isolation and each one passed. The path *through* them was never walked, so a journey with no way to start it looked fully covered. That is the transferable lesson here, and it is worth more than any single assertion in the story.
- **The prompt gate is reused, not re-formed.** `batch.prompt_rejection` already owns what counts as a prompt worth rendering, placeholder refusal included. A second opinion about the same question is how two judgements drift apart.
- **Marking ready is not a certificate**, mirroring the decision already made for render-again: the render's own gate asks again, so a shot marked ready and then emptied is still refused at submission. The no-op sits *after* the refusals here, unlike render-again — marking an already-`ready` shot whose prompt has since been emptied returns a 422 naming the prompt rather than a silent 200.
- **Going back to `draft` has no prompt gate**, deliberately. Un-committing a shot whose prompt you just emptied is precisely the thing you would want to do.
- **Auto-marking is forbidden and tested, not merely unimplemented.** A mutation that has expansion arm every shot it prompts is caught. A Director running expansion to see what the model suggests must not discover they armed a plan.
- **Five spec errors, all mine.** The matrix answered 422 for an in-flight shot where the sibling `render_again` answers 409 for the same live render — now aligned to 409 alongside the song-context restore correction. "Always permitted to return to draft" contradicted "never touch a locked shot" for a locked `ready` shot; Never won. The "has rendered" row directed in-flight shots at render-again, which refuses them too — that would have spent a second click on a second refusal. `index.html` was listed as a task target and needs no change, the inspector being generated markup. And `approved_output` on a `draft` shot was absent from the matrix, though it is reachable and would otherwise route around render-again's approval argument entirely.
- **18 mutations, 18 caught**, all three files restored byte-identical under SHA-256. Including the render gate treating `status == "ready"` as a certificate, expansion arming what it prompts, the control's direction inverted, the click wired to the generic shots write instead of the purpose-built route, and the click re-deriving its direction from `shot.status` rather than from the decision that drew the button. Both browser guarantees are proven by executing — `markReadyControl` under node across all seven statuses, and `app.js` booted against the stub DOM with the control clicked in both directions.

## 2026-08-18 — Render a shot again, and the gap it exposed

Closed under `spec-render-again.md`. A shot that had rendered could not render again: the readiness gate refuses anything past `ready`, so comparing two takes meant hand-editing status through the generic shots route with an API client — which is exactly what had to be done earlier the same day to compare the two sampling profiles.

- **The gate is not a "render once" rule, it is a "do not render nonsense" rule.** Re-opening a shot that already satisfied it is not a bypass, which is why the prompt check is asked **again** from the prompt as it stands rather than remembered from the first render. Mutation-tested with a shot that rendered successfully and then had its prompt emptied.
- **Guard order was decided rather than inherited.** In-flight (409) first, because it is the only state where a second submission does concrete harm *and* the state a hand-walked-back status hides — so it reads both `Shot.status` and the job records keyed by `target_id`; a status-only check is mutation-tested and fails. Then the never-rendered no-op, before every refusal, because a `draft` shot carrying the `"New shot"` placeholder is what every shot the interface creates *is* — checking refusals first would turn the commonest state in the application into a 422, and refuse it for its prompt, which is not the reason. Then locked, approved, and the prompt gate.
- **The approval refusal is about meaning.** Re-rendering over an approved take would leave the approval describing something that no longer exists, and the message says that rather than just refusing.
- **The application does not track takes, and the docs now say so plainly.** ComfyUI's numbered outputs mean the earlier file survives on disk; what changes is that `latest_output` stops pointing at it. Nothing records that an earlier take existed. Believing otherwise would be the easy misreading of "not silently lost".
- **One fix beyond the matrix, taken deliberately.** `Shot.latest_review` is cleared when a new output displaces the file it describes. A vision review inspects one specific file, so carrying it across a take would leave "Inspect latest take" reporting on the previous render under the new take's name. The defect predates this story — only an API client could reach it — but this control makes it a button.
- **11 mutations, all caught**, files restored byte-identical. The four UI guarantees are caught by *executing* `renderShotInspector` under the stub-DOM harness, which is why it is now exported, on the `renderSong` precedent.
- **What it found is bigger than what it fixed.** Nothing in the shipped frontend ever writes `status = "ready"` — it is only ever read, to filter what the queue button may submit — and `Shot.status` defaults to `"draft"`. **So no shot can reach its first render through the interface at all.** Verified independently: the only server assignment of `"ready"` is this new route, which requires the shot to have already rendered. That is now open item 14, and it explains why every live render in this project has been driven by a script or an API client.

## 2026-08-18 — A visible control for the VRAM eject

Closed under `spec-vram-eject-control.md`. The eject shipped configurable only by an environment variable read at startup, so keeping a model warm meant restarting the application.

- **Precedence was the open question and is now a stated rule: the environment decides how the application starts, the control decides what happens after.** An explicitly set `MVP_LLM_EJECT_BEFORE_RENDER` beats a stored choice at startup; otherwise the stored choice beats the default. A runtime change applies immediately and is stored, and the next start re-applies the same order — so with the variable pinned, a change made in the interface does not survive a restart, and the hover text says exactly that rather than leaving it to be discovered. The alternative, a stored choice overriding the variable forever, was rejected because it makes a startup file silently inert.
- **"Explicitly set" is read from pydantic's `model_fields_set`, not by comparing against the default.** `MVP_LLM_EJECT_BEFORE_RENDER=1` and no variable at all are the same value and mean different things; only that can tell them apart.
- **A corrupt preferences file cannot read as "off".** `get_bool` returns `None` rather than `False` for missing, unreadable or wrong-typed values, so the eject fails safe toward running.
- **It is not in a project manifest, tested from both directions** — the manifest is byte-identical across a toggle, and a full-project `PUT` carrying the key changes nothing. That second test exists because the generic full-project `PUT` is the guard hole this repository has twice left open.
- **The free-VRAM half of Story 4.1 was dropped with its reason recorded**, not quietly omitted. The measurement is in the previous entry: 31.6 → 16.0 GB across an eject of a 4.71 GB model, confounded by ComfyUI's concurrent cache release. A mutation that adds a VRAM figure to the report is caught.
- **12 mutations, 10 caught, and the two survivors are the interesting part.** Removing the ComfyUI client's exception shield alone, or the ejector's outer shield alone, both survive — three independent layers absorb a failing eject, so no single-point mutation can make one fail a render, and a parametrized test that toggles the control across every failure mode catches the case where all three go. A third initially survived for a worse reason: clearing the setting on a project load was invisible because the following refresh restored it. That was a real gap, now killed by a test that fails the refresh.
- `uv run pytest` **504 passing**, ruff clean, `node --check` clean. **Not browser-verified**: the control is proven by executing `app.js` against a stub DOM, and the live sequence with a real model loaded has not been run.

## 2026-08-18 — A way back for song context

Closed under `spec-song-context-recovery.md`. Song lyrics were the largest hand-authored text this application accepts and the only one with no way back. The work was **declined** two commits earlier because the import story's frozen block said "no new model fields", and it went to the deferred ledger rather than being built. The Director renegotiated the constraint the same day. The earlier spec was left untouched — it shipped honestly under its own rule, and rewriting it would erase why the gap existed.

- **The exclusion is a classification, not a path, and that was the whole design problem.** `DIRECTOR_CONTEXT_EXCLUDE` strips top-level keys, and its own comment warns that a nested path silently stops covering a field added beside it later — which is exactly the shape these two fields wanted, since they live inside `song`. Instead, `SONG_DIRECTOR_VISIBLE` and `SONG_DIRECTOR_WITHHELD` must between them account for every field `Song` declares; unclassified, double-classified and stale-classified each raise at import. **Verified by doing it**: adding a `bpm_previous` field to `Song` aborts `uv run pytest` during *collection* with the field named, across three test modules. A leaked slot would mean the model reading back a lyric sheet the Director deliberately discarded, so failing loudly is the requirement, not a nicety.
- **`str | None`, not `str = ""` — and the spec contradicted itself here.** The Code Map said to mirror `treatment_previous` rather than invent a second shape, but the frozen matrix required an empty previous value to be a valid recovery target. The document slots are `str = ""` and cannot tell "nothing was ever kept" from "a blank was kept", which is precisely why `restore_document` refuses an empty slot. Mirroring the shape would have made the matrix row unimplementable. The frozen block won, correctly.
- **A no-op save does not spend the slot**, and each field's slot moves independently. Opening the editor and clicking save is the likeliest accidental path; consuming the one slot there would destroy the thing it protects.
- **The availability predicate tests `typeof previous === "string"`, not emptiness.** Copying `documentRestoreAvailable` by reflex would have disabled the button on exactly the blank-recovery case the matrix calls out — the one case that motivated the different field type in the first place.
- **Two silences in the matrix, decided and recorded.** A song-context restore appends nothing to the chat thread, unlike the document restore — that thread audits what the *model* did unasked, and this is the Director's own action. And the whole-project `PUT` must adopt the stored slots *before* its song comparison, or an ordinary save from an older client reads as a song replacement.
- **A stale consequence string the spec never mentioned.** `SONG_CONTEXT_CLEARING_CONSEQUENCE` literally said "A song keeps no previous version of its context" — a warning that overstates the damage stops a Director looking for the button that would undo it. Rewritten, with the test that pinned the old wording updated.
- **An inconsistency I introduced, resolved later the same day.** This restore first refused with **422** where the document restore uses **409** for the identical condition — the frozen matrix said 422 and was correctly followed. The Director renegotiated it; both now answer **409**, and so does the in-flight refusal on `mark-ready`, which had reproduced the same defect against `render_again`. The fix that matters is not the number: the codes are now asserted as an **equality across the sibling routes** rather than as the right literal written separately in each place, because separate literals are exactly what let them drift. The conditions are asserted separately from the codes, so dropping a refusal while leaving its number right still fails.
- **15 mutations, 15 killed**, every file restored byte-identical under SHA-256. Including: the no-op spending the slot, restore popping instead of swapping, a slot surviving the whole-project `PUT`, the client body trusted for the slots, the classification guard short-circuited, `if previous is None` weakened to `if not previous`, an import inheriting the old song's sheet, both slots moving on a one-field edit, and the availability predicate testing emptiness.

## 2026-08-18 — Shot expansion sees the song

Closed under `spec-expansion-sees-the-song.md`. The import story gave an imported track its lyrics and a style caption and sent both to the chat route. Expansion — a separate call over a deliberately trimmed payload — kept sending title and duration only, so **every expanded shot prompt was still written without the words** for two commits, while `docs/LLM-DIRECTOR.md` implied otherwise. Review caught the documentation, and the Director ratified extending the code rather than narrowing the claim.

- **The size objection was real but applied to the wrong call.** The recorded root cause of Director degradation is context *accumulating* across chat turns. Expansion is a single stateless whole-plan call, so a maximum sheet costs its ~3,050 tokens **once per expansion, not once per turn**. Separating those two cases is what resolved a question that had looked like a genuine trade-off — and it is why this was accepted on the same day the chat thread's unbounded growth was deliberately left uncapped.
- **Absent means absent.** Both fields are omitted rather than sent empty, matching `song_fraction` and `section`, so a song carrying neither produces a payload byte-identical to the one that shipped before. Pinned at the byte level on the serialised payload, not just by dict comparison.
- **The prompt names what the payload carries.** A field present in the payload but absent from `EXPANSION_SYSTEM_PROMPT`'s key list is a field the model may quietly ignore, which would have made this change look done while doing nothing.
- **Three things this spec got wrong, all mine.** `EXPANSION_SYSTEM_PROMPT` is in `director.py` and has never been in `app.py`. The exact-equality assertion in `tests/test_timeline.py` was predicted to fail and did not — its fixture `Song` defaults both fields to `""`, so under absent-means-absent it stays green; it is the byte-identical guard, not the failing one, and a mutation emitting empty strings is what proves it fires. And the claim that the omission was "pinned" by that assertion overstated it.
- **A hole in the prompt-coverage test, found by the work rather than by the spec.** It walked the payload's top-level and per-shot keys but never the `song` block's, so both fields could have been added to the payload and never named in the prompt with the suite green.
- **One mutation survived at first.** Striking `lyrics` from the `- song:` entry left the word elsewhere in the prompt, so a whole-prompt substring check passed. The field name now appears once in its naming position and the test is scoped to that entry. A whole-document substring assertion is not a key-list assertion — worth remembering, because it reads like one.
- **7 mutations, 7 killed**, every file restored byte-identical. Verified on a detached worktree at the baseline commit with only this story's changes applied, because a concurrent story was in the shared tree — the suite is green there, and the failures visible in the live tree at the time belonged to the other work.

## 2026-08-18 — The language model gets out of the way before a render

Closed under `spec-llm-eject-before-render.md`. The Director asked for this on 2026-08-17 — *"make it so it Ejects the LM Studio model before ComfyUI generation"* — and it was never built. Worth recording that it was not in `docs/ROADMAP.md` or the deferred ledger either: nothing was tracking it, so it was not deferred, it was dropped. It surfaced only because a VRAM check before an unrelated render went looking for the mechanism and found none.

- **Verification is the whole story, not the eject.** Shelling out to `lms unload --all` and trusting the exit code would pass a naive test while leaving VRAM held — indistinguishable from doing nothing, and the VRAM is the entire point. So the release is confirmed by re-reading `GET /api/v1/models`, and "the command succeeded but a model is still resident" is a **failure** with its own status. `/api/v1` over `/api/v0` because `v0` is the explicitly experimental namespace and `loaded_instances` names what is resident rather than describing a model's state. The parser **raises** on a shape it cannot read rather than returning empty, since returning empty would report every failed eject as a success — the same lie, told one layer down.
- **No REST unload could be committed to, and the reason is worth keeping.** LM Studio returns **HTTP 200 with an error body for every unknown path**, so status codes carry no information: `GET /v1/chat/completions`, a real POST route, is indistinguishable from `GET /api/v1/definitely-not-real`. No read-only probe can establish whether a POST unload exists, so none was claimed. Corroboration that needed no POST: `lms.exe` contains no `api/v0` or `api/v1` route strings at all, but does contain `ws://127.0.0.1:1234` and `unloadModel` — the vendor unloads over WebSocket RPC. The CLI ships behind a three-line protocol so a proven REST or WS unload is one new class later.
- **One hook, five call sites.** All five `await comfy.submit(...)` sites funnel through `ComfyClient.submit`, so a `before_submit` hook there covers them and any route added later. Injected rather than imported, which keeps `comfy.py` ignorant of LM Studio and testable without a ComfyUI server.
- **On by default**, because every failure path ends with the render submitted and the common cases cost nothing or one local HTTP call. It steps aside while a Director call is in flight, tracked by a counter across `_completion` including its retry, unwound in `finally`.
- **A logging defect found on the way, and it mattered here specifically.** uvicorn leaves the root logger at `WARNING`, so every `logger.info` in this package was being dropped and warnings arrived nameless through the last-resort handler. The eject reports through exactly those calls — it would have been invisible in the one situation it exists to be watched in. Fixed at the entrypoint, scoped to the package so httpx's per-poll chatter stays out.
- **8 mutations, 8 caught, all files restored byte-identical.** Verification removed; a hung CLI reported as success; an absent host ejected anyway; the hook's exception shield removed; the parser guessing "nothing loaded"; the eject moved after the POST; a failed eject refusing the render. **One initially survived**: the ejector's outer `except` was genuinely unexercised, because `_attempt`'s own catches already absorbed everything the unloader could raise. The real gap it pointed at was `is_busy` — application-injected code that runs *before* those catches — and a test for a broken busy gate now kills it. A mutation that survives is more informative than one that dies.
- **Verified live, with a model deliberately loaded.** `eject()` returned `status=released`, `resident_before=('huihui-qwen3-vl-4b-instruct-abliterated',)`, `resident_after=()`, and `lms ps` agreed afterwards. Through the running application the log line lands **before** the route's `202 Accepted`, which is the ordering the acceptance criterion asks for, and the render completed normally after it. Stated carefully: the raw VRAM reading fell 31.6 → 16.0 GB across the eject, *more* than the model's 4.71 GB, because ComfyUI released its own cache at the same time. The residency listing is the evidence; the VRAM delta is confounded and proves nothing on its own.
- `uv run pytest` **430 passing**, `uv run ruff check .` clean. `tests/test_api.py` still needs no language-model host — checked by running it against an unroutable `MVP_LLM_BASE_URL`.

## 2026-08-18 — An imported song can say what it is

Closed under `spec-song-context-on-import.md`. `upload_song` took a title and a duration and nothing else, so an imported track carried empty `lyrics` and `caption` forever — even though both fields already existed on `Song` and were already in the Director's context dump. A Director working from a finished track was planning from a filename.

- **No new fields, no parsing.** The two fields already meant this for generated songs, so the import fills them rather than inventing a second pair that would need an answer to "which one wins". Nothing sections or interprets the sheet: section tags in supplied lyrics carry no timestamps, so nothing here can place a chorus on the timeline, and `timeline.song_section` stays a named empty branch. Trimming is edges-only, matching the known-lyrics path's established contract — interior blank lines, indentation and tags are the structure.
- **The reach is the chat route only, and the docs now say so.** Review caught `docs/LLM-DIRECTOR.md` claiming the change reached "every expanded shot prompt". It does not. Shot expansion is a separate call over a deliberately trimmed payload, and `timeline.expansion_input` still sends only the song's title and duration — pinned by an exact-equality assertion that predates this work. Whether expansion *should* see the words is a real question and the likeliest thing the Director actually wanted, but it changes how the Director is prompted, which the spec marks Ask First. Tracked in `docs/ROADMAP.md` rather than guessed at.
- **The one call that enables the editors is now pinned by executing it.** `renderSongContext()` is what seeds the boxes and clears the `disabled` that `index.html` ships, and it had exactly one call site that nothing asserted — delete it and the entire edit-after-import path is dead on screen with the suite green, because the test read the function's body out of the file instead of running it. `tests/test_frontend_contract.py` now boots `app.js` under node against a stub DOM and reads the boxes after a real render. This is the third time this project has found a UI guarantee that could invert while green; reading source text is not verification.
- **Unsaved typing had three loss paths, not one.** `songContextDirty` is deliberately outside `state.dirty` — that flag decides whether an incidental render may re-seed, and the audio element's `loadedmetadata` fires one mid-paste. But `state.dirty` was also what gated the project-switch question and `beforeunload`, so an 8,000-character sheet vanished without a question where three characters in a document textarea produced one. A third path was found that no review listed: `loadProject` cleared the flag unconditionally, so a queue refresh, a generate, a multiview or a batch reload of the project *already on screen* wiped the box silently. Both guards now consult `unsavedWorkPending`, and the re-seed is gated separately.
- **The client's `maxlength` was silently eating the tail of a paste.** An oversized sheet lost its end with no message at the client while an API client got a documented 422. All four textareas lost `maxlength`; the route's bound is now the only bound, and a live counter measured *after* trim — matching what the server measures — reads `8,001 / 8,000 — too long to save`. A save that would replace stored text with nothing now asks first; replacing it with different text stays silent.
- **A recovery slot was declined, not forgotten.** Song lyrics are the largest hand-authored text the application accepts and the only one with no way back, while treatment and style bible both got `*_previous` in Story 2.1. But that needs a new model field, which this spec's frozen block forbids, so it is recorded rather than taken. The threat models also differ: the document slots exist because the *model* rewrites those documents unprompted, whereas song context only ever changes when the Director clicks save. The full alternative shape is in the deferred ledger, including the objection that a nested `{"song": {"lyrics_previous"}}` context exclusion is exactly the fragile pattern `app.py`'s own comment warns against.
- **The context cost was measured rather than assumed.** A maximum sheet plus caption is a constant **12,191 characters (~3,050 tokens) added to every Director call**, not just the first — 46% of the turn-0 prompt, still 23% at turn 40, and roughly 61,000 tokens of identical re-sent text over 20 turns. An 8,192-token window is crossed at turn 8 with a maximum sheet against turn 27 without one. Nothing errors, so nothing was changed: capping what the model sees is the Ask First boundary, and the real answer is a prompt-budget story, since `messages` is the only unbounded term.
- **12 mutations, all caught, every file restored byte-identical**: the seed call removed and the counters unwired; the switch guard and `beforeunload` reverted to `state.dirty`; the question dropping its consequence; `loadProject` clearing the flag on every load again; the clearing confirmation removed and its reporter silenced; `maxlength` returning; the client bound drifting to 9000; `>=` at the exact boundary; and the style box wired to the lyric limit.
- `uv run pytest` **385 passing**, `uv run ruff check .` clean, `node --check` clean on `app.js` and `api.js`. Scope: the lyrics are proven to reach `RevisingDirector.contexts[0]`, so the value is measured and not assumed — but **no browser-driven run covers this UI**, and the executed-DOM harness is a stub, not a browser.

## 2026-08-18 — Two sampling profiles, each proven whole

Closed under `spec-h3-turbo-sampling-profile.md`. The reference adapter could render exactly one way: the audited export's 20-step graph with no LoRA. The Director's own H3 stage renders the *same* `ref2va` checkpoint at 4 steps by applying a LoRA trained for it — roughly a fifth of the sampling — and the application could not reach that at all.

- **Profiles, not a boolean, because the difference is not one switch.** `H3_REFERENCE_PROFILES` in `workflows.py` holds two `H3SamplingProfile` records: `default` is `simple`/`res_multistep` at 20 steps with no LoRA; `turbo` is `minimax_h3_ref2v_turbo_4step_v0.1_comfyui_bf16.safetensors` at strength 0.7 with `beta`/`euler` at 4 steps. Each is a bundle someone tuned together. Applying the LoRA while keeping the export's scheduler would be a third combination with no evidence behind it, presented as if it were proven — so the adapter takes a profile name and never the individual values.
- **Both sources were read rather than trusted.** The turbo numbers come from node `5959` (`LoraLoaderModelOnly`, 0.7) fed by the `ref2va` UNET loader (`127`) in the Director's saved `04 - H3 Music Video - LTX 2.5 READY.json`, with node `124` scheduling `beta` at 4 and node `123` selecting `euler`. Stated precisely, because an earlier draft here said the LoRA sat "between `127` and the sigma shift (`5960`)" and the file does not show that: the chain is `127 → 5959 → 142 → 5960`, where `142` is `PathchSageAttentionKJ` with `sage_attention: "disabled"`. The adapter has no attention node at all, so its LoRA does feed the shift directly — but that is a property of the adapter, not a reading of the export, and the two were being conflated. The default's come from the audited export: node `2382` (`simple`, 20), node `2388` (`res_multistep`), and node `2383` `Power Lora Loader (rgthree)` carrying `lora_1` with **`"on": false`**. That off-switch is the entire justification for a no-LoRA default, and it is now asserted against the export file itself — if anyone switches it on, the test that says "this is why" stops agreeing. Worth recording: the LoRA the export holds switched off is the *generic* `minimax_h3_turbo_4step_ckpt500_comfyui_pruned.safetensors`, **not** the `ref2v` one the turbo profile applies. They are different files.
- **"Unchanged" is pinned as a digest, not as a copied dict.** `tests/test_workflows.py` hashes the default profile's payload for one fixed set of arguments and compares it with a digest taken by running commit `7e25ad0`'s builder — key order included. A literal expected payload written today would only prove the adapter agrees with itself.
- **The wiring assertion is phrased as the failure, not the fix.** "Nothing but the LoRA reads `mvp:model`", rather than "the shift node reads the LoRA": a node added later that quietly kept reading the loader would satisfy the second and leave half the graph sampling the unpatched checkpoint.
- **An omitted step count now means "the profile's", which is why `H3Request.steps` became optional.** A literal default of 20 cannot be told apart from a Director who typed 20, so every caller who never touched the field would have sent 20 steps into a 4-step LoRA bundle. The text-only Director path takes no profile — different checkpoint pair through `MiniMaxH3DirectorCS`, and the installed generic turbo LoRAs are not the `ref2v` one — so its omitted count falls through to `H3_DIRECTOR_DEFAULT_STEPS`, the same 20 it always meant. An explicit count still wins on every path.
- **Naming a profile on a text-only Shot is a 422, not a silent no-op.** Review found the field was bound on both branches and passed on only one, so `{"profile": "turbo"}` on a text-only Shot returned 202 and rendered the ordinary 20-step graph — a full-price render logged under a configuration nobody applied, indistinguishable after the fact from one that was. Refusing was chosen over accepting-and-proving-inert because this project has no evidence for that path at all. The test draws the line rather than the point: it submits the profile and asserts nothing was queued, then proves the same Shot still renders without it *and* that attaching a reference makes the very same profile submittable.
- **The smoke reports what the server built, not what the constants say.** It printed the profile's LoRA, steps and sampler out of `H3_REFERENCE_PROFILES` — so a Shot that had quietly lost its reference would fall to the Director branch and still print `profile: turbo, steps: 4`. It now reads `/history/{prompt_id}` after the job settles and reports the sampling actually submitted, fatally on mismatch. The submitted graph is located **by shape** — the member that is a mapping of `class_type` nodes — rather than by index, and a duplicated class summarises as empty rather than picking the first, because a guess printed as a measurement is the failure being avoided.
- **An unknown profile is refused by validation, before a payload exists.** The `Literal` on `H3Request` is what does that, and the test asserts the 422's `detail` is FastAPI's *list* shape rather than the builder's string — widening the field to `str` would still 422 through the builder's own refusal and satisfy anything weaker. The builder keeps its refusal anyway; it is the belt.
- **The pre-flight audits both profiles: `OK 157 nodes across 9 variants (19 classes)`** against live ComfyUI 0.33.1, up from `OK 138 nodes across 8 variants (18 classes)`. The turbo variant is the only one carrying a `LoraLoaderModelOnly`, and the LoRA's filename reaches the model-file check by being *loaded* by a payload rather than by being listed in the audit. `LoraLoaderModelOnly` left `UNRECORDED_CLASSES` and the fixture gained it — 1 new class, **0 changed**, so no other recorded schema moved underneath. A side effect worth naming: the Krea multiview LoRAs are now range-checked offline too, because that class is finally recorded. The audit gained a **sixth check**, comparing `H3_LORA_STRENGTH_LIMITS` with `LoraLoaderModelOnly.strength_model`'s declared bounds on the live server — the strength range a profile is validated against is now pinned to the range the node actually accepts, rather than being a number chosen here and left to drift.
- **The smoke's 4-step override is gone, and that override was the story's real origin.** The first live reference render sent `steps: 4` against the default profile — a 20-step graph with nothing to compensate — and produced a badly undersampled frame. That was a cost-saving choice in `tests/smoke_h3_reference_app.py`, not a defect in the adapter. The script now names a profile and sends **no step count at all**; the body it posts is a module constant so a test can assert the absence of `steps` without a socket, an app, or a GPU.
- **Thirty-five mutations across two rounds, all caught, every file restored byte-identical.** Round one (eighteen): each of the default profile's scheduler and its missing LoRA; the turbo LoRA's filename and strength; its scheduler/sampler pair and its step count; the downstream rewiring; explicit steps ignored and the profile's default ignored; an unknown profile silently defaulting; a half-configured profile accepted; the Director path's step default; the route dropping the profile, widening it to `str`, and reverting `steps` to a literal 20; the audit losing its turbo variant; the coverage ledger claiming the LoRA class is still unrecorded; and the smoke hardcoding a step count again. Round one's review then added seventeen more: the text-only refusal removed; each of the four turbo values drifting from the export; a second LoRA-carrying variant appearing; each of the four field validations disabled; the type guard dropped; the strength limits drifting from the node's; the new check unwired; and three on the smoke — reporting constants instead of the submitted graph, taking the graph by index, and summarising a duplicated scheduler by picking the first. The audit-hardcodes-a-count mutation had to touch two files at once, since a hardcoded-but-equal literal is only detectable once the profile diverges from it.
- **Where the turbo profile's evidence lives, corrected.** Round one asserted the four turbo values as literals typed into the test while the *default* profile was asserted against its export file — so the standard the story claimed held for the profile carrying no LoRA and not for the one carrying a LoRA into a GPU job. The source was in the repo the whole time. The test now finds the `ref2va` `UNETLoader` in `h3-ltx25-user-export.json`, takes the one `LoraLoaderModelOnly` fed by it, walks the model chain forward to the one `BasicScheduler` downstream, then to the `SamplerCustomAdvanced` on that scheduler's `sigmas` — chain-walked rather than picked by class, because that export holds several samplers and its LTX stages are `euler` too, so matching on class alone would be coincidence dressed as evidence.
- `uv run pytest` **385 passing** on this shared tree (the song-context story below landed in it too, so this figure is not this change's own arithmetic); this change adds 16 test functions / 18 collected cases. `uv run ruff check .` clean, `node --check` clean on `app.js`. Nothing was rendered *by the offline work above*; the live A/B that followed it is recorded in its own section below.

## 2026-08-18 — Both sampling profiles rendered, and the speed claim corrected

The turbo profile shipped schema-audited with no picture behind it. Two renders closed that, and the second one was submitted specifically to remove a confound in the first.

- **The first turbo render was not a fair comparison, and saying so is the point.** It produced a good frame in 185 s against a remembered 497.6 s for the default profile — a tempting 2.7×. But that turbo run had generated its *own* Reference Sheet rather than reusing the earlier run's, whose project manifest was already gone, and the 497.6 s run had loaded its models cold. Two variables moved. The composition differences between those two frames are not attributable to the profile and were never claimed to be.
- **So the same shot was re-rendered on the default profile.** Same project, same Reference Sheet `asset_21cc26355142`, same shot, same seed `20260819`, same 640×384 / 3.75 s window, models warm in both cases, one variable changed. The shot's status was walked back from `complete` to `ready` through the shots route to get past the readiness gate — worth noting as the only way to re-render a settled shot today.
- **Turbo is not degraded.** A coherent cinematic frame, the sheet's wardrobe carried faithfully, and **none of the panel bleed the earlier 4-step run without a LoRA produced**. That contrast is the actual evidence that the LoRA does the work the step count would otherwise do — a 4-step render of this graph looked broken before, and does not now.
- **The speedup is 23%, not 5×.** Turbo **182.7 s**, default **237.5 s**, both ComfyUI execution time from `/history` rather than wall clock. Five times fewer sampling steps bought under a quarter off the render, so **sampling is not what dominates this graph** — roughly four-fifths of the time is elsewhere. The story's own framing, "roughly a fifth of the sampling for comparable output", is true about steps and badly misleading about renders, and `docs/WORKFLOW-MAP.md` now says so explicitly. The profile is a modest saving with a different look, not a fast path.
- **The two frames differ in look, not quality.** Turbo is higher-contrast and darker, tighter in composition, with less legible background detail; the default is wider and brighter with more environmental fidelity. Neither is obviously better and the choice is the Director's.
- Scope: **one shot, one sheet, one seed, one window.** Nothing here establishes how either profile behaves on other subjects, longer windows, or multiple references, and no frame beyond index 45 of either clip was inspected.

## 2026-08-18 — The reference path renders, and what it rendered

Closed under `spec-3-2-first-live-reference-render.md`. Character consistency is this product's second success measure and it rests entirely on one path — character Asset, promoted to a multiview sheet, attached to a Shot, rendered through the reference workflow — which was unit-tested, schema-audited by Story 3.1, and had never produced a single real output from this application. Promotion had never run from the app either. Two GPU jobs were authorised and exactly two were spent.

- **What ran.** ComfyUI 0.33.1 at `127.0.0.1:8188`, app on port 8766 with an isolated data root, project `project_9d237e8fbddb`, 2026-08-18. `tests/preflight_h3_ultra.py` first: `OK 138 nodes across 8 variants (18 classes)`. Then the promotion — `job_cab46ecd37f5`, prompt `1471fa58-24a9-4810-8de7-de7c0861f7b1`, seed `20260818`, complete in 56.1 s, producing `asset_d0486f608572` at 2,025,707 bytes and 1536×1024. Then the render — `shot_f5a5b9f4ab72`, `job_cc558fd0ff89`, prompt `6dbe4ff7-08d2-468c-bd5b-8dce37bd68fd`, seed `20260819`, complete in 136.5 s: h264 640×384, `nb_frames` 90, 24/1 fps, video 3.750 s, aac 32000 Hz stereo, audio 3.744 s, container 561,942 bytes. Duration delta from the request 0.000 s; audio/video sync delta -0.006 s. `latest_output` set, `approved_output` empty, Shot `complete`.
- **The reconciliation is the evidence, not a step on the way to it.** `generate_multiview` creates the child with `path=""`; only the ordinary job refresh copies `output_files[0]` onto it. Until that happens `resolve_asset_path` fails `is_file()` and the reference branch 404s, so the sheet cannot be attached at all. The smoke asserts the populated path and resolves it on disk rather than trusting the job — and it never writes `source="krea-multiview"` itself, because a hand-written provenance would assert something the application never did.
- **`duration=3.75`, not a round 4 s.** The builder pads to the 17k+5 grid, so 4.0 s becomes 107 frames and 4.458 s, while 3.75 s is exactly 90 frames and a measured duration the assertion needs no allowance for. The run measured 90 and 3.750 s, so the grid arithmetic holds at a second point on it. The smoke recomputes the expectation from `align_h3_frames` before it starts and refuses to run if the grid ever moves.
- **The probe target is chosen by name.** A completed H3 shot leaves a `.png`, a silent `.mp4` and a muxed `-audio.mp4`, and only the last carries synchronized audio. This run's `output_files` happened to carry only the `-audio.mp4`, so the file chosen by name and the file `latest_output` received were the same — but that is a measurement, recorded as one, not a property to rely on. `output_files[0]` remains the app's own choice and this run does not license it.
- **The node trims a long audio reference to the window — measured, previously unknown.** The 154 s master song was attached whole via `use_song_audio` and the route hands the node the entire file with no offsets. The emitted audio is 3.744 s. Before this run nobody knew whether a long master forced the whole track through the audio VAE.
- **The acceptance criteria are met and the render is not usable output.** At 4 steps the frame is heavily degraded. That is what a deliberately minimum-cost setting buys and it is not a defect — but no sentence in any document may be read as claiming this produced a good shot.
- **An unresolved observation, recorded without a cause.** The first frame shows the Reference Sheet's four-panel layout carried into the *composition*: the face plate visible on the left, the vertical panel bands persisting, a figure standing inside one band. The model appears to have treated the sheet's layout as scene structure rather than purely as identity reference. Two factors plausibly contribute and neither is established — 4 steps is far below anything usable, and `ref_image_size` was `"match"` (the default), so the 1536×1024 sheet was conditioned at its own aspect. Whether a production step count, `"max"` sizing, or a single-view reference changes it is untested. Filed as deferred work.
- **So state which half was earned.** The mechanism is proven: promotion runs from the app, reconciliation populates the child, attachment resolves, the reference graph is accepted, and the output verifies for frames, duration and synchronized audio. Epic 3's goal — "a character recognizably the same person across the Shots of a finished video, judged by the Director's eye" — is **not** demonstrated by this run, and one 4-step clip could not demonstrate it even if it looked perfect.
- **The evidence is a script, and it was verified without spending GPU first.** `tests/smoke_h3_reference_app.py` refuses without `--confirm-gpu` before any network call — proven under a socket guard patching `connect`/`connect_ex`/`sendto`/`create_connection`/`getaddrinfo`, across five argv shapes including an unknown flag and a `--confirmgpu` typo, all exiting 2 with zero socket attempts. The ComfyUI-offline abort, the pre-flight-failure abort (the real audit against a stubbed `/object_info`), the promotion-error abort, the unreconciled-child abort, the output selection, `resolve_output`'s path refusals and the whole measure-and-assert tail were driven against a stub transport and against real prior H3 media on disk; the multipart uploads, the `PUT /shots` body and the readiness of the Shot it writes were driven against the real app on an isolated port. One real fragility surfaced there and was fixed before the live run: the uploaded Asset was being found by scanning backwards for "a character Asset with a path", and a promoted Reference Sheet is *also* `kind="character"` with a path, so a project already holding one could have had the wrong Asset promoted.
- **`probe` is now one implementation for two smokes.** `tests/smoke_songplanner_app.py` reports the video stream — dimensions, frame count, frame rate, duration — alongside the audio one, plus the audio stream's own length so synchronization can be measured rather than inferred from both streams merely being present. `decodable` still means *audio* decodable, so the song variants read it exactly as before. A frame count absent from the container header is counted by decoding, never derived from duration times frame rate, and which source produced it travels with the number.
- `uv run pytest` **337 passing**, unchanged: the deliverable is a manual smoke and manual smokes are not pytest-collected. `uv run ruff check .` clean, `node --check` clean on `app.js` and `api.js`. Scope of the claim, exactly: two live jobs, measured with `ffprobe`, plus the offline verification above. No browser was driven and no second render was attempted.

## 2026-08-17 — A pre-flight whose verdict is worth trusting

Closed under `spec-3-1-h3-ultra-preflight-audit.md`. The H3 Ultra reference path has never rendered, and nothing checked it before GPU time was spent: 17 node classes and 4 model files unverified against the running server, 13 of those classes absent from the recorded fixture, and the existing validator reporting **eight failures on a correct reference graph** — four on the Director graph — because it understood two ComfyUI schema shapes the SongPlanner graphs never used. Several ratified behaviours had no assertions at all: the per-kind limits, the routing rule, reference-file resolution, and that promotion leaves its source untouched.

- **The rules moved to `tests/preflight.py`; the scripts became callers.** `preflight_songplanner.py` is now its two payloads and a `main`, and prints the same `OK 20 nodes across 2 variants (10 classes)` line it did before. Epics 1 and 7 cite this pattern by name, so the extraction is the deliverable, not a tidy-up: a third adapter adds a payload list, not a fourth copy of the range check.
- **Autogrow groups, the first false failure.** `MiniMaxH3ReferenceToVideo` publishes `ref_images` as a template plus an index range rather than as nine keys, so the payload's `ref_images.ref_image_0` was reported as an input that does not exist — once per attached reference. The validator now materialises the `prefix`+index slots the template describes and reports only an index past its `max`, with a message that says so instead of "does not exist". The audited user export uses `ref_image_0`…`ref_image_8` for a `max` of 9, which is what fixes the off-by-one reading of `max` as the last index.
- **Format-conditional inputs, the second.** `VHS_VideoCombine` puts `crf`, `pix_fmt`, `save_metadata` and `trim_to_audio` inside `format`'s options dict, keyed by the selected format, because a different container takes different switches. All four were reported absent from a correct save node, and `crf` resolved no bounds — so its 0–100 range was silently unchecked, the exact vacuous-pass failure `unbounded_numeric_inputs` exists to make loud. The merge reads the payload's own `format` value, and a test switches it to `video/ffv1-mkv` to prove the selection is what decides: `crf` disappears, `pix_fmt` stays.
- **A pre-flight that lies is worse than none.** Both extensions are in scope for this reason rather than deferred: eight failures on a correct payload trains the Director to ignore the verdict, and a validator that cannot see a whole class of input is not checking what it claims. `tests/preflight_h3_ultra.py` now reports `OK 90 nodes across 5 variants (17 classes)` against live ComfyUI 0.33.1 — one picture, the per-kind limits filled, a video with its paired soundtrack, `ref_image_size="max"`, and the longest window the frame ceiling allows. Precisely: those variants feed **16 of the graph's 18 autogrow slots** — the only paired-audio variant carries one video, so `ref_video_audio_1` and `_2` are never exercised. "Every slot filled" was the first wording and it was wrong.
- **The audit checks the adapter against the schema that produced it, not against itself.** `H3_REFERENCE_LIMITS`, `H3_SPLIT_OFFSETS` and `H3_REFERENCE_MAX_FRAMES` are named in `workflows.py` so the audit can read the adapter's numbers and compare them with the live ones: 9/3/3 against the autogrow maxima, every `mvp:split` index against the splitter's `output_name` list (`picture_1…9`, `video_1…3`, `video_audio_1…3`, `audio_1…3`), and 3600 against `length`'s declared maximum. Copying those numbers into the audit would have made it agree with any drift.
- **Recording merges.** The old recorder overwrote the fixture with a subset derived from its own payloads, so the second audit to run would have deleted the first's coverage — and `UNRECORDED_CLASSES`, the ledger of what nothing range-checks offline, would have gone stale in the direction that looks safe. The fixture went 10 → 23 classes with every SongPlanner entry byte-identical, and the ledger shrank by exactly the 13 recorded names. A failing audit still records nothing.
- **`length` above 3600 is refused here rather than by ComfyUI**, which rejects the whole prompt at validation time and reaches the Director as an opaque 502 after the round-trip. The inline 17k+5 alignment that duplicates `timeline.align_h3_frames` is now asserted to agree with it across every eighth of a second from 0.125 s to the ceiling — they always did; nothing said so.
- **The ratified behaviour, asserted.** All five builder refusals translate to 422 through the route, each message produced by *calling* the builder rather than copied from it. The routing rule is a pair, each asserting the other branch's marker class is absent — `shot.asset_ids or shot.use_song_audio`, never `Shot.mode`, so a `mode="text"` Shot carrying an Asset still renders as a reference shot, and the song alone routes to the reference payload. Mixed-kind tags are numbered per kind in attachment order (`<Picture 1>`, `<Video 1>`, `<Picture 2>`, `<Audio 1>`, then the master song), and reordering the attachments reorders them. Both file-resolution 404s keep their existing wording and submit nothing. Promotion is compared as *bytes*: the persisted source Asset record and its media file are unchanged, which the previous `parent_id` assertion would have allowed a route to violate.
- **Nineteen mutations reintroduced across fifteen guarantees. All nineteen caught, every file restored byte-identical.** Each per-kind limit; the frame ceiling both moved and removed; a split offset shifted by one; each of the three other builder refusals; the alignment arithmetic; autogrow expansion; the conditional merge; the fixture merge; a model filename; the routing condition; the per-kind tag counter replaced by a shared one; both file-resolution `is_file()` checks; promotion writing to its source; and the coverage ledger claiming a recorded class is unrecorded. A failing audit run with `--record` was also confirmed to leave the fixture byte-identical.
- `uv run pytest` **312 passing** against 289 on the tree before this change, `uv run ruff check .` clean, `node --check` clean on `app.js` and `api.js`. Scope of the claim, exactly: this is a schema audit and an offline test suite. **Nothing was rendered** — the H3 Ultra reference path still has no live evidence (true on the day; it rendered live on 2026-08-18, see the entry above), and the `shift_audio` discrepancy between the Director graph's `4` and the reference graph's `3` is reported, not resolved: both are in range and neither is provably wrong.

## 2026-08-17 — A protective refusal that cannot be mistaken for a reply

Closed under `spec-2-4-director-safety-notices-unmissable.md`; the binding decisions are AD-14 and AD-5 in `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md`. Every refusal this project produces was concatenated into the assistant message as plain text after a `\n\n---\n` separator. Nothing rendered it distinctly, no test touched the thread render at all, and the document-rejection notice — the only one with no named constant — embedded up to 400 characters of the model's degraded output straight into `content`. The chat thread is dumped back to the model as context on the next turn, so the guard written to stop "JSON in context begets JSON" was the thing supplying it. Story 2.2 closed that loop for expansion by dropping the refused text entirely; the chat route still had it open. FR-15's prose-claims-Shots clause had no implementation anywhere.

- **Structure instead of a text convention.** `models.MessageNotice` carries a display `text` and the `raw` model output it is about; `TreatmentMessage.notices` is a defaulted list of them, so a manifest written before this loads unchanged and simply carries none. `content` keeps carrying the joined text — it is what every saved project already holds and what `api.js`'s two marker-scanning helpers read, and changing it would break both for no gain.
- **`DIRECTOR_CONTEXT_EXCLUDE` drops the whole `notices` list, not the `raw` field inside it.** Every notice's sentence is already in `content`, so keeping the structured copy would echo a second copy of each notice into the prompt — the same objection that put the recovery slots in this mapping. And a nested exclusion path silently stops covering a field that is renamed or added beside it, which is not a property to want on the one invariant that turns the degradation guard into the source of the degradation.
- **Asserted against the recorded context, not the stored message.** Two route tests refuse (chat, then expansion), take a further chat turn, and read the `project_context` the double was actually handed. The containment check walks the payload's strings instead of searching `json.dumps`: `json.dumps` escapes the quotes that degraded model output is made of, so `'[{"style":"moody"}]'` is never a substring of a dump containing it — the previous assertion passed whether or not the text was there, which is exactly the class of green-suite inversion this story exists to end.
- **The expansion route's refused prompt is restored into `raw`**, which was the spec's one "ask first". Story 2.2 dropped it only because there was nowhere to put it that the next call would not read; the excluded field is that place. `EXPANSION_REJECTED_NOTICE` claimed in its own words that the text was not recorded, so that sentence was rewritten rather than left to become a lie the tests still asserted.
- **No splitting on `---`, and the test proves the shortcut would have been wrong rather than merely forbidding it.** The Director's prose can contain that sequence and so can the raw output a notice embeds — which would split one notice into two blocks, the second being the model's own degraded text presented as a protective refusal. `messageParts` reconstructs the exact tail the server joined and strips it; the fixture's model message deliberately contains `\n\n---\n`, and the test asserts the naive split really does lose most of it. A message whose tail the client cannot account for keeps all of its content and still shows every notice: printing one twice is visible, swallowing half a refusal is not.
- **One separator, typed once.** `NOTICE_SEPARATOR`/`NOTICE_JOIN` and one `assistant_reply` helper replace the byte-identical literals both routes carried, and `api.js` exports the same two constants with a contract test asserting they are identical — the `READINESS_REFUSAL` pattern.
- **FR-15's last clause, implemented.** A reply whose prose describes a shot plan while the structured list is empty is reported as a mismatch. Ungated on `apply_shots`: the browser hardcodes that flag to `false`, so gating it there would put the notice exactly where the existing empty-shot-list notice already is — unreachable. Detection is two narrow patterns, the word itself and a counted structure, because the recorded 2026-08-16 defect never used the word "shot": it said "Splitting your vision into a four-beat sequence", and a check that misses the one recorded instance of the defect is not a check. It reports and never blocks — with no shots there is nothing to apply, so a false positive costs a paragraph and a false negative costs the Director a plan they believe exists. The two empty-list notices stay independent and can both fire: one says the reply contradicts itself, the other says the consent given produced nothing.
- **The collapse floor now runs through the route.** It had four unit tests and no request had ever reached it, because every double either echoed the stored document or returned a long replacement — the route could have stopped consulting `document_rejection` for short candidates with a fully green suite.
- **Unmissable is a label plus an edge, never an edge alone.** Each notice renders in its own block with the existing `--amber` caution token on the left border — no new colour — a text label above the sentence, and the raw output behind a `<details>` collapsed by default. `escapeHtml` moved into `api.js` so the whole message body is one pure export: the thread was a single `innerHTML` map that escaped `content` whole, so notice markup had to be built by splitting *before* escaping each part.
- **The render is executed, not grepped.** `messageParts` and `messageBodyHtml` run under node against a reply the live route produced, and the assertions read the HTML the browser would get: two blocks, one label each, exactly one closed disclosure, the fixture's `<script>` tag escaped, and each notice printed exactly once. Story 2.3's review found three UI guarantees pinned only by substring matching that could invert with a green suite; a substring of `app.js` proves the source contains a string, not that a notice renders.
- **Twenty-four mutations reintroduced. All twenty-four caught on the first attempt, and every file was restored byte-identical afterwards.** The exclusion dropping `notices`; `raw` emptied on either route; the raw pasted back into `content`; the mismatch notice deleted, or gated on `apply_shots`; the claim patterns narrowed to the literal word, or widened to fire on everything; the consent notice suppressed by the mismatch; the reply built without the shared helper; `MIN_REPLACEMENT_RATIO` zeroed; `notices` made required so an old manifest fails to load; the splitter searching for the separator, or leaving the joined tail in the prose; the label dropped; the disclosure opened by default; the raw interpolated unescaped; the notices rendered before the prose; `renderTreatment` reverting to escaping `content`; the amber becoming a literal hex; a later rule reclaiming the notice edge; the notice paragraph rule dropped; and `_raw_excerpt` left uncapped.
- `uv run pytest` **271 passing** against 262 on the tree before this change, `uv run ruff check .` clean, `node --check` clean on `app.js` and `api.js`. Scope of the claim: these are offline tests and no live model or GPU was involved. **The notice block has not been driven in a real browser** — not the amber edge, not the label, not the disclosure — so only the executable contract is claimed for the UI half.

## 2026-08-17 — A Shot Plan that cannot be submitted unprompted

Closed under `spec-2-3-shot-plan-readiness-gate.md`; the binding decision is AD-5 in `ARCHITECTURE-SPINE.md`. An empty-prompt Shot reached ComfyUI and burned a full GPU pass to return noise. `generate_h3` checked only `status == "ready"` and never looked at the prompt; the text branch submitted `"local_prompts": ""` without complaint; `compile_timeline` serialised `"prompt": ""` in silence. Nothing anywhere computed whether a plan was fit to submit, and nothing noticed that two Shots carried the same prompt.
